- [x] **Search-as-admission prototype** (gates 49's protocol): run the
  §5 compatibility check as a query predicate over the existing
  examples/ components — does "find me an admissible provider" work
  with zero new machinery? If yes, the registry's core is already
  written.
  **VERDICT (f8c6927): YES** — a registry query IS the §5 compatibility gate run
  as a filter, proven over examples/ with the real predicate and zero new
  compatibility logic (admits identical/superset/purer providers, refuses a
  dropped-but-still-emitted method and an added unmarked emission). A registry
  adds only an index + a batch driver; **key namespacing (item 9's deferred
  half) is the one remaining prerequisite.** docs/registry-probe.md.
- [x] **Non-ASCII cross-tier probe** (gates 51's scope): one probe
  (`"😀".length()`, `charAt`, `slice`) through the cross-tier
  execution suite — turns the predicted string divergence into a
  measured table in an hour.
  **VERDICT (2841397):** measured — `"😀".length()` = 1 (py) / 2 (ts, java) /
  4 (wasm) / rejected (go, rust — a UTF-16-surrogate literal-escaping defect;
  their length/slice are already code-point). **DECIDED unit = code points**
  (docs/strings.md); float interpolation = ECMAScript Number::toString. Per-tier
  fix work is enumerated for the string wave (item 51) — cross-cutting IR
  literal-as-code-points + one Float renderer, then per-tier: py free, go/rust
  literal-escaper only, ts/java method rewrites, wasm largest (WAT UTF-8 decode).

10. (issue #377) **Instance-parametric components** — phase 1 landed, and ✅ **PHASE 2 DONE
    ON ALL SIX TIERS:** `spawn` lowering executes on cordis-py (reference), ts,
    rust, go, java, **and wasm** — each proven on its real runtime (the 4 spawn
    properties: distinct local realms, independent LIFO teardown, request-scoped
    anti-leak, supervision-tree addressing). wasm was the hard one — it needed
    **B3** (per-instance local/identity realms), a contained change in the
    first-party `cordis-wasm` runtime (a realm prefix on the flat provider
    table), *not* a module-per-instance; the revl-side emitter is on `main`,
    and the cordis-wasm B3 commit is pushed to the cordis-wasm repo (`f5c0562`).
    ✅ **Instance accessor** — reading a provision back through a handle
    (`s.<key>` field access, no new grammar; frozen `instance-get` IR node in
    docs/design-v2-instances.md) — is landed on **all six tiers** (the fan-out
    included the async-activation fix `fcc041c`: spawn waits for the child to
    activate so the read resolves through the live context). ✅ **Hot-swap**
    with live instances (migrate-state) landed — structural migration is the
    canonical substrate (item 53), the same-typed-reorder hazard named + pinned.
    Subsumes the old "dynamic realms" item.
    **Named horizon — placement-aware spawn:** today `spawn` always lands
    in the spawning process, and placement assigns *components*, not
    instances. Their convergence ("spawn C for tenant X *in process P*",
    and with item 23, migrating a hot tenant's instance to another tier)
    is verified rebalancing — elastic sharding with checked isolation.
    Not scheduled; recorded so phases 2–5 leave room for it: the spawn
    lowering must not bake "same process" into any tier's shape.
    ✅ **Placement-file spelling + plan-time gate landed (the "checked
    isolation" half):** a placement assigns a named instance by ADDRESS —
    `[processes.<p>] instances = ["Spawner/name", ...]` —
    `named_instance_placement_diagnostic` validating each address as a pure
    read against `manifest["named_instances"]` (malformed / unknown /
    double-placed refused before anything spawns), reported in the boot
    summary. Additive (no `instances` key ⇒ byte-identical). It makes no
    location claim the runtime honours yet; **still open** is the runtime
    honour + cross-tier fan-out (land the instance in process P, reference
    tier first) and the item-23 migration, under the standing "no tier bakes
    same process" constraint (docs/design-v2-instances.md).

73. (issue #73) **Golden policy: snapshot, not freeze.** The per-backend byte-equality
    goldens (`backends/*/golden/user_cache.*`) have quietly hardened from
    change detectors into a compatibility contract — and with no external
    users, that contract protects nobody while bending emitter design.
    Two fossils prove it: `_uses_builtin_result`
    (backends/python/emit.py) walks the whole IR to keep the v1 golden's
    bytes, and rust's `_string` (backends/rust/emit.py) keeps ASCII
    byte-identical to the old `json.dumps` escaping — a second dialect
    inside one function whose only reader is a frozen fixture. The
    *decision*: goldens are snapshot tests. The invariant they enforce is
    "emitter output never changes **unreviewed**", never "output never
    changes" — regenerating (backends/typescript/scripts/regen-golden.py
    and siblings) plus reviewing the diff is always an acceptable
    resolution, and a byte-stability concern is never grounds for a dual
    code path. Work: (a) write the policy into docs/conformance.md where
    the goldens are specified; (b) apply it once — collapse `_string` to
    one uniform code-point escape path and regen the rust golden, and
    re-judge `_uses_builtin_result` on its independent merit (dead-code
    hygiene) with the golden clause dropped from its docstring; (c) fold
    the per-backend golden runs into the default test entry point so a
    stale golden can't hide behind a green `pytest tests/` (the recurring
    CI gap); (d) decide v1 IR input support explicitly — every emitter
    carries a two-dialect body for v1 (`_emit_v1` and kin), and if its
    only remaining consumers are the goldens and self-tests, freeze or
    retire it rather than letting fixtures prop it up. When a real
    external consumer appears, byte-freezing may return — deliberately,
    as a versioned promise, not as an accident of test setup.
    (a)–(c) ✅ **Landed:** policy written into docs/conformance.md ("Golden
    policy: snapshot, not freeze"), stability.md's freeze promise and the
    roadmap's "goldens must never regenerate" corrected to the snapshot
    reading; rust `_string` collapsed to one code-point escape path with the
    rust golden regenerated (regen diff reviewed: byte-identical) and
    `_uses_builtin_result` re-judged on dead-code hygiene; every golden
    byte-equality run folded into `pytest tests/` via tests/test_goldens.py
    (reference-IR user_cache goldens for py/ts/rust/java + the wasm
    functions.wat golden). **(d) ✅ DECIDED 2026-09-02 (via 419d): FREEZE, not
    retire.** The premise the question rested on is false: the v1 emitter
    bodies are NOT propped up by the goldens and self-tests. `lower.py` picks
    the version from the features a program uses (`IR_VERSION_V3 if uses_v3
    else (IR_VERSION_V2 if uses_v2 else IR_VERSION)`), so the SHIPPED frontend
    still mints `ir_version: 1` for a program declaring no type, fn, extern,
    timer or `spawn` and no `isolate`/`intercept`/`routes`; 13 of the
    `examples/*.rvl` compile to v1 today, `user_cache.rvl` among them, and
    `_emit_v1` is the live path for every one of them. So v1/v2 stay supported
    INPUT dialects, frozen: no new v1-only feature, no new v1-only code path,
    no second dialect written for byte stability (that is (b)'s rule), and a
    shared-code change must keep the v1 path CORRECT rather than just keep the
    golden green. **Retiring has a precondition: the frontend must stop minting
    v1 first**, since deleting `_emit_v1` today breaks programs the compiler
    still produces, and "retire the dialect" would silently mean bumping every
    stored document's version. Written up in docs/conformance.md ("v1 IR input
    support: frozen, not retired") and PINNED by
    `tests/test_goldens.py::test_the_frontend_still_mints_ir_version_1`, so a
    change to version selection reopens the decision deliberately instead of
    leaving the v1 bodies as quiet dead code.

75. (issue #74) **Errata harvest, checker side — the typing frontier's next
    slice.** The errata's "typing gaps" section names its own remaining
    work; none of it is on the roadmap. Three concrete closures, in
    value order:
    (a) **Arrow parameter annotations** — the grammar change the errata
    itself names as the blocker: an arrow's params are un-annotated, so
    the arrow value and everything obtained by calling it types unknown,
    and call-site arity goes unchecked (`let f = (x) => "s"` then
    `f(1)` in an `Int` position flows silently). Arrow *bodies* are
    already checked; annotations close the value half. Spec-first —
    the spelling should be TypeScript's, per §0. **DEFERRED to wave 9** —
    the arrow half of the typing frontier stays as the errata's narrowed
    entry (bodies checked, values unknown); do not build it in this slice.
    (b) **The stdlib-named-method sliver** — ✅ **closed.** A receiver
    whose provenance no constructor pins refuses non-stdlib method names
    but *lowered* a stdlib-named method as that builtin, wrong at
    runtime: `x.get(k)` on an extern-returned value dispatched as the Map
    builtin. Stdlib-named methods on unpinned receivers are now refused
    too (same HOST-METHOD diagnostic —
    `examples/rejections/t24_opaque_receiver_builtin.rvl`), so the fence
    closes to exactly "host-object results are on the audit surface" —
    the G8 line as designed, with no accidental hole through the builtin
    table. Same fault line, tooling half: the mapiter run proved the
    host/stdlib name collision is *latent* on the contributor side too —
    adding `remove` to `_BUILTIN_METHODS` silently reclassified every
    host stub's `store.remove(k)` and broke 16 tests, with the
    disjointness invariant living only in a test assertion
    (findings-mapiter §2). The guard is now at the source: a module-load
    assertion in `typecheck.py`/`lower.py` that the builtin and host-verb
    tables stay disjoint except for the documented `remove` overlap,
    failing at *table-edit* time with the collision named, not at
    golden-diff time.
    (c) **Type-parameter hygiene** — ✅ **closed.** The implicit
    one-letter heuristic could not be turned off, so a typo'd one-letter
    type name silently quantified instead of erroring. When a fn carries
    an explicit `[T]` list, the heuristic is now *off* for that
    signature: declared means declared, and a stray one-letter name is an
    ordinary undeclared (opaque nominal) type that errors where it is
    used (`examples/rejections/t25_explicit_tparam_heuristic_off.rvl`).
    Bounds (`[T: Ord]`) stay deferred — no consumer demands them yet; the
    decision (not the machinery) is noted in docs/generics.md.
    Each closure follows the t8–t20 pattern: a rejection file naming the
    tier error it prevents, plus the false-positive tests that keep the
    legal spellings compiling. Exit: the errata entries for (b) and (c)
    move from "fenced, open" to closed-with-lock, and (c)'s heuristic
    interaction is pinned in docs/generics.md. (a) remains open as
    specified, deferred to wave 9.

     interaction is pinned in docs/generics.md.

77. (issue #75) [FR-1..11 re-tested vs harness (agent/harness-dogfood): verified; 3 per-tier follow-ups → 175/176/177 + nits 178] **The lighthouse harvest — an agent harness as the lighthouse
    (dogfood/findings-harness.md).** The roadmap's lighthouse workload
    ("glitch agents / DSH") is now being built for real as a revl
    composition (a separate repo from the language repo, first green on
    py), and its findings are the language's first workload-shaped bug
    list. In
    cost order:
    (a) **Expressible bounded iteration over effectful calls in a
    component (P0).** The agent loop — prompt → model → tools → model,
    bounded by max_steps — cannot be written in revl: provide-method
    bodies have no `while`/`for`/`if` (G6), arrow *parameters* are not
    bound in provide-method scope (so the pure-helper + callback escape
    fails exactly inside components), a component cannot call its own
    provision, and service methods are not first-class values. The
    harness shipped `step(session_id)` with the loop in the driver —
    honest, but the loop is the workload's core and it is exiled to host code.
    Smallest fix: bind arrow params in provide-method scope (lower.py),
    which unblocks recursion-with-callbacks; strongest fit: a bounded
    `loop`/iterate component form. This is the single highest-value
    change for "first product in revl".
    (b) **Wire `revl run --backend ts`.** DSH is TS/Cordis and the
    lighthouse entry explicitly targets the ts tier, yet `run --backend
    ts` says "not wired yet". Without it the harness cannot dogfood the
    tier the reference harness runs on. Mirror the rust tier's `--once`
    round-trip (emit → node boot → LIFO teardown → no-residue proof).
    (c) **`Map` values beyond `String` on rust.** The tier's host `Map`
    is hardcoded `HashMap<String, String>`; a session ledger
    (`Map[Str, List[Msg]]`) fails E0308/E0599. Genericize the value type
    from the IR, as py/go already do.
    (d) **Diagnostics that name the method-body surface.** "not a
    declared requirement" for an arrow parameter actively misdirects
    (the hint suggests `requires msgs: <Service>`); "found 'while'" reads
    as a parse error but is a deliberate G6 boundary. One line in
    guide-ai-agents.md ("provide-method bodies admit: let, var, assign,
    effect…undo…, emit, return") plus the arrow-param diagnostic saves
    every author the probe loop this harvest ran.
    (e) **Run/test driver parity.** `revl test --backend java` compiles
    `--release 21` (matching the Java 21 pattern-switch emitter) but
    `revl run --backend java` compiles `--release 17` and fails on any
    `match`; `revl run` has no `go` backend while `revl test --backend
    go` exists; the go v3 emitter lowers `return None` to a bare
    identifier. Each is a small parity fix with a named test.
    (f) **Lifecycle tests on non-py tiers + a verdict-column summary.**
    `revl test --all` reports "4 tier(s) failed" for py-only lifecycle
    tests by design; the no-residue claim is asserted only on the
    reference tier, and the summary line should be
    `pass/skip:reason/fail` so the by-design refusals read as skips.
    **Status (FR-5, first half):** the verdict column is in (each tier
    prints `pass` / `skip: reason` / `fail`, plus a
    `summary: N pass, M skipped, K failed` line), and `lifecycle test`
    lowers to native drivers on rust (`#[test]` over live cordis-rs),
    ts (async vitest over live cordis), and go (`go test` over the live
    stc-go runtime), each proving `assert no_residue` with the
    tier-neutral `registry().len() == 0` shape (R4) plus an R1
    host-resource pairing; the leaky-undo and left-loaded negative cases
    fail on all of py/ts/rust/go. **Remaining:** java (cordis4j) and wasm
    still refuse lifecycle tests by name — `--all` reports those refusals
    as skips with the reason, and lowering them is a follow-up (wasm has
    no live runtime; java needs a lifecycle driver against cordis4j).
    Exit: a harness-shaped composition (session ledger + string-protocol
    tools + step loop) compiles and runs on py/ts/rust/go/java, the agent
    loop is expressible in a component (or the diagnostic says exactly
    why not), `revl run --backend ts` boots a `--once` round-trip, and
    the four parity bugs have red tests before/after.
    **Status (FR-1…FR-11, verified against the harness re-test):** all
    eleven landed; the harness now runs the agent loop *inside* the
    component on py (`loop.run(prompt)`, recursion-with-callbacks over
    effectful arrows — FR-1's exact shape), lifecycle tests pass on
    py/ts/rust (`revl test --all` now prints a pass/skip/fail verdict
    column), `revl run --backend ts` and `--backend rust` both boot the
    composition to NO-RESIDUE (FR-2/FR-4), and the toolbox dropped its
    hand-rolled `parse_int` for `Str.to_int()` and `startsWith`
    (FR-6/FR-9). The re-test surfaced three follow-ups, filed in
    `dogfood/findings-harness-followup.md`:
    (i) the **TS emitter does not bind arrow parameters in component
    scope** — FR-1 fixed the frontend (parse/check/IR) and the py tier,
    but `revl run --backend ts` still refuses
    "reference to unbound name 'msgs' in component 'Agent'" when a
    provide-method body passes an arrow into a helper fn; the ts
    component-dialect scope (backends/typescript/emit.py `scope.locals`)
    needs the same arrow-param binding FR-1 gave lower.py;
    (ii) **java's host `Map` stub remains `HashMap<String, String>`** —
    FR-4 genericized rust only; the java emitter's host stub
    (emit.py:1956) still hardcodes String values, so the session ledger
    fails `incompatible types: List<T> conforms to String`;
    (iii) **go's live component world is v1/v2-only** — a v3 typed-core
    composition (records/ADTs) emits "placement on the go backend needs
    v1/v2 services"; fine as a documented tier line, but the FR-8
    run-driver claim should say *v1/v2 compositions*.
    Exit status: the harness's step-shaped composition now boots and
    proves no-residue on py/ts/rust; the loop-shaped one on py only, until
    (i) lands.
    **Status (milestone 2 — real HTTP provider, verified):** (i), (ii)
    and (iii) all landed (`revl run --backend ts` now boots the
    loop-shaped harness; java Map values are generic; go places v3
    records/ADTs). The harness's milestone 2 (a real OpenAI-compatible
    `http_post` emission extern with `@py`/`@ts` bodies, JSON wire
    protocol on FR-3's `json_parse`/`json_stringify`) runs end-to-end on
    py and ts: `agent.run("add 2 3")` → HTTP → tool call → answer, with
    lifecycle tests 3/3 on both tiers. Milestone 2 surfaced two new
    findings, both filed:
    (iv) **multi-line extern bodies are copied verbatim by the py and ts
    emitters** — the author's indentation inside `@py { }` landed inside
    the emitted function and broke Python (`IndentationError` at boot,
    not at compile); fixed on `agent/fr13-extern-dedent`
    (`textwrap.dedent` before splitting lines, both emitters). The
    failure class is "emitter produced something the tier rejects", which
    `compile` does not check — a `--validate` flag (parse emitted code
    with the tier's real compiler) would make it a red compile instead of
    a boot-time error (findings-extern-dedent.md).
    (v) **JSON narrowed multi-tier reach, honestly**: switching the wire
    protocol to JSON moved the harness from "runs on py/ts/rust/go/java"
    (string protocol) to "runs on py/ts" — `json_parse` has no @rs/@go
    bodies (FR-3's documented scope: an `Any` return type-erases on rust,
    go can't reach `encoding/json`), so rust/go now refuse with the
    honest "no @rs body" message. This is the FR-3 tradeoff made visible:
    a harness that wants structured args on all six tiers needs the JSON
    module on those tiers (or a protocol per tier). The string protocol
    still runs everywhere and remains the multi-tier fallback.
    Exit status update: harness milestone 2 (real model, JSON protocol)
    green on py + ts with the G8 boundary surface exactly one crossing
    (`http_post`); string-protocol variant keeps the full-tier matrix.
## Toward early production

The items above complete and harden the *language*. These turn it into
something a second team can build on and operate. Multi-runtime stays (see IR
versioning and the Cordis backends); nothing here blesses a single tier.

### 1. Soundness: close or fence every gap, prove each with a rejection test

The one promise revl sells is that the checker is the admission gate: a
component that would corrupt a running system cannot compile. That promise is
only as strong as its weakest silent gap, so the soundness surface has to be
complete and self-proving.

- ✅ **Enumerate the contract.** Every guarantee G1-G8 and lifecycle rule
  A1-A8 is now accounted for in the executable spec. Ten (G1-G4, G6, G8, A1,
  A2, A6, A8) have a rejection fixture in `examples/rejections/` that the
  checker must refuse with the guarantee-naming diagnostic. The other six are
  **not independently checker-rejectable** — G5 and A5 are prevented by the
  grammar (an `effect` in an `undo`/teardown position is a parse error, and
  `compensate` is an optional slot with no "required-but-missing" program),
  G7 is derived-by-lowering (Thm. 16, not a compile check), and A3/A4/A7 are
  lowering transforms (identifiers renamed, `$$` escaped, flags advisory) —
  each documented, with where it is actually enforced, in a block after the
  `REJECTIONS` table. The one plausible hole checked (an emission reached from
  a teardown body) is sound: it stays on the G8 audit surface. So the suite
  *plus that block* is the definition of sound, not a sample.
- **Close the known holes** (today: "sound where declared, silent where
  unknown", item 12):
  - ✅ stdlib method return types are now typed on known receivers, so
    `charAt`/`charCodeAt` misuse is a compile error (`s.charAt(0) + 1` where
    an `Int` is expected is rejected). Host-*provenance* objects (an extern's
    return) still infer to unknown and stay silent — fenced in
    docs/contract-errata.md, on the G8 audit surface.
  - the "Typing follow-ups" under Next (generics instantiation, match-arm
    check-position, component-setup op sweep) are soundness work, not
    ergonomics; treat them as blockers here.
  - **stratum-3 function values are unchecked** (function-types.md §5):
    arrow syntax is accepted in component and provide-method bodies, but
    `infer_ir` types no arrow and no call through one there — checked at
    the service boundary, then silent inside. The G4 side is hardened
    (first-class emission references propagate `*`); the *typing* side
    remains open. Documented in function-types.md, but the standard this
    section sets is the errata, loudly — either close it or move the
    fence there.
  - nullary user-ADT constructors as values inside `test`-block expression
    scope — closed: a `test` body is the same expression scope a `fn` body
    is, so a bare `FirstTime` resolves as a value
    (`test_nullary_ctor_resolves_in_test_block` pins it).
- **Fence what stays open.** Anything not closed for a release gets a named
  entry in `docs/contract-errata.md` as a *known* unsoundness, with its trigger
  and blast radius, the way the cordis-rs A1 divergence already is. "Silent
  where unknown" is acceptable only when it is loud in the errata.

Exit test: no reachable program can violate a guarantee without either a
compile error or a documented errata entry.

### 2. `revl run`: one command, on a released runtime, any tier

**Status: roundtrip closed on py.** `revl run <manifest> --backend py` is a
first-class command (ba33674, extended by `--placement` 5ce5e55, replay's
`--record`, and the config-admission preflight): it compiles, boots the
composition on cordis-py with no host-language driver, streams the
load/lifecycle/host trace, and holds a live REPL over the provided services
(`:keys` to list, `:q` to quit; `--once` boots, tears down and exits for CI).
On EOF/Ctrl-C it unwinds LIFO and *proves* no residue (registry, provisions,
disposables, listeners back to baseline) before exiting. Config is a host
concern and is preflighted at admission — a component admitted with a missing
required config field refuses the run with a clear diagnostic, and the runtime
is loaded only after that check, so the diagnostic works with no cordis
installed. `--watch` is the file-watching hot-swap loop (demo/live.py
promoted to a first-class command): it recompiles on edit, refuses a rejected
or mis-hosted edit while keeping the running generation untouched, and swaps
in the new generation with derived LIFO teardown. `--placement` splits a
composition across processes. The end-to-end boot/exit contract is exercised
against the real runtime in `tests/test_run.py`; see also
docs/guide-humans.md (Tooling). A plain `python -m revl run
examples/user_cache.rvl --config cfg.toml < /dev/null` runs the whole
round-trip non-interactively.

Still open:

- [ ] the other tiers are not runnable — `--backend ts|rust|java|wasm` refuses
  loudly today (exit 2); each needs its runtime wired behind the same driver
  contract. The roadmap's multi-runtime exit test needs every tier to boot
  from the one command, not just py.
- [ ] pin cordis-py to a *tagged* release cut from `harden-fiber-lifecycle`
  (the R1-R5 documented contract): cordis-rs already targets 0.3.0 and
  cordis4j a source tag, so nobody builds the flagship tier against a branch.

Exit test: `git clone && revl run examples/...` prints a running composition on
every supported tier with no host-code edits.

### 3. Lighthouse workload: glitch agents (the self-deploying harness, DESIGN §1)

DESIGN §1's second motivation is a self-evolving agent harness: agents author
components, and revl is "the language such a harness could require its own
components to pass" before they self-deploy. **glitch agents** are the candidate
dogfood for exactly that. Running one real workload on revl is what turns
"research language" into "the language we run X on", and it will order the work
in 1 and 2 faster than any roadmap guess.

Shape to validate:
- agents emit `.rvl` components; `revl compile`/`audit` is the pre-deploy
  admission gate (reject means do not deploy, with the guarantee-naming
  diagnostic fed back to the generating agent);
- accepted components hot-swap into the running agent system via
  `revl run --watch`, with derived LIFO teardown on rollback so a bad
  generation reverts to no residue;
- the G8 boundary surface (externs, emissions) is the review surface for
  anything a generated component reaches outside its declared capabilities.

**Candidate answer (2026-08): DeepSeek Harness.** The first open question
below — what the harness runs on, and where revl slots in — now has a
concrete candidate. DSH (developer preview, 2026-08) is an agent harness
whose plugin kernel is Cordis itself: every capability, including the agent
loop, is a Cordis (TS) plugin, mounted and hot-swapped at runtime, and its
self-modification mode mounts *runtime-generated* plugins with no
pre-admission verification. That is precisely the gap this section
describes: revl slots in as the admission gate — the agent authors a `.rvl`
component, `compile`/`audit` admits or refuses it with the guarantee-naming
diagnostic, and an admitted component is emitted by the TS tier onto the
same runtime family DSH already runs on. What this sharpens:

- the target tier for this workload is **ts**, not py — which promotes the
  TS half of `revl run`'s "other tiers" item and gives item 14
  (`revl import cordis`) its consumer: a lighthouse composition will want
  to *require* an existing DSH plugin, not only replace one;
- a concrete first demo exists: orchestrate a real published DSH plugin
  (e.g. `dsh-ios`, a Cordis plugin with clear acquire/undo pairs —
  boot/shutdown, install/uninstall) from a revl composition, inject a
  failure mid-sequence, and show derived LIFO teardown leaving no residue;
- version reality check first: DSH pins its Cordis version — confirm the
  TS backend's emitted plugin shape loads on that pin before building
  anything else on this entry.

Still open:
- glitch agents remain the *authoring* side of the dogfood (who generates
  the components); DSH answers the *hosting* side. Whether the first
  lighthouse pairs them or starts DSH-only is unresolved;
- whether agents author whole components or fragments, which sets how much
  of `revl run --watch` is needed first.

### 4. Distribution & visibility, right-sized

A second team cannot build on a toolchain it cannot install. Right-sized
means: ship the cheap high-leverage pieces now, and *decline* the expensive
conventional ones that revl's shape does not actually need yet.

- **Publish the compiler to PyPI.** `pyproject.toml` (hatchling) already
  builds it; the missing step is a release workflow so `pip install revl`
  works from a clean machine. Cut releases from tags, and gate the publish
  on the same CI that gates main — a version on PyPI is a claim the suite
  passed.
- ✅ **A tree-sitter grammar.** The cheap, high-visibility slice of editor
  support: syntax highlighting in every tree-sitter host (GitHub, Neovim,
  Zed, Helix) for the cost of one grammar file, kept honest by parsing the
  same corpus the reference parser and selfhost/parser.rvl already agree
  on.
- **Explicitly deferred: a human-facing LSP.** For an agent-first language
  the MCP server *is* the language server — diagnostics, why-traces,
  in-memory compile, live-session state are already served to the client
  that writes most revl today. A conventional LSP duplicates that surface
  for a second client class and is worth building only when human authors
  are the bottleneck; record the decision here so its absence reads as a
  choice, not a gap.
- **The attention-readiness bundle — shipped *before* the item 18
  write-up goes out, not after.** The evidence items (18–20) are designed
  to generate inbound attention, and the repo currently has no front door
  for it. All cheap, all sequenced ahead of the post:
  - `SECURITY.md` — a project making safety claims without a disclosure
    path is a bad look on day one;
  - `CONTRIBUTING.md` — guide-ai-agents.md teaches agents to *use* revl,
    not to contribute to it; the wave/worktree/integration-branch
    workflow, the pre-commit contract, and the "every rejection joins the
    executable spec" rule live only in commit history today. Write it for
    both contributor kinds this repo actually gets: humans and agents;
  - issue templates — a bug report that arrives with the `.rvl` source,
    the tier, and the emitted output is triageable; one that arrives as
    prose is a day of archaeology;
  - a published **stability statement** — the frozen-v1 invariant is iron
    discipline internally but exists nowhere as a *promise*; state what
    is stable (v1 IR, byte-identical), what is versioned (ir_version
    tiers), and what may break without notice. Required before the PyPI
    publish above anyway — a version number on an index is implicitly
    this promise, so make it explicit.

## Declined, deliberately

Decisions against, consolidated so they read as choices and are not
relitigated item by item. Each lives in detail where cited:

- **The cordis4j global-realm fix** — errata'd, characterization-tested,
  revisited only if global realms on Java become load-bearing

### Reconsidered and reversed (kept on record so the change of direction is explicit, not the original stance)

- **Full self-hosting** — originally stopped at the differential-oracle
  boundary; later pursued and landed. The A to C to B arc reached a native
  `revl_compile`: `selfhost/lower.rvl` produces the full interchange IR
  natively (items 242, 241), and `selfhost/compile.rvl` compiles both
  function and component programs fully-native, byte-exact against the
  reference on py and rust with no reference in the chain (items 230, 262).
  The six tier emitters are self-hosted and cross-checked. What remains is
  native-tier performance (items 283, 284) and per-tier run coverage, not
  the self-hosting question itself.
- **A human-facing LSP** — originally deferred to the MCP server; later
  un-deferred and built as slice 1 (item 126): `src/revl/lsp/` over stdio,
  with diagnostics, hover, and go-to-definition.
  (docs/backends-roadmap.md).
- **wasm tier boundaries** (config blocks, host builtins, non-Int
  service types) — deliberate substrate-tier limits, each a precise
  EmitError; item 41's slices are the sanctioned path past them.
- **Mechanized metatheory** (Lean/Coq) — not pursued; the research claim
  is transfer-by-construction (docs/vision.md), and mechanization taxes
  the iteration speed that is the project's best asset. Revisit only
  alongside a paper submission that demands it.
- **Copying the workflow-engine signal pattern wholesale** — item 48
  states the mapping instead; only durable delivery and event
  subscription are real gaps.
- **Sandboxing extern host code in-place** — item 24's threat-model
  non-goal; item 45's quarantine tier is the architectural answer.
- **A component repository's *scale tier* before key namespacing** —
  re-scoped 2026-08: the collision argument gates publishing at scale
  (item 49 phase 2, still behind item 9's namespacing), not the
  read/reuse path — G2 already refuses colliding providers at
  admission, so phase 0 (structural search + fetch + reuse hints) is
  unblocked and prioritized. The demand test ("a second author")
  survives as phase 2's gate; the second *agent* author is already
  here.
- **A first-class `stream` type** — the WIT importer already took the
  stance (docs/import-wit.md §2): `stream<T>` is an open-ended effect,
  not a value — model it as a service whose operations are `emission`.
  Streams-as-services keeps every guarantee's surface intact; a native
  stream type would need its own lifecycle story on six tiers for a
  construct the service model already expresses. Revisit only if item
  41's Component Model work makes wire-level streams unavoidable.
- **Placement discovery/orchestration/scheduling** — item 56 stops at
  static placement maps over machines. revl is not becoming an
  orchestrator; it composes with one.

## Invariants (checked every step)

- v1 goldens byte-identical; frontend/backend-py/backend-ts suites green
  (currently 200 / 21 / 24).
- Every new rejection lands in `examples/rejections/` and joins the executable
  spec (`tests/test_frontend.py` REJECTIONS).
- Error messages are a deliverable (DESIGN §9): each names its guarantee and
  the fix — and is written for the model that will read it.

Curated from the 2026-08-24 strategy review. **Excluded as already built:** TCP+mTLS
bridge transport (item 56), Wasm sandboxing for generated code (item 45, quarantine

These items are horizon work, not the current wave; each notes what it builds on.

### Distributed execution
118. ◑ (issue #79) DESIGN DONE 2026-09-01 (docs/design/118-revl-deploy.md, two independent design passes reconciled into one doc). SLICE 1 LANDED 2026-09-06 (merged c443c45c: the deploy map/descriptor and the `revl deploy` CLI; the apply/rollback path stays deferred). Slice split: S1 [landable on today's code, single-host / local-multiprocess] full attestation chain verified at admission, cross-process LIFO rollback via a coordinated protocol, effect-correlation identity on every seam crossing, all-or-nothing federation over co-located compositions, reusing apply/placement/bridge [item-56 TCP+mTLS Endpoint already exists]/recovery/attest/federation/version/item-309 idempotency_key; S2+ [deferred, the heavy part] cross-machine mTLS orchestration + replicated WAL [none exists in-tree] + partition-safe distributed commit coordinator. TWO CRITICALs folded: (1) the LIFO rollback theorem apply proves is IN-PROCESS and does not cross a seam, so cross-seam rollback needs a coordinated protocol not a lift of apply.py; (2) federation split-brain under partition, fixed by holding every irreversible crossing as an item-245 class-(b) deferred emission until a durable federation-commit-approved record exists, a stranded participant applying recovery.py's record-present-roll-forward / absent-roll-back rule and failing closed, and refusing admission of a plan that necessarily crosses a non-deferrable irreversible effect. Binding rules: a receiver re-hashes the IR + artifact bytes it will execute (never trusts the attestation's self-declared backend/hash), and correlation envelopes are authenticated against the mTLS peer identity [dedup scoped on peer_identity, composition_id, generation, idempotency_key]. Note: item 253 (Temporal target, landed) is the cheap distributed alternative, so 118's impl stays DEFERRED until the attested-supply-chain provenance is itself the goal. **`revl deploy` — composition as a distributed deployment.** Extend `apply` to remote hosts (SSH/container per component), bridge over TCP (item 56), WAL replicated across nodes, LIFO rollback on partial failure. Builds on `apply.py`/`distribute.py`/`placement.py`/`recovery.py`. ~2-3 wk. Novelty: verified deployment with LIFO rollback on partial failure. Note 2026-09-29: the private pool (item 524, issue #1198) is this item's caller, not a second transport. `revl run --pool private` dispatches over its own signed, unencrypted loopback channel and names this item's mTLS work as its confidential cross-machine channel; `via = peer` in a deploy map is still refused as `peer-pool-unavailable`. 

    **+ attested supply-chain deploy (external proposal #7, reconcile here not a new item).** Link source -> IR hash -> backend-artifact hash -> capability policy -> gauntlet evidence -> signature -> deployment -> runtime trace, building on attest.py (item 127, done), truc, federation, mTLS, and the item-33 policy gate. A remote host REJECTS an artifact if: its source or IR hash changed, its backend differs from the attestation, its capability set expanded, its policy is incompatible, its evidence is stale, or its signer is untrusted. Gives `revl deploy` a cryptographically-linked identity: deploy only artifacts whose source, guarantees, capabilities, and runtime target are all linked. 

    **+ transactional multi-composition federation update (external proposal #5, folded here).** `revl apply federation.plan` commits an atomic change across several compositions (replace model in A, migrate router in B, update policy in C) ONLY IF every composition admits + network contracts stay compatible + no capability surface expands + state handoff succeeds + all health probes pass; otherwise the WHOLE federation returns to its previous generation. All-or-nothing distributed control plane.
 

    **+ distributed effect correlation (external proposal #6, folded here).** Every effect crossing a TCP+mTLS boundary carries a common identity (composition_id, generation, realm, effect_id, idempotency_key, parent_effect) so a remote provider correlates the chain for recovery, OTel (120/121), duplicate detection, cross-process teardown, and incident investigation.

    **+ STRATEGIC NOTE (external-reviewer batch, 2026-08-26).** An external reviewer's whole 'productionization' cluster - reproducible bundle, migration planner, effect correlation, production fault-injection, SLO contracts, resource-aware placement - is DOWNSTREAM of THIS item's remote transport. Build 118's remote parts (cross-machine mTLS, replicated WAL, distributed rollback) FIRST; the production-ops layer stands on it. It is the single highest-leverage unlock for that batch.
 

    **+ 253 is the cheap distributed ALTERNATIVE to this item (Fable).** Item 253 (Temporal emission target) gives durable/distributed/compensating execution on someone else's cluster as an emit-target variant, with NO transport layer to build - so if distribution pressure arrives before 118's remote transport is affordable, 253 is the answer, which strengthens the case that 118 is safely deferrable.

    **+ SLICE 2a LANDED 2026-09-02 (PR #237): the deploy map, and the boundary a deploy may cross.** Slice 1 (`53550baf`) built the coordinated PREPARE/COMMIT protocol and left the caller to construct `Participant`s in Python, so a deploy could not be WRITTEN DOWN and therefore could not be refused before things were spawned. Slice 2a added the written form — the item-56 placement map with one new per-process `[deploy]` table (`via` / `image` / `host` / `runner` / `trust`), where a placement with no `[deploy]` table anywhere is still a valid deploy map — plus `admit_deploy_map`, the plan-time admission of it. The map is UNAUTHENTICATED OPERATOR INPUT (design R4: not in the attested bundle, nothing signs it, and it is the file that says which machine runs the composition), so admission is all-or-nothing and every rule REFUSES rather than downgrades — item 428's audit found several fail-open shapes in this same file and none of these has a warn-and-continue path: `unknown-via` (an unimplemented `via` is not "assume local"), `machine-boundary` (`via = ssh` refused outright, not best-efforted), `container-seam` (the seam is a UDS and a UDS does not cross a container bind mount portably — `sandbox_runtime` refuses the same shape on the same measurement), `seam-set-unknown` ("not told" is not "none"; the shape `federation_admission` already uses for an unknown provider), `network-provider` (its address is a contract other machines hold, so moving it is the re-tier `revl swap` refuses), `no-trust-store` (a receiver that cannot verify the chain makes this a copy, not a deploy), `container-without-image`, `local-with-remote-fields`. **The organizing idea is the BOUNDARY, not the launch mechanism** — `process` / `container` / `machine` — because G7 is LIFO-complete over the REGISTERED entries of an accumulator and each boundary changes which set outlives the failure. `TEARDOWN_PROMISE` records one answer per boundary so two mechanisms crossing the same boundary cannot pick up different guarantees: a `process` participant runs its own G7 unwind and one the conductor cannot reach is `unresolved`, never `rolled-back`; a DESTROYED `container` takes the accumulator and every closure-only inverse with it so the verdict is `unresolved`, but its WAL is on a mount this side also holds so the target stays SETTLE-able by `recovery.py`'s existing rule (settling is a separate step and the deploy never claims it happened); a `machine` promises NOTHING, which is why it is refused — a machine that goes away takes the accumulator, the closures AND the WAL, so there is no set on this side for G7 to be complete over. `launch_container_participant` then puts a real participant behind a container boundary; it is a `ProcessParticipant` and nothing more, which is the finding — the protocol never held an inverse, so it does not care what kind of boundary the other end is behind. It reuses item 411's `container_flags`, so the boundary gets the same hardening and the same `revl.sandbox=411` label the leaked-container audit watches. **One fail-open shape came out of building it and is now a refusal:** container mounts are identity-mapped, so a spec whose `world`/`wal` path is non-canonical (a macOS `/var` -> `/private/var` symlink suffices) names a file that does not exist inside the boundary — PREPARE reads nothing and succeeds, then the participant dies MID-COMMIT with a `FileNotFoundError`, at the one point where `unresolved` is the only verdict left. Tests: `tests/test_deploy_map_118.py`, 17 refusals needing no runtime plus 3 gated on `REVL_SANDBOX_DOCKER` (the item-445 env-gate pattern) that run a participant inside a real container (`pid: 1`, WAL out through the one rw mount) and destroy one mid-protocol to pin `unresolved` with a surviving settle-able WAL; added to the `sandbox-container` CI job so the gated level runs where the runtime is. **Still absent, and the reason this item stays ◑:** the machine boundary's whole control plane (bundle staging, a remote `deploy-admit` runner, a load-measured signed COMMIT receipt the conductor compares [R2], a pinned SSH host key [R4], receipt-key provenance [R5]), a replicated WAL, a partition-safe quorum commit coordinator, a seam-carrying container target (blocked on item 411's per-rung seam transport), the `revl deploy` CLI command that would wrap this library surface, the Ed25519 upgrade to `attest.py`, and hardware remote attestation. The deferred S2 coordinator is the typed multi-agent SAGA: participants classified reversible / deferred-irreversible / forward-recovery / best-effort-compensation / final-approval, committing one durable decision (commit-all or compensate-all) rather than a fictional global transaction.

119. ✅ **Capability/realm-aware placement optimization.** `placement.py` places components on backends (which tier); extend to place on HOSTS by capability, realm, topology, resource (`emission[database]` near the db; `realm("eu")` in the EU). Compile-time-verified deployment constraint. Builds on `placement.py`/`plan.py`/realm+capability systems. ~2-3 wk. High novelty.

Connects the capability/realm system to embedded/edge/cloud. Relates to wasm runtime limits (289).

121. ◑ (issue #80) SLICE 1 LANDED 2026-09-01 (revl trace model-crossing surface, merged a8f2b7f: honest-degrade producedSeq with fiber-token isolation, salted-HMAC prompt digest + coarse bytesBucket [no raw prompt/response text on any span], secret/confidential SUPPRESSES rather than refuses, revl-measured latency bracket with host-reported provenance tags, byte-identical non-model emit; mechanism + reader + OTel + CLI wiring, 24 + 206 tests. run.py driver-glue landed (merged 6d14799: model-crossing payload assembled at the step-back crossing site via revl_model_hop, byte-identical non-model emit); the live digest stays fail-closed and producedSeq honest-degrades to absent until a compile-to-runtime taint-origin + completion-token channel feeds them, and Slice 2 evidence-derived retries are deferred). Slice 1's model-hop attribution was corrected 2026-09-02 (PR #248, closes #242): the discriminator was a fiber-wide register consumed by whichever crossing the driver processed first, and `replay.Timeline.step_back` reports `emissionsCrossed` NEWEST FIRST, so a completion followed by a later non-model crossing gave its model/token/cost/latency numbers to that crossing (it could not forge a `produced` edge — the back-patch requires the target to carry an `llm` payload, so a misattached payload degrades to no edge — the hop itself named the wrong crossing). `Timeline.record_emission` now marks each crossing at RECORD time with `(component, stepIndex)`, the `validate_retry` seam binds its observation to the crossing `make_call` just recorded, and the driver reads the entry for the crossing it is recording, so two completions in one body stay apart and the walk order stops mattering. SLICE 2 LANDED 2026-09-02 (the produced edge, proved rather than guessed, PR #241: the emitter's must-derive value-flow analysis marks a crossing whose arguments read a validated completion's binding, the seam mints a fiber-local token holding that completion CROSSING's replay.Step.index keyed by static site rather than by recency, the recorder stamps detail[producedBy], and the driver maps step index -> trace seq and back-patches llm[producedSeq] so the edge points FORWARD at the emission the hop produced instead of the self-loop the inverted read would have exported as an OTel SpanLink; activation id gains its per-activation ordinal (component#gN#aK), replacing the component+gen the design's NEW CRITICAL calls insufficient; the digest posture is untouched and every unproven path OMITS the edge; byte-identical emit for any body with no validated-retry completion). Still open under this item: cross-host distributed trace stitching and evidence-derived retries. DESIGN DONE (docs/design/121-revl-trace.md). **`revl trace` — distributed tracing with LLM hops.** Extend causal traces with LLM fields per agent decision (prompt, model, token count, cost, latency, confidence), linked to the emission it produced and the G-rule that verified it. Builds on `why_runtime.py`/`why.py`/replay timeline. ~1 wk. Novelty: LLM-aware tracing with compile-time-verification context.

Builds on the interchange format, `audit_diff.py`, the runtime trace ledger. ~1-2 wk.

### Security & safety
128. ✅ **Prompt-injection resistance — framing/documentation (effort ~0, it already works).** Capability confinement already means a generated component cannot exceed its declared emissions regardless of what a prompt injection says — G4 rejects at compile time (language-level, not prompt-level, defense). Articulate and document this guarantee. Builds on G4 + the agent generation loop. Documentation, not a build.

### Language features
130. ◑ (issue #81) SLICES 1-5 LANDED (the whole named v1 surface; the remaining work is tiers and durability, listed below). 2026-09-29: PR 1523 in review lowers §4.5's last-n replay backlog on ts, go and java; the durable cursor stays refused by name there, and rust keeps refusing both forms. §4.5 REPLAY-REFUSAL LANDED 2026-09-06 (merged b25a7bbf: a `replay(...)` at a subscribe is now refused). S1-S3 on main: `Stream[T]` / `Stream[T, State]`, the subscribe/next/close bracket with the cancellation-first `next` and rule 3.6, `map`/`filter`/`take` with the declared backpressure policies and the drain clock, and the `merge` fan-in with the go/rust blocking lowerings. S4 `every x in sub { … }` async iteration (#149): the iteration boundary `yield` sits immediately after the await so a divert while parked abandons the loop, a `Closed` terminal ends it without becoming an item, and a `Faulted` is deliberately NOT caught so the activation fails and the prefix reverts LIFO with the subscription bracket on it; an acquisition in the body is REFUSED (§4.7 allows `effect … undo …` there and requires a per-iteration discharge to bound it, which does not exist — one accumulator entry per delivered item is unbounded in stream length). S5 typed events (#260): `event E(key: f[, window: n]) { … }` + `on E as e in sub { … }`, which parses to the SAME `StreamIterStmt` and lowers to the SAME `stream-iter` step as S4 (§6's specialization call, so the two forms cannot drift on the properties that carry the guarantee), plus an additive contract: the item is TYPED inside the handler (an undeclared field / a field at the wrong type are compile errors) and every delivered item is validated against the derived schema at the boundary before the body runs, on item 257's machinery and under its §3.3 refuse-an-inexact-schema rule; a REQUIRED item-309 identity key plus a BOUNDED dedup window collapses redeliveries (constant memory per handler, so the §4.7 acquisition refusal is untouched — it bounds nothing an acquisition would need bounded). A schema violation raises the same `Faulted` a provider abort does, so "a failed handler does not leave a subscription active" is delivered by A8 with no events-specific teardown. `event`/`on` are CONTEXTUAL keywords (the corpus uses `event` as a parameter name), so neither lexer needs a sync. Also closed on go: a document with top-level declarations routed to that tier's pure typed-core path, which DROPS components — every event program hits it, so go would have emitted a bare struct and silently never subscribed; it now refuses a dropped component holding a stream, by name. Also closed on rust (S5): the `on … as` typed-event handler is lowered, so an event program on that tier compiles to the blocking `next` loop S4 already emits with one additive `Stream::contract(<name>, <derived schema>, <identity key>, 64)` gate built ONCE above the loop and tested AFTER the `Closed => break` arm, so a terminal still ends the loop without being validated or delivered as an item; the gate's schema violation returns the same uncaught `Err` a `Faulted` propagates, so A8 reverts the prefix LIFO with the subscription bracket on it; a redelivered identity key inside the bounded window is collapsed and traced, never silently dropped. A frontend prerequisite fell out of this: the multi-file `compile_files` path copied `type_decls` into the merged program but never `event_decls`, and `_rewrite_module`/`_rewrite_stmt` never visited `StreamIterStmt`, so a CLI multi-file build could not name an event at all while the in-memory `compile_source` path could; both are fixed. STILL OPEN: the Slice 2 DRAIN WINDOW on rust and java, which is the only thing `_refuse_unlowered_stream_surface` still refuses on those tiers (its resume needs the deterministic clock no blocking tier carries, and java has no `advance` lowering at all, so lowering it would resume EARLY and quietly disagree with the reference; refusing beats a silent divergence). CORRECTED 2026-09-14, twice over. This clause previously said all four non-py tiers refuse the combinator chain and the three non-default backpressure policies. TYPESCRIPT NEVER DID: it has no call to `_refuse_unlowered_stream_surface` at all and emits `stages`, `capacity` and `drainMs` into `host.Stream.subscribe`, pinned by two tests. That error was mine, introduced in PR #1006 while correcting a different staleness in this same line, and it is the second wrong tier list this clause has carried. go, rust and java have since had both halves lowered: the three policies by #1042 and the `map`/`filter`/`take` chain by #1060, each verified by executing the tier (go under `-race`, java under real javac and a JVM, rust under cargo) rather than by reading the emitter. #1060 also closed two concurrency defects the single-threaded py reference cannot exhibit: `take(n)` read its budget and decremented AFTER forwarding, so two concurrent emits both saw `remaining == 1` and delivered n+1 items, and `emit` discarded the acceptance `deliver`/`forward` returned, so a blocked provider could never learn it was blocked. CORRECTED 2026-09-24, and this is the THIRD correction to this one sentence. Three of the four things it listed as open are closed, and the tier list on the fourth was wrong again. **go LOWERS the drain window**, landed by PR #1105, which emits `drainMs`, `drainCancel` and `streamArmDrain` on that tier, so the parenthetical above is true of rust and java only and go is no longer an instance of it. **The §4.5 `replay(n)`/`replay(from: <durable>)` declaration is landed**, by PR #1066: it parses, typechecks, lowers and carries into the IR at both ends, py emits it, and ts, go, rust and java refuse it by name. The clause that said the FRONTEND refuses it before any emitter runs was ALSO wrong, and wrong in the fail-open direction for a reader: the frontend ADMITS a declared replay and refuses only an undeclared or mismatched one, naming which (a request that outruns the declared backlog, a durable request against a last-n provider, a count request against a durable cursor, a wrong cursor name). **The §4.9 reconstructible crash-recovery case is landed**, by PR #1066 at the WAL and PR #1076 across a process boundary, so "tier-independent and unimplemented" was false twice over: it is neither tier-independent nor unimplemented. py LOWERS it and the durable cursor resumes in a FRESH PROCESS under the pinned cordis fork; ts, go, rust and java refuse by name citing §4.5 and §4.9; wasm refuses the whole stream surface. Worth stating plainly rather than leaving in the per-tier answer: the RUNTIME reconstructibility ships on py only, and what the other tiers have is an honest named refusal, which is what item 130's exit asks for and is not the same as the feature. **The required-`Stream[T]` coeffect wiring is landed**, by PR #1076: `on E as e { … }` drops its `in <sub>` clause on py, and ts, go, rust, java and wasm refuse by name citing §6b. Measured on `9615b919`: `tests/test_stream_reactive.py` is 333 passed, including the 54-cell exit matrix of nine surfaces over six tiers, and the same 333 under the pinned cordis fork, so no cordis-gated skip hides in that file; `backends/python/tests/test_stream_runtime.py` is 56 passed with the cross-restart cursor test among them. The two previous corrections to this sentence each left or introduced a further error in it, which is why every clause was re-measured this time rather than only the one that looked stale. CORRECTED 2026-09-13: this line previously read 'the iteration and handler forms on java, the only tier left that refuses them', which #982 had already made false. Both the `every … in` iteration form and the `on … as` typed-event handler are landed on java, and go, rust and typescript each carry a `stream-iter` arm, so NO tier refuses the iteration or handler forms today (wasm refuses the whole stream surface by design). Verified on origin/main 5f16937b: every emitting backend's emit.py has a `stream-iter` arm and java's `_UNIMPLEMENTED_HOST_ROOTS` is empty. was: ◑ DESIGN DONE 2026-09-01 (docs/design/130-stream-reactive-types.md; v1 per the spec below). Hard-parts decided: single-consumer v1 (multicast refused, bridge is the escape); cancellation owned by the subscription owner (its LIFO stack); provider death delivers a terminal event Closed/Faulted never silent Pending; bounded-buffer backpressure with an explicit policy (default error, no silent loss); replay only if the provider declares replay(n)/replay(from durable); six-tier protocol subscribe/next/close with py/ts async, go/java/rust blocking cancel-select, and wasm REFUSING (same honest EmitError as awaited steps); effectful callbacks allowed in the every body (G1/G8 checked, A8 on failure); cross-realm only via an explicit bridge; crash recovery non-reconstructible by default (reported as teardown-contract unreconstructible residue) unless a durable replay cursor is declared. Typed events are a Stream[T] SPECIALIZATION not a distinct surface (event schema=G1, on..as desugars to every..in subscribe), deferred to slice 5. Slice split: S1 [landable, no dep] Stream[T]+Stream[T,State]+subscribe/next/close+bracket lifecycle+core-guarantee tests, parser+typecheck+lower, py full / wasm refuses / ts if free; S2 map/filter/take+backpressure+test-clock; S3 merge+go/java/rust blocking lowerings; S4 every..in async iteration (needs item 131); S5 typed events. Adversarial review CRITICAL: a consumer parked in next on a silently-dead provider makes the bracket inverse close unreachable (deadlock on blocking tiers, fiber leak on event-loop tiers), violating the core guarantee; fixed by cancellation-first next (next awaits a race of item-vs-cancel-token, close trips the token synchronously on every tier) + admission rule 3.6 refusing any provider that can vanish without delivering a terminal. **`Stream[T]` reactive types.** A verified reactive sequence: a stream provider emits values over time, a consumer processes them as they arrive; provider teardown closes the stream, consumer teardown releases the subscription (LIFO). The language-level form of reactive coeffects for event-driven sagas (Kafka/webhooks/real-time). Needs its own lifecycle story on six tiers. Builds on the reactive coeffect model + provide/inject graph. ~3-4 wk. High novelty. **EXIT (added 2026-09-18, because this item had none and therefore could not close).** For each of the nine named stream surfaces on each of the six tiers, the answer is one of exactly two things and a test proves which: the tier LOWERS it, or the tier REFUSES IT BY NAME. Nothing is silently dropped, in the reference emitter or in its self-host port — a port that answers a stream document with a banner and no marker fails this exit as surely as a wrong lowering, because a stated refusal made silent by the port is the failure this design exists to prevent. Durability (§4.5 provider-declared `replay`, §4.9 the durable cursor across a crash) is held to the same rule. Measured 2026-09-18: the reference tiers meet it — py lowers all nine; ts and go refuse `replay` and the required-`Stream[T]` coeffect by name; rust and java additionally refuse the drain window, each naming its own reason; wasm refuses the surface entirely per §4.6. CORRECTED 2026-09-20: that gap is closed on main at `23e94c27`. Both self-host ports now refuse a stream document BY NAME, one `<<UNSUPPORTED-COMPONENT:...>>` marker per components entry, asserted by `tests/test_selfhost_emit_go.py` and `tests/test_selfhost_emit_wasm.py`, which pin the marker and keep the pre-fix sizes (144 bytes against 40502; a 376-byte module) as the record of what was wrong rather than as a live measurement. One cell of the same shape survived that fix and was found by measuring rather than by reading the prose: a stream document that ALSO declares a top-level type left the go port's marker suppression armed, so it answered with 242 characters against the reference's 38,858 and no marker anywhere. A stated refusal made silent by the port is the failure this exit exists to prevent, so that cell counts against it exactly as a wrong lowering would; it is a separate landing under issue #81.

    **v1 design spec (from an external proposal, verified: no Stream type exists yet).** Do NOT start with a general reactive calculus. v1 = `Stream[T]` + a small API (`subscribe`/`next`/`close`/`map`/`filter`/`take`/`merge`) and an `every x in subscription { ... }` async-iteration form. Support ONLY: one consumer, explicit `close`, bounded buffering, a deterministic test-time clock, documented refusals for unsupported tiers/shapes; NO implicit multicast, NO automatic retry. Model the subscription as an `acquire`/`undo` resource with an optional state type `Stream[T, State]` (Created->Active->Paused->Closed). CORE GUARANTEE: every admitted stream subscription has an explicit cancellation path, and unloading its owner closes the stream before the owner disappears (a natural extension of acquire/undo + G5/G7/A8 LIFO teardown). Hard-parts to DECIDE before implementing: single-consumer vs multicast (start single), cancellation ownership (the subscription owner), provider-death behavior (consumer becomes Pending or gets a terminal event), backpressure (bounded buffer + explicit policy), replay (only if the provider declares it), the common minimal six-tier protocol (wasm has no async, so it REFUSES streams like it refuses timers), effectful callbacks (allowed but capability+lifecycle checked), cross-realm (only via an explicit bridge), crash recovery (recreate the subscription or mark it non-reconstructible). Blast radius: parser + typecheck + lower + all six backends + async-coloring (A1/106). Design-first, ~3-4 wk. 

    **+ typed external EVENTS (external proposal #10, folded here).** A first-class event contract (`event OrderCreated { order_id: Str, timestamp: Int }`, `on OrderCreated as event { ... }`) with the checker verifying schema compatibility, consumer lifecycle, duplicate handling, event ownership, replay behavior, handler idempotency, and that a failed handler does not leave a subscription active. Related to this Stream[T] item (an event is a stream item with a contract); decide whether events are a distinct surface or a Stream specialization. Useful for queues, MCP notifications, WebSockets, IoT.

131. ✅ **Explicit async/await effect composition (surface syntax).** Async EXTERNS already land (items 80/90/92/106/115) and the runtime supports async bodies; this is the remaining piece — explicit language-level async/await for EFFECT composition (a provider doing I/O awaits without blocking the consumer's coeffect activation), with verified LIFO teardown. Scope carefully vs. what async externs already cover. ~2-3 wk. Novelty: async with verified LIFO teardown.

G4/G5/G8 were re-proved over the effect-classification lattice (step 4) and G7 over the three-kind teardown stack (step 5); `RevL.G5.teardown_registers_nothing` remains true but CONTENTLESS and is labelled so. Issue #1142 (PR #1164) and issue #1161 are in flight in other sessions. **Mechanized soundness proof (Lean/Coq).** Formalize G7 (LIFO), A8 (no residue), G4 (capability containment) as a type/effect system with a soundness proof. Huge but incremental (the checker alone is 66 KB of revl and is done). The strongest dogfood + the academic self-hosting contribution. (a) THE EMITTERS: each of the six `selfhost/emit_<tier>.rvl` reproduces its reference emitter BYTE-FOR-BYTE when fed the reference IR, over the whole enumerated corpus, with every unported construct refusing by a named marker rather than emitting nothing. MET as of 2026-09-18 and recomputed on every run since: every document of every tier's enumerated corpus, pinned by a per-tier ratchet in `tests/test_selfhost_compile.py` that reds both when a real gap is dropped from the list and when a tier is padded with a passing document. The per-tier counts are not restated here, because the three hand-typed copies of them disagreed (issue #1300); they are generated into `docs/selfhost-compile.md` from `LOWER_GAP_DOCS` and the six `CORPUS` lists. (b) `revl_compile` AS A NATIVE OPERATION: `crates/revl-gate::compile_to(src, tier)` returns emitted source byte-identical to the reference for every document the fully-native chain reproduces, instead of refusing. NOT MET: the fully-native chain does not reproduce the whole corpus, and every residual document is a `selfhost/lower.rvl` gap, i.e. item 391's arc and not this item's. The live figure is the generated table in `docs/selfhost-compile.md`; measured at `23e94c27` on 2026-09-20 it was 41 of 259, against the 59 of 255 this line carried from 2026-09-18. This item ticks when (a) holds and (b)'s residue is zero; the item-332 Stage-4 tripwire XPASSing on rust is 332's exit clause, not this one's.

186. ◑ **Path C beyond-single-source — ambient/running-manifest composition admission (lower priority).** Issue #86 tracks this item, and item 419 (c) is folded in here. The single-source `admit_src` gate (184) is complete; this mirrors `check_and_lower`'s `ambient` parameter — a DIFFERENT admission entry point (hot-swap / state-migration, not "does this one program admit"): ambient (running-manifest) composition, service replacement, `handoff` STATE replacement (184 did handoff wiring), and multi-realm routing VALIDATION (`isolate … in realms(...)` — parsed-but-unvalidated: component-level refusals + the link-time per-realm provider check, item 162). Plus residual coloring approximation (b): callee collection descends into a nested COERCED arrow instead of stopping (`stop_async_arrows`) — needs an async-typed-but-uncalled param, reached by no fixture; rule-2 (184) masks it in practice. Not required for the native single-source `revl_compile`. `selfhost/lower.rvl` + `tests/test_selfhost_lower.py`. **SHAPE, folded in from item 419f (2026-09-02) so it is not lost when 419 closes:** every one of the ambient consumers reads `check_and_lower`'s `ambient` input, and `admit_src(src)` has NO analogue (there is nothing in one program text to compare against), so this needs a NEW entry point (`admit_ambient(src, manifest)`) plus a new differential-oracle shape that constructs a running manifest on BOTH sides. That is a wave, not a slice; more corpus does not help. The bounded pieces (multi-realm routing validation, the residual coloring approximation) landed separately. **ALSO IN SCOPE, from item 419c:** `admit_src` reports the FIRST refusal it reaches, in its own phase order (module fns, per component, spawn bounds, then the G2/G3 link), while the reference's item-386 sink orders collected diagnostics by LINE, so a program carrying an earlier-line G2 and a later-line component refusal gets different (both true) answers from the two. Closing it needs a line number on EVERY gate refusal plus a collecting sink in `lower.rvl`, whose `admit_src` is also what `tools/build_gate_crate.py` digests, so the phase order is not a local edit. CLOSED 2026-09-07 (merged 16d1a225, #525): the gate gained an internal `Refusal{tag,msg,line,seq}` and a collecting sink, so `admit_src` now anchors every recoverable refusal at the line the reference's diagnostic carries and reports the minimum by `(line, seq)` — the reference's `diagnostics[0]`. The divergence pin `tests/test_selfhost_lower.py::test_which_refusal_wins_diverges_when_a_program_has_several` was deleted in that PR, as its own instructions said to do once the two agreed, and `tests/test_selfhost_lower.py::test_multi_refusal_programs_agree` pins the agreement in its place: three programs each carrying two or three TRUE refusals of distinct families on distinct lines, in a multiline and a one-line variant, where the one-line variant collapses every line to 1 so the tie-break falls to the gate's `seq` pipeline order. The corpus still deliberately keeps every case single-refusal (see `_ROUTE_PROVIDERS`), which is now a property of the oracle corpus rather than an avoidance. **LANDED 2026-09-13 (reference side of the replacement leg, issue #86):** the unmet-consumer refusal that `docs/design/186-ambient-admission-guarantees.md` decided under "Replacement semantics" is now enforced by the reference gate, not only predicted by `revl plan`. `compile_files(X, manifest=M, replacing=R)` refuses when `R` provided `(k, r)`, a RETAINED component of `M \ R` requires `k` in `r`, and `X` does not provide `(k, r)`; before this the admission was GRANTED and the consumer deactivated into PENDING at runtime with no diagnostic anywhere in the gate. The refusal names the withdrawn provider, the lost key with its realm and the consumer, and classifies as (G2, admission). Only the transition met to unmet refuses, so an already-unmet requirement still admits. `src/revl/admission.py` (`_admit_provision_withdrawal`) + `src/revl/compiler.py` (`ambient["withdrawn"]`) + `tests/test_withdrawn_provision_admission.py`. STILL OPEN and keeping this item ◑: the same refusal inside `selfhost/lower.rvl`'s `admit_ambient`, the `-C` and `C=k:T` wire rows, and oracle B.

250. ◑ (issue #87) SLICE 1 LANDED 2026-09-01 (session fork: revl_fork / revl_fork_confirm MCP verbs + a scope-gated compensate=False step_back rewind on the WAL timeline, so a branch diverges without re-running the parent's committed side effects; test_mcp tools-list updated. Full branch/replay/compare + LLM-aware WAL replay modes deferred). DESIGN DONE (docs/design/250-session-branching.md). **Session branching — fork a conversation *with its side effects
    rewound*.** The lighthouse workload has durable sessions; revl has the
    timeline, witnessed inverses (243/244) and the commit protocol
    (245). Composed, they make a primitive no harness has: `fork
    <session> at <step k>` — replay inverses back to step k (witnessed
    fs state included), snapshot, and branch: the user or the agent
    explores an alternative from a known-good point, with the
    workspace *actually in* the step-k state, not a transcript
    illusion of it. Deferred-class actions (245) make the semantics
    honest: branches hold their outbound sends until *their* commit,
    so exploring N branches costs zero external residue until one is
    chosen; already-crossed emissions on the abandoned branch are
    enumerated on its abort report (§6.1 — printed, never pretended
    away). Workload surface first (a sessions view offering "branch from
    here"), language surface later if a `fork` form earns it.
 

    **+ branchable / counterfactual execution history + LLM-aware replay (external proposals #1 and #4, reconcile here + item 121).** Branch a run at a step and diverge: `revl branch run --at N --name repaired`, `revl replay branch`, `revl compare`. The branch preserves component generation, source hash, provider versions, capability surface, LLM/tool calls, random seeds + clock state, WAL position, and inverse witnesses. Make the WAL LLM-aware: record each model decision (model, provider, prompt/response hashes, tool_calls, tokens, temperature, seed, latency) with replay MODES exact / tool-only / model-substitute / counterfactual. The distinctive capability: explain not just what the agent did, but what would have happened if it had acted differently. Overlaps item 121 (revl trace with LLM hops).
253. ◑ (issue #88) SLICE 1 LANDED 2026-09-01 (Temporal emission target, merged acc8683 + golden typecheck shim fb185c4: an emit(ir, target="temporal") seam emitting the workflow/activity split, the derived write-ahead saga, and the two-phase LIFO drain with a workflow-side compensation budget, faithful at-most-once [maximumAttempts:1] matching the revl runtime, plus a residue-query envelope. Slice 2 evidence-derived retries + real SDK/cluster integration deferred). DESIGN DONE (docs/design/253-temporal-target.md). **A Temporal emission target — the saga framework arrives with an
    installed base.** (Suggested by a worker agent; accepted with an
    alignment that makes it stronger than it first looks.) Temporal's
    two cardinal rules — workflow code must be deterministic, IO
    belongs only in activities — are *exactly* revl's stratum
    discipline: pure/effect code is the workflow, emissions are the
    activities, and revl checks at compile time the rule Temporal can
    only document and hope. So `revl emit --target temporal` (a target
    *variant* over the existing py/ts emitters — not an eighth
    runtime): a component's activation becomes a workflow, each
    `effect … undo …` registers its inverse in a compensation chain
    whose LIFO order is G7-derived (the code Temporal users write by
    hand and get wrong), `compensate` (247) maps onto Temporal's own
    best-effort idiom, and item 44's idempotency evidence configures
    activity retry policies *safely* — retries only where the type
    system says retry is sound. What the target buys free: durable
    execution (Temporal's history is item 47's WAL, operated by
    someone else), timers (57), signals (48), production retries — and
    a distribution channel: "keep your Temporal cluster; write the
    workflow in revl; the compensation chain is derived and the
    determinism rule is a compile error." Honest scope, stated first:
    this is *the durable-workflow subset* — realms, hot-swap and
    reactive withdrawal do not map in v1 (workflow versioning may
    carry drift-checked upgrades later, its own design note); a
    composition using them is refused for this target with a
    why-trace, never silently narrowed. Spec-first: the mapping table
    (component/effect/emission/fault ↔ workflow/compensation/activity/
    failure) is the design doc, and the exit test is one saga demo
    running on a real Temporal dev server with an injected mid-saga
    fault compensating in derived order.

294. ◑ (issue #94) SLICES 1+2 LANDED 2026-08-31 (Slice 2 merged 8347ade: cone-aware grant lookups via cap_order.covers [mint-narrow works, F1 no-over-coverage-across-tokens preserved], ceiling-erase-to-remainingUses, and the ticket-gated effect lease runtime [class-(c)-gated acquisition minted from an approved ticket, own-requestId revoke disposer on LIFO teardown; an ungated run refuses it, self-mint bypass closed]; 17+718 tests. STATIC Slice 1: docs/design/294-parameterized-capabilities.md [designed + adversarially reviewed + revised - ticket-gated lease, key-to-token bridge]; impl merged e1f1959 - new src/revl/cap_order.py [single parse point + closed param registry, the (T,P) Cap, covers partial order: path component-prefix, integer ceiling, * top], the param grammar, and the key-to-token attenuation bridge so _check_spawn_attenuation compares real valuations; 469 tests green, tenant_attenuation byte-identical. SLICE 3 = 411 mount enforcement of path=/host= bounds, deferred with 411 Slice 2 [runtime driver].) **Parameterized / resource-bounded capabilities - extends the EXISTING attenuation algebra (item 66) (external proposal #3, corrected).** The capability ALGEBRA the proposal asks for already exists: item 66 enforces child_caps subset-of parent_caps on spawn (attenuate / delegate / 'delegate but cannot create authority'), with docs/capability-attenuation.md + examples/tenant_attenuation.rvl + g4_spawn_widens_capability.rvl. What is genuinely NEW is making capabilities RESOURCE-BOUNDED: `filesystem.read(path="/data/incoming")`, `database.read(table="orders")`, `network.call(host="api.example.com")`, so subsumption becomes bounded (path subset-of path, table/host match) and a spawned worker gets a truly attenuated subset (`spawn Worker with capabilities[filesystem.read("/tmp/job-42"), database.read("orders")]`). LARGE: the capability type gains parameters, and G4 + attenuation (66) + all six backends + the wasm least-authority policy (289) must handle bounded subsumption. Design-first. Unlocks real least-authority for agent security, multi-tenant, embedded, wasm hosts, IAM policy generation, compliance evidence. Cordis's runtime `ctx.capability` (define/provide/check) is the complementary runtime grant layer to revl's static reach (G1/G8); see docs/design/528-capability-reach-alignment.md.
 

    **+ lease-bound (expiring) capabilities (external proposal #6, folded here).** A capability that auto-revokes: `filesystem.write(path="/tmp/job-42", until=job_finished)`, bound to a component generation, a task, a realm, a human approval, a time duration, or a max call count; the runtime revokes when the lease ends. Temporary authority is safer than permanent for agents. Builds on component leases (mcp/leases.py, docs/component-leases.md) + this item's parameterized capabilities.

    **+ declared resource dimensions — the registry is closed against UNDECLARED names (issue #1938, LANDED).** The core registry (`src/revl/cap_order.py:161`) was a closed table of *kinds*, and it was read as a closed table of *names*, so a product capability could not carry a resource dimension the core vocabulary does not name: `emission[mail.send]` could not say *which mailbox* sends, and the kit carried that fact inside the gate spelling, outside what `revl audit` checks. The fix is the extension point the design doc's own "Scoped out" stub deferred: a top-level `capability <token>(<name>: <kind>, ...)` declaration, where `<kind>` is one of the three orders that already exist (`path` / `discrete` / `ceiling`). The kind vocabulary stays CLOSED — a declaration picks an order, it cannot introduce one — so the no-widening argument (three orders, one component-prefix canonicalizer) is unchanged and `domain` stays out of scope (a mail domain reads like a set, and `Cap` valuations are single-valued by construction; its home is the declared-scope surface, item 470). The declaration is per capability, must agree with the declaring extern's own signature (the typo hazard is RELOCATED to each declaration site, not removed), and the kind is NOT in the token's bytes, so an existing token is byte-identical and a stored declared name read back without its declaration fails closed. Ticket `hash` is untouched (bound spellings compose strictly after the hash body); a composition that declares no new dimension is byte-identical on every surface. See docs/design/294-parameterized-capabilities.md §"Declared resource dimensions".
