SLICE 2 SELF-HOST O1/B1 PARITY LANDED 2026-09-06 (merged 91876fd4: decide and pin the self-host O1/B1 ownership-parity gap). **[PRODUCT-VISION - design pass with 243-261] Effect ownership modes - owned / borrowed / shared (external proposal #3).** Explicit ownership on resources; verify: the OWNER performs the final inverse; a BORROWER cannot close an owned resource; a borrowed handle cannot outlive its owner; SHARED handles use reference-counted or lease-based teardown; a realm boundary requires explicit transfer. Extends the G4/G7 resource model from 'there is an inverse' to 'the correct component owns the inverse'. Deep type-system change.

310. ◑ (issue #97) SEAM-METHOD SLICE LANDED 2026-08-31 (designed + adversarially reviewed + revised + implemented, merged 8073b07: cache pure/capability/external as a contextual trailing clause on a seam service method [handoff_cache.rvl unbroken]; the seam gate checks entry liveness pre-execution so a cache capability HIT skips consumption and cannot launder authority [ungranted access refused, revoke kills the entry, a miss is byte-for-byte consume-before-fire]; cache external refused without invalidated_by/ttl; cache on a witnessed/compensate/acquire/deferred reach or a resource-carrying result refused; distribution refuses cache; 35 tests, non-cache byte-identical. SURFACE-H + BODY-MEMO SLICE LANDED 2026-09-02: surface H is a worst-over-reach applicability fold over the PROVIDER closure at load [the same closure the class map folds, so it follows the spawn seam, the transitive closure and the `*` widening; escrow-shaped and widening reaches refuse pre-boot/pre-swap/pre-turn]. It closed a real hole the declaration-only checks could not see: `cache pure` on a non-emission method whose PROVIDER reads its own effect-created state passed every compile check and was memoized at the seam, so an entry outlived the state it described [examples/handoff_cache.rvl is the exact shape]; refused now, with a per-instance `config` constant still admitted. Body-level `cache pure` memo lands in the cordis-py emitter [the seam memoizes a METHOD, a plain `fn` is called inside a body where no seam exists], keyed on a structural whitelist that answers no-key for any unrecognized shape [a miss always recomputes a pure fn, so an unknown shape costs speed, never correctness]; `cache pure` is excluded from the 231a inliner so the declaration is not silently erased. 51 tests, non-cache output byte-identical, no self-host port [the clause is not in the self-host grammar]. INTERIOR-CROSSING extern caching stays refused and is the remaining slice: it needs the crossing-level ledger transaction (an emitted cache-step wrapper, an owner-carried liveness callback, WAL ordering between seam consumption and crossing hit, mid-execution refusal) that does not exist, and it gets its own design pass. Design + review folded: docs/design/310-capability-aware-caching.md.) **[PRODUCT-VISION - design pass with 243-261] Capability-aware caching (external proposal #9).** The language distinguishes `cache pure_fn` / `cache capability_result` / `cache external_effect`, and REQUIRES invalidation/freshness semantics for cached external results (`cache user_profile invalidated_by user.updated ttl 5m`). A cache changes authority + consistency, not just performance. Useful for agent memory, service discovery, registry resolution, multi-realm state.

## The embeddable compiler: revl as a safety kernel (post-v3.0 arc)

_78 closed item(s) of this section (✅ landed or ➖ declined) are archived in [roadmap-archive/13-the-embeddable-compiler-revl-as-a.md](roadmap-archive/13-the-embeddable-compiler-revl-as-a.md)._

These build on two settled results. First, the self-hosted compiler is a
PURE-FUNCTION pipeline (`selfhost/compile.rvl`: `admit(source) -> verdict`,
`compile_to(source, tier) -> output`, no cordis dependency), already compiled
and run natively on py + rust (items 283/284 profiled its rust runtime). Second,
per-tool-call witnessed effects + session commit + six-tier crash recovery
shipped (243/244/245/322). Together they turn revl from "a language with a CLI"
into an admission gate you EMBED in any host runtime. The remaining lever the arc
depends on is the self-host coverage frontier: the function/type surface is
byte-exact today, the component/method-body surface is the `revl`-column `lim`
frontier in docs/conformance.md.

Deferred: rust revl-gate crate, npm/wasm [335], async multi-gate [333/334]. Design: two-layer gate API [pure verdict surface + py Gate session facade], per-tier packaging [py wheel + rust revl-gate crate], fail-closed embedding contract, never-admit-what-reference-refuses security clause; open Q sync-vs-async-multitenant) **Embeddable-gate API + packaging (the foundation of this arc).** Emit
     `selfhost/` as a real consumable library per tier: first a Rust crate and a
     Python package (npm / wasm-component later), exposing a STABLE
     `admit(source) -> verdict` / `compile_to(source, tier) -> output` surface so
     a host program imports the revl admission gate as a native function - no
     subprocess, no cordis dependency, because the compiler is pure `fn`s. Design
     the API boundary (error/verdict shape, versioning) once, tier-agnostic.
     Exit test: a standalone Rust binary depending ONLY on the published crate
     calls `admit` on a component and returns the same verdict as `revl compile`,
     and `compile_to` is byte-identical to the reference on the covered corpus.
     Blocks 333-338. `selfhost/` + a new crate/package build. Depends on the
     self-host coverage frontier above.

335. ◑ (issue #101) SLICES 0-2 LANDED 2026-09-02 (#101; `tools/build_gate_wasm.py` generates the committed `crates/revl-gate-wasm`, gated by `tests/test_gate_wasm_drift.py` + `tests/test_gate_wasm_vector.py`; design docs/design/335-wasm-edge-gate.md). The design's go/no-go spike RESOLVED YES: cordis-rs 0.3 and the whole generated `revl-gate` crate cross-compile clean to wasm32, so the rust lane carries the gate to the edge with no vendoring and no upstream fork. Shipped: a `revl:gate@1.0.0` WASI-P2 component exporting Cut A as `admit` / `admit-json` / `gate-version`, plus `admit-artifact` present and failing closed. MEASURED: 317,203 bytes, an EMPTY import section, 1,525,847 wasmtime fuel for one `admit` over a one-line program, ~85s cold build - against the playground's 1.4 MB wheel plus a Pyodide runtime, which is the cost baseline this item exists to beat. Exit test met: the wasm `admit` under wasmtime returns the reference verdict, code AND message byte-identical, on all 81 programs of the self-host corpus (`tests/test_selfhost_lower.py`'s own, imported not copied), with item 332's asymmetry intact - the packaged gate has no admitting arm, so no input can false-admit. TWO CORRECTIONS TO THE DESIGN, both measured, neither a scope change: (a) the target is wasm32-unknown-unknown and NOT wasm32-wasip2, because on wasip2 rust's std links the WASI world into every artifact and the import section is then not empty, and the empty-import property is the load-bearing one (it is what makes a verdict provably a function of its arguments); (b) the .wasm is NOT committed under a byte-difference drift gate, because a rust binary is byte-reproducible only against one exact toolchain - what is committed and drift-gated is the crate SOURCE plus an exactly-pinned wit-bindgen, and the conformance vector is computed live from the reference in-process, which is 'at the build sha' by construction and cannot go stale. Also measured: on every wasm target rust's panic strategy is `abort`, so the crate's catch_unwind fail-closed path does not catch and a native gate panic TRAPS instead of returning outside-frontier; a trap is neither a verdict nor an admission, and the vector requires zero of them. REMAINING: slice 3 (`admit-artifact`, the 289 chain) is blocked on the chain's MIDDLE set - the declared caps are the G8 boundary projection, which the reference derives with a whole-IR reachability walk (`revl.__main__._boundary`) that has no native port, and a guessed declared set would be exactly the wave-through this arc exists to prevent; slice 4 (jco transpile + a browser/serverless harness showing the double enforcement). Cut C still deferred [Map has no first-party wasm representation]). **Edge / browser / serverless admission (the wasm gate).** Compile the
     admission gate to wasm so it runs client-side: a web playground / IDE that
     type-checks + admits revl with no server round-trip, and an edge-worker gate
     that admits agent code sub-millisecond with no cold-start Python. Exit test:
     the wasm-compiled `admit` runs in a browser / wasmtime host and returns the
     reference verdict on the corpus. Depends on 332 + native-run coverage on the
     wasm tier (frontier). The committed playground wheel (site/vendor) is the
     first step in this direction.

Lifts the four load-bearing properties of the landed _repoint_decision/_apply_repoint + swap_admission seam into one seam invariant required at every tier boundary: the wire carries a component+backend SELECTOR (never the manifest, never an IR to re-link), the receiver derives both gate inputs from independently-held state and re-compiles from its own source, fail-closed with no admissible reference, no-blip on refusal, full re-admission not a re-link.      embed the gate (332 per tier), a component crossing a placement seam is
     RE-ADMITTED by the receiving tier's embedded compiler before it is accepted,
     so a heterogeneous fleet self-verifies rather than trusting the wire. Exit

Frontier-gated: `cargo add revl-gate` still needs the publish step (the 332 stage-3 crate itself HAS landed and a path/vendored dependency on it works today; layer-1 on the self-host frontier; CORRECTED 2026-09-14: this note read "no admission arm, no admit_into, no layer 2" and the first two are no longer true. #860 (03a25878) landed the manifest-aware `admit_into` and #987 landed an admission arm as `issue_admission`/`issue_admission_into`, which return a separate `Admission` type gated behind `admit == NoObjection` and confined to `ADMISSION_SURFACE_ID`. The crate's "issues no admissions" claim survives, but strictly of the VERDICT surface: `admit` and `admit_into` return a `Verdict` whose three arms contain no admitting arm and whose `to_json` writes "admitted": false on every one, which is why `issue_admission` returns a different type rather than a fourth arm. The admission arm is SMALL and bounded, not the type layer: `ADMITTED_LAYER` (`crates/revl-gate/src/lib.rs:295`) is "interface declarations only: service method signatures and scalar type aliases, over a closed scalar type vocabulary; no term the reference type layer decides", so a `fn` body is still withheld and issue #346's exit test is still unmet (UPDATED 2026-09-29: rust now resolves `cache_layer`'s `requires` against a held manifest, PR 1396, and admits an interface-only candidate into it, PR 1429, but cannot yet issue an admission for a component with a provide-method body, design 457 T6; PR 1538 in review), carried by docs/design/457-selfhost-type-layer.md and items 417/391/146. **CORRECTED AGAIN 2026-09-15:** the clause that stood here, "#990 (cbe4deef) separately rebuilt `revl_gate::session` from a reserved empty module into a real Session state machine. Layer 2 is still absent", was stale in its own second half, in the same sentence that cites the PR which made it stale. `crates/revl-gate/src/session.rs:1` reads "Layer 2, the session surface — item 334 slices 1-2", `lib.rs:254` carries `pub mod session;`, and all eight of the design's layer-2 operations ship (`load`, `admit`, `admit_into`, `propose`, `call`, `commit`, `abort`, `unload`) over a real witnessed-effect runtime: an `Externs` registry enforcing the item-243 pair rules at declaration time, LIFO inverse replay so `residue_free` is MEASURED rather than reported, and deferrals discharged at commit. `tests/test_gate_crate_drift.py::test_the_crate_is_admit_only_and_says_so` asserts exactly that surface. So layer 2 is PARTIAL on rust, not absent. What is missing is the ACCEPT half of `propose` (compile, activate, run the health gate, hot-swap generation N+1 — blocked on `compile_to`, item 332 stage 4), the item-322 WAL and `recover`, and the item-246 approver callback. `session.rs` never sets `admitted: true`, pinned by the same test, so the fail-closed direction holds) and the npm publish (335 slice 4 LANDED 2026-09-02: the transpiled-to-JS wasm gate and its browser/worker/Node consumer are in-tree and run against a path dependency today, so only the registry publish remains). Adversarial review caught the safety-kernel overclaim [a consumer reads admit-means-safe-to-run and admit-is-one-fact-across-the-fleet, neither of which a compile-time gate honors]; fixed by the ASYMMETRIC contract, a refusal is authoritative and fail-closed while an admission is a compile-time judgment scoped to gate_version().frontier (not runtime confinement), the runtime half is a separate py-only layer-2 dependency, and frontier is promoted to a first-class contract field. **revl as a dependency for other agent infrastructure (the ecosystem
     step).** Publish the crate/package (332) so other MCP servers, CI systems,
     and agent frameworks pull in the admission gate as a LIBRARY rather than
     orchestrating the CLI: revl as the safety kernel for agent-generated code
     that other tools build on. Downstream of 332's stable API; exit is a real
     external consumer example (a third-party tool that `cargo add revl` / imports
     the package and admits code). Relates to truc (the component manager) + the
     component registry (item 293).

350. ◑ (issue #105) REVL SIDE LANDED (`feat/350-environment-binding`): the exit surface exists — `boot component X { config { … } }` IS the environment contract (one per composition, refused at link if two; not spawnable), `revl run --env FILE` is its ONE door (a `--config` table naming the boot component is refused, an undeclared `--env` key is refused rather than dropped, a missing required field refuses the boot before any runtime is imported), the MCP `revl_load` seam enforces the same value-level rules (it has one config channel, so a boot entry there IS the env injection: undeclared key / wrong type / out-of-bound all refused before the runtime is touched, closing what would otherwise be an inject-past-the-bounds door), and the contract is published by `revl audit` (a valueless name/type/required/secret/bound table) with an `env:<name>:<bound>` drift crossing so a NEW field or a DROPPED bound reads as a widening. The authority half beyond the item's own exit: a contract field may declare an admission bound (`under "<prefix>"` for a path, `in [...]` for an enumerated choice), checked against the injected value, because an environment value adds no capability but DOES spell a resource scope (a data dir is the path reached, a provider identity is the destination posted to) — so an unbounded one widens the real reach while the declared capability set and the audit stay unchanged. A bound outside a boot component is refused: unenforced grammar would be the same fail-open. Additive and conditional throughout (a composition with no boot component is byte-identical through parse, IR, manifest, audit and every tier); the self-host gate is ported (`at_boot`/`boot_names` in `selfhost/lower.rvl`, the new BOOT tag in the differential oracle) so the native rust gate does not admit what the reference refuses. TWO EDGES STATED, NOT CLOSED, both in docs/environment-binding.md: (a) a host can still route an environment-read value through a NON-boot component's `--config` — the composition-bootstrap floor, since the host compiles and plugs; the contract removes the silence, not the trust; (b) `under` is LEXICAL (the preflight must not touch the filesystem), so a symlink inside the prefix still points wherever it points — that needs a runtime enforcer, item 411's lane. REMAINING: harness-side adoption (revl-harness's own roadmap) — `_config()`'s data-dir/model/secret-dir derivation moving behind a `boot` component. docs/environment-binding.md + syntax-2.0 §6.2 + examples/environment_contract.rvl; tests/test_environment_binding_350.py. **GAP (harness-in-revl, found by the revl-harness dogfood goal): environment binding — tokens, data-dir paths, and model choice are read before the composition exists.** The host must know the port, the auth token, the data dir, and which model provider to boot before it can compile the composition; these flow in through component `config {}` blocks and env injection at plug time (the sanctioned seam). The composition itself now lives in revl (manifest.rvl + composition.rvl ship/revert), but "the thing that decides the port" is host. Exit: a revl `boot` component that declares the environment contract (what the host must inject), so the boundary is explicit and audited instead of implicit py. LOW effort, MED harness benefit. <!-- auto-revl-harness:environment-binding-is-host -->

391. (issue #106) **🚧 PARTIAL (3 slices landed, the gap is now MEASURED not estimated; latest 2026-09-06 merged a396b310: native @rs bodies for 4 host-formatting helpers in `selfhost/emit_rust.rvl`) — SELF-HOST PARITY CATCH-UP; PO DECISION: the self-host is a FULL CURRENT-LANGUAGE compiler for v3, not a subset proof.** The differential byte-agreement oracles (tests/test_selfhost_{lexer,parser,checker,lower,emit_*}.py) prevent SILENT divergence on the corpus but do NOT force new features in — a feature the corpus does not exercise leaves the oracle green while the self-host lacks it. **THE MEASUREMENT (2026-09-02, on `fabd0be5`), which replaces this item's earlier estimate.** Two numbers, both produced by running the reference and the self-host over the SAME inputs rather than by reading code: (a) *gate parity* — over all 122 `examples/rejections/*.rvl` fixtures, `selfhost/lower.rvl`'s `admit_src` agreed with the reference on tag AND message for **17**; it FALSE-ADMITTED **87** (it raised no objection at all to a program the reference refuses) and gave a different message on 18. (b) *lexer parity* — over all 862 reference-lexable `.rvl` files in the tree (not the 8-file oracle corpus), `selfhost/lexer.rvl` diverged on **9**, and 6 of those 9 were SELF-HOST SOURCES: the self-host lexer could not lex `selfhost/{lower,emit_go,emit_java,emit_py,emit_rust,emit_wasm}.rvl`. This item's original claim ("does not lex 0x/<</&") was already stale — hex/bin/oct, `_` grouping, single quotes, `& | ^ ~ << >>` and the `break`/`continue` keywords all lex correctly. The real lexer defect was elsewhere and invisible to the corpus: the `"` spelling ran an escape-BLIND scanner (item 382 gave the `'` spelling the escapes and left `"` behind), and the raw-brace balancers counted braces inside strings — which is exactly why a self-host source full of `\"`-built JSON could not be lexed by the self-host. **THE TYPE LAYER IS A DISTINCT, LARGE SUB-GOAL, NOT ONE MORE PORT SLICE (item 417).** Of the 87 false-admits measured above, ~40 are T1/T2 type errors and there is NO type layer in the self-host at all: `admit_src` decides the composition/guarantee layer (G1..G4, A1, PRELUDE, ROUTE, SPAWN, HANDOFF, BAD) and nothing else. That sub-goal gets its own slices (type environment, expression typing, return-path totality, generic instantiation, Int32 width rules, match exhaustiveness, the stdlib method surface), its own corpus family, and it must NOT be folded into the per-feature port list below. **LANDED SO FAR** (`fix/391-selfhost-parity`): the foreign-construct redirect table (384) — all 21 `examples/rejections/foreign_*.rvl` fixtures now refuse with the reference's message byte-for-byte, under the new `FOREIGN` tag, taking the false-admit count 87 → 67; and the lexer's string-escape + string-aware brace balancing, taking lexer parity 853/862 → 861/862 (the self-host can now lex every self-host source). **STILL OPEN**, measured: 67 false-admits and 17 message divergences over the rejection corpus, of which the largest families are the type layer (~40, see 417), the `lifecycle test` block surface (6, unparsed), extern `undo`/`compensate` slot validation (5), module-fn scope resolution and `let`-reassignment (5), the stdlib method surface (3), `use`-module resolution (3), match exhaustiveness, `verified` totality, and the Int-literal 64-bit range check (which currently CRASHES the gate with an OverflowError rather than refusing). THE PAYOFF (why this is more than parity-for-its-own-sake): once ported, the self-host's OWN SOURCE gets to USE the new features — bitwise in the lexer hot path, .map/.filter/break to simplify the compiler code, single-quote/hex literals, canonical json, the total field read — so it is real dogfooding that makes the self-host source cleaner and faster, and every new feature is exercised by the compiler that most stresses it. PORT each remaining feature into selfhost/{lexer,parser,checker,lower,emit_*}.rvl and EXTEND the corpus: .map/.filter/.reduce + comprehension redirect (383); Value dot-accessors (189); total field read + Any-shape (367/H24); json_try_parse (362) + canonical json_stringify (385); string-interp + return-type-hole diagnostics (380); extern config{} coeffect (378); emission[caps] (343) + emission(confined) reach (373); async effect composition (131); sync/async arrow poly (342); async match-block arm (361); admit crossing (330); multi-error reporting (386); the per-backend host-body trivia tables (`_HOST_TRIVIA`: `'` char/rune and string literals, `/* */` and wasm `(; ;)` block comments, Python triple quotes — only the `"` span is ported). Slice by stage where a feature is stage-local, vertically (all stages) where it is not. Multi-wave. STANDING PROCESS: every future reference-side language feature includes a 'does this need a self-host port?' step and files the port slice if yes. NOTE for the next slice: a new verdict tag (this pass added `FOREIGN`) is invisible to the oracle until `tests/test_selfhost_lower.py`'s `_classify` learns the family — that classifier work belongs to item 429, so a port that introduces a tag pins itself with in-file `test` blocks and says so. selfhost/*.rvl + tests/test_selfhost_*.py (extend corpus). Relates to 417 (the type layer), 429 (corpus coverage), 146 (full self-host). **EXIT (added 2026-09-18, because this item had none and therefore could not close).** `tools/gate_reference_census.py` reports **zero documents in every `false-admit` bucket** — the self-host gate never admits a program the reference refuses — and zero in `false-reject` and `msg-mismatch`/`tag-mismatch`, so the two implementations agree on the verdict, its guarantee tag and its sentence. `tools/checker_reference_census.py`'s `msg-mismatch/parse` is likewise zero. Measured on 2026-09-18 over 812 programs: `false-admit/T1` 14, `TYPE` 3, `G1` 2, `G4` 2 (21 total, all type-layer or name-resolution and tracked per slice under issue #346), plus `false-reject/G1` 5. The fail-open direction is what this item is FOR, so a `false-admit` bucket at zero is the load-bearing half; `false-reject` and the mismatch buckets are the byte-agreement obligation the self-host design rests on and close with it. **2026-09-29:** the gate half of this exit is met (PR 1396, merged `68c9c3545`: 973 programs, no false-admit, false-reject or mismatch bucket on either engine); the checker half is not, with `msg-mismatch/parse` at 14 in `tools/checker_reference_census.py` (7 of them `selfhost/*.rvl` files the census feeds without `modules=`) and `msg-mismatch/semantic` at 1. Emitter parity is PR 1530, PR 1521 and PR 1517 in review for py (a stack, 13 byte divergences to 0) and PR 1551 in review for ts.

411. ◑ (issue #107) SLICE 1 LANDED 2026-08-31 (STATIC sandbox placement: [processes.X.sandbox] + [tiers]-form [sandbox] surface, expand_tiers split into sandbox_<component>, both-forms refusal, sandbox-table validation, the [sandbox.needs] fail-closed gate for a need outside the fs/net vocabulary, per-process config/spec narrowing so secrets do not enter the boundary, boot-summary + `revl audit --placement` envelope view, swap refusal; merged cf838b6, 28 tests. Additive + links identically with/without the assignment. SLICE 2 remaining = the RUNTIME driver: container/microVM/wasm-cell launch + health + teardown, the in-sandbox boot canary, the per-rung transport variant, and the conductor-served approval-across-boundary channel; needs Docker/wasm at runtime.) SLICE 2 MIXED-ARCH LANDED 2026-09-06 (merged 3f58086e: the mixed-arch `platform` key for the container rung; the container/microVM/wasm-cell runtime driver stays remaining). DESIGN DONE + adversarial review folded 2026-08-31 (docs/design/411-sandbox-placement.md: sandbox as a 4th placement column over the 363 seam, plan-time cap gate + trusted-runtime enforcer, wasm-cell/container/microvm ladder, mixed-arch, makes 329/249 enforced; open Q = cap-name<->OS-resource vocabulary bridge) **Capability-enforced sandbox placement — a component runs in its own isolation boundary (wasm cell / container / microVM) with a declared capability envelope enforced at RUNTIME, still composed with unsandboxed components over the 363 seam.** Extends item-363 per-component placement from "which tier" to "which isolation + capability envelope": a component declares no-disk/no-net (or an allowlist) and runs in `docker run --network=none --read-only` / a microVM / a wasm cell, communicating with the rest of the composition over the EXISTING 363 proxy/stub seam (value-copy, G8 allowlist, seam deadlines, peer-death-as-withdrawal = container-death) across the isolation boundary. The sandbox is the RUNTIME ENFORCEMENT of the statically-declared capability envelope (`emission[caps]` / attenuation / item 289 host-imports-subset-of-caps): a component whose declared caps EXCEED the sandbox grant is refused at plan time (like 363's tier-capability gate). SECURITY PAYOFF: makes item 329 (untrusted author) + item 249 (taint) ENFORCED not just checked — an opaque/malicious host body confined to `--network=none` physically cannot exfiltrate whatever G8 cannot see inside it. USE CASE: mixed-arch compositions — a foreign-arch binary wrapped as a revl component runs in a container of that arch (multi-arch image / qemu) and talks to the host composition over the arch-agnostic (serialized-value) seam. TRUST BOUNDARY: revl DECLARES + REQUESTS the confinement and refuses caps that exceed the grant, but the container runtime is a trusted enforcer (revl cannot verify Docker actually applied --network=none), the way the OS is today. Cross-sandbox witnessed rollback + cross-tier determinism scoped out (like 363). The conductor gains a sandbox-runtime driver (launch/health/teardown). Isolation ladder: wasm cell (in-process, weakest) -> container -> microVM (strongest), one capability contract, different enforcement strength. Converges 363 (placement mechanism) + the capability model (289/attenuation/emission[caps]) + 329/249 (the payoff) + 337 (polyglot mesh) + 335 (wasm cell). DESIGN-FIRST.

416. (private security advisories, see SECURITY.md) **SECURITY AUDIT RESIDUE (2026-09-02 three-audit campaign) — the proven-but-unfixed tail plus the unattempted surfaces.** Three adversarial audits (admission gate, teardown/streams, confidentiality) found 13 findings with EXECUTED reproducers; 11 got fixes (R2 decoy-provision allowlist bypass, 337 seam unjudged address, 334 attacker-controlled template exclusion, taint activation-body hole, `Secret[T]` receiver laundering, subscription undo-shape, G5 site-undo emission, Phase-1 bracket-fault, recovery double-apply, secret in WAL/MCP, secret config echo). THIS item tracks what was NOT fixed, so it is not lost:
    (a) **PROVEN, unfixed.** ts/go/java/rust silently EMIT a `Stream.source()`-only program against a host that does not exist in their runtimes, instead of the honest `EmitError` wasm gives (the `subscribe` IR kind IS refused on all five; only `Stream.source` leaks through). The failure surfaces as the tier's own build error. Low blast radius, but a silent-emit where the design promises a refusal.
    (b) **PROVEN, unfixed.** A parked `next` busy-spins: `backends/python/runtime.py:3304-3317` `while True: ... await asyncio.sleep(0)` consumed all 200 of 200 event-loop turns in a probe. A deliberate cooperative poll (mirrors `JobHandle._drive`) and cancellation still reaches it, so correctness is intact, but N idle consumers make loop overhead linear in N for zero work.
    (c) **HYPOTHESES, unexecuted, same class as the fixed secret-externalization leaks.** `src/revl/mcp/approval.py:350-357` renders actual runtime string argument values into the capability spelling and `:432-434` copies them into the approval body, which `replay.py:1801` writes to the WAL and `server.py:503` returns over MCP; and `backends/python/runtime.py:243-246` `ResponseValidationError` embeds `{value!r}` of the raw model response and retains `value=value`, so any host logging the exception logs the raw completion.
    (d) **OPERATIONAL FINDINGS.** `revl_reset_run_trace_state`'s docstring says "called at run start" but it is called ONLY by tests, so the item-121 digest nonce and the fiber-local model-hop registers are never reset by `run.py`, including across `--watch` generations. Related: the 121 digest is effectively DEAD in the shipped driver (`run.py:1086` passes `taint_engaged=False` unconditionally), so its suppression logic is never exercised in production; decide whether to wire the compile-to-runtime taint-origin channel or to state the surface as inert.
    (e) **BOUNDARY-TYPE BREAK.** `src/revl/gate.py:672-677` `Gate.call` is the one facade method with no `except _session_error()` wrapper, so it leaks a raw `SessionError` where its siblings raise `GateError`. Fails closed; a contract break, not an exploit.
    (f) **UNATTEMPTED SURFACES, on record as unexamined rather than cleared:** codegen injection into emitted Python (the auditor read `repr()` usage but did not attack it), capability widening / attenuated-capability re-widening (item 294), and secret flow across the Temporal emission target (253), OpenAPI-imported network effects (254), and non-py bridge/seam crossings.
    Exit: (a)+(b)+(e) fixed or consciously accepted with a stated reason; (c) executed and fixed or refuted; (d) decided; (f) audited. Prioritize (c), it is the same externalization class as two already-confirmed HIGH leaks.
    **TRIAGE + RESOLUTION 2026-09-02 (`fix/416-audit-residue`). Each tail item re-checked against current main before any code changed:**
    (a) ✅ **FIXED (and the finding was half stale).** go and rust are NOT silent-emits any more: item 130 Slice 3 gave both a real `Stream`/`Subscription` runtime, so they CORRECTLY emit a stream program. Only ts and java were the silent emit, and only for the `Stream.source()`-only shape (a `subscribe` was already refused by name on both). ts rendered `host.Stream.source()` against a `runtime.ts` that has no `Stream`, java rendered `Stream src = Stream.source()` naming a type `_emit_host_stubs` never declares, so the failure surfaced as the CONSUMER's build error. Fixed by refusing the whole `Stream` host root at the `host`-node sink on both tiers (`backends/typescript/emit.py` `_refuse_missing_host_root`, `backends/java/emit.py` same), message shaped like wasm's and naming py/go/rust as the tiers that carry it. Reproducer + regression: `tests/test_stream_reactive.py::test_source_only_program_still_emits_on_the_lowered_tiers` (go/rust/java must keep emitting). CORRECTED 2026-09-15: the ts/java refusal this letter landed has since been SUPERSEDED by item 130. Both tiers carry a real `Stream` runtime now, `_UNIMPLEMENTED_HOST_ROOTS` is empty on ts, and the refusal test that pinned this fix (`::test_source_only_program_is_refused_not_silently_emitted`) was deleted with it. wasm is the one tier still refusing the shape, and it refuses for a different reason (no async at all).
    (b) ✅ **FIXED.** Reproduced first: 1 owner-bearing parked consumer took 200 event-loop re-entries in 200 driver turns, and it scaled exactly linearly (10 -> 2000, 50 -> 10000). The three conditions a parked `next` owns (a delivered item, a provider terminal, the cancel token) now WAKE the consumer directly through an `asyncio.Event`; only owner-withdrawal, which flips synchronously inside cordis with no callback out, still needs polling, and that is now ONE shared `_stream_withdrawal_sweep` re-checking each DISTINCT owning context once per turn rather than one poll per consumer. After: 1 re-entry per consumer regardless of N, and per-turn work is O(distinct owners) (200 turns cost ~200 checks whether 1 or 50 consumers share the owner). Ladder order and cancellation-first semantics unchanged; `tests/test_stream_reactive.py` + `backends/python/tests/` green. `backends/python/runtime.py`.
    (c) ✅ **ALREADY CLOSED by later work, refiled where residue lives.** Both hypotheses were executed and fixed in `0dd4c040` (item 416c, "Redact resource-scoped Secret[T] capability values and model-response faults"): `bind_resource_scope` binds a `Secret[T]` resource dimension to the redacted placeholder before it reaches the ticket/WAL/MCP, and `ResponseValidationError` now scrubs both its message and `.value` through `confidential.redact_value`. The RESIDUAL of the same class (a value passed through a PLAIN `Str` param named `path`/`host`/`table`, and the caller-vs-declaring-extern arg-index confusion) is already tracked with tighter scope under 427 F2/F3/F4 and 421 F3, not here. Nothing left for 416(c).
    (d) ✅ **FIXED (reset) + DECIDED (digest).** Reset: `run._Driver._emit_module` now calls `revl_reset_run_trace_state()` at every generation boundary (gen 1 for a plain run, gen N+1 for each `--watch` reload), BEFORE the emit, so a completion observed in gen N and never consumed can no longer be mis-attributed to the first crossing of gen N+1, and each generation gets its own digest salt. Regression: `tests/test_trace_121.py::test_a_new_generation_resets_the_run_trace_state` (driven through `_emit_module`, so removing the wiring reds it) + `::test_the_reset_is_wired_into_the_generation_boundary` + `::test_each_generation_gets_its_own_digest_salt`. DECISION on the dead digest: the shipped driver runs no taint analysis and passes `taint_engaged=False` unconditionally, so `revl_prompt_digest` always returns `None` (fail-closed, `runtime.py:500`): the surface is INERT BY DESIGN and safe (a suppressed digest never leaks a prompt). Wiring the compile-to-runtime taint-origin channel so the digest becomes LIVE is a real feature, not a residue fix, and is deferred; filed as its own item (see below).
    (e) ✅ **FIXED.** Reproduced: `Gate(...).call("no_such_key", ...)` leaked a raw `revl.mcp.session.SessionError` across the library edge. Both `Gate.call` and `Handle.call` (the two `call` methods, current `gate.py:~700` and `:~323`) now wrap in `except _session_error() -> GateError`, matching every sibling facade method. `GateRefused` is a `GateError` subclass and `ApprovalRequired` is resolved inside the retry loop, so only a genuine session refusal reaches the new arm and it still fails closed; pure boundary-type fix. `src/revl/gate.py`.
    (f) ◑ **OpenAPI surface AUDITED, one HIGH found and FIXED; the rest was substantially covered by 421.** Attacked `revl import openapi` as a codegen surface fed by an untrusted document (the ordinary case is projecting a vendor's PUBLISHED spec). **HIGH, codegen injection / supply-chain RCE, FIXED here.** `info.title`, `info.version`, `openapi`, the source filename and resolved server URLs were interpolated verbatim into `//` comments and `@<backend> { ... }` stub bodies of the generated `.rvl`. A `//` comment runs to the next newline (`lexer.py:351`), so a newline in ANY of those values ended the comment and dropped the rest onto its own line of COMPILED source. Executed end to end: a document with `info.title = "W\nextern pure fn INJECTED() -> Int = @py { ... }\ncomponent ZZZ { }\n// "` (and the same through `info.version`, `openapi`, `servers[].url`) produced a generated `.rvl` that COMPILED with a top-level `component ZZZ` and an `@py` extern; the injected `@py` body runs arbitrary host code on the importing developer's first `revl run` (confirmed: a `pathlib.Path(...).write_text(...)` payload wrote its marker file). Fixed with `_comment_safe` (`import_openapi.py`), applied at every comment sink (header lines, `_host_comment`, notes), collapsing CR/LF/U+2028/U+2029/U+0085 to a space so a document string can no longer terminate a comment; narrows comment text only, leaves the typed boundary untouched. Reproducer + regression: `tests/test_import_openapi_injection_416f.py` (5 vectors executed, benign doc unchanged). NEGATIVE results kept: op `summary`/`description` were already safe (`operation()` takes `str(...).strip().splitlines()[0][:88]`); a path-template key with a newline is refused by path validation; the server URL as a CAPABILITY token was already fixed (421 F4) and this fix is independent of it. The OTHER 416f surfaces are already covered elsewhere and not re-opened: capability widening / attenuated re-widening is 294 + 421 F1/F2 (CRITICAL/HIGH, fixing on their own branch); Temporal secret flow (253) was cleared by 421 (clean by exhaustion over emit_temporal.py, minus the bounded 421 F7 error-text leak tracked there); non-py bridge transport was cleared by 421 (mTLS, fail-closed marshal). Not attacked, still genuinely open surface: codegen injection into emitted PYTHON via `repr()` (416f's first clause) was not re-examined here.
    **EXIT: item CLOSED except one deferred feature.** (a)(b)(e) fixed, (c) closed by 0dd4c040 and refiled under 427, (d) fixed + decided, (f) OpenAPI audited with a HIGH fixed. The one thing not done is a residue fix: wiring live compile-to-runtime taint so the 121 digest stops being inert, filed as item 444 below (this line said 432, which is the wasm codegen performance audit; corrected 2026-09-02 during the finding-marker reconciliation).

417. (issue #108) **🚧 PARTIAL — FIRST EXIT STEP TAKEN on `agent/391-selfhost-parity` (PR #270, merged `b5320846`; the branch was previously misnamed here as `fix/391-selfhost-parity`, which never existed) (2026-09-02): item 391 is restated and the gap is MEASURED; the 335-338 gating LANDED (PR #494, merged `2026-09-06`, `tests/test_gate_decision_boundary_417.py`), so the ONLY remaining exit piece is the `tests/test_selfhost_lower.py` corpus family, which item 429 (issue #125) owns and delivers (its T0 slice, PR #521, is open and sequenced ahead of T1). This issue has no remaining independently-actionable slice; close it and track the residual under #125.** **SELF-HOST IS A GUARANTEE GATE, NOT AN ADMISSION GATE — item 391 was understated, and 335/336/337/338 must not lean on the crate until this is stated.** (Found 2026-09-02 while building the item-332 stage-3 `revl-gate` crate, by MEASURING the frontier rather than assuming it.) `selfhost/lower.rvl`'s `admit_src` decides the composition/guarantee layer (G1..G4, A1, PRELUDE, BAD) and runs **no type layer at all**. The reference refuses every one of these; the self-host gate objects to none: `fn f() -> Int { return "s" }`, `fn f() -> Int { return undefined_name }`, `fn f() -> { }`, `fn f() -> Int { }`, `component C provides s: S { }`. So the gap between self-host and reference is not "a few unported constructs" (391's original framing) but a whole missing layer. The crate handled this correctly by issuing NO admission at all (`Refused` | `NoObjection` | `OutsideFrontier`, `"admitted": false` on every arm) rather than shipping a false-admit factory, but the ROADMAP still read as though parity is a porting exercise. What kept it invisible: `tests/test_selfhost_lower.py`'s corpus discipline explicitly scopes a T1/parse refusal as "out of slice", so the differential oracles stay green while the type layer is absent (the same shape as 391's own "a feature the corpus does not exercise leaves the oracle green"). **WHAT THE FIRST EXIT STEP DID.** The complaint was that no exit step had been taken and 391's text never named the type layer. It now does, backed by a count rather than an adjective: over all 122 `examples/rejections/*.rvl` fixtures the self-host gate agreed with the reference on 17 and FALSE-ADMITTED 87, of which ~40 are the type layer; 391 now names the type layer as a distinct sub-goal with its own slices and its own corpus family, lists the other measured families, and separates them from the per-feature port list. The same pass closed 21 of the 87 false-admits (the item-384 foreign-construct redirects, message-identical to the reference) and fixed the lexer defect that stopped the self-host lexing 6 of its own 11 sources — so "no exit step taken" is answered with a landed diff, not a restatement. **WHAT REMAINS OF THIS ITEM'S EXIT.** (1) a corpus family in `tests/test_selfhost_lower.py` that FAILS while the self-host lacks a type layer, deliberately left to item 429, which owns the oracle/corpus, so two agents do not edit the same generator (this is the residual, in flight as its T0 slice PR #521 and NOT this issue's to implement); (2) **LANDED (PR #494, merged `2026-09-06`, `8bc2c95` green):** gating 335 / 336-slice-2 / 337-non-py / 338-cargo on an explicit statement of what the crate does and does not decide, done in `tests/test_gate_decision_boundary_417.py`, which pins the four dependents to the crate's `COVERED_LAYER` sentence and `issues_admissions: false`, and states in its own header that it touches neither item 429's oracle nor its generator. With (2) landed and (1) owned by 429/#125, this issue can close. Relates to 332 (the crate), 391 (parity), 429 (corpus coverage), 146 (full self-host).

419. ◑ (issue #109) **FLAGGED-IN-PASSING FINDINGS from the 2026-09-02 parallel wave — recorded so they are not lost in agent reports.** Each was found by an agent working on something else, confirmed, and deliberately left out of that agent's scope. **SWEPT 2026-09-02 (`fix/419-flagged-in-passing`), one verdict per letter, no letter skipped: (a) fixed and generalized, (b) reproduced and fixed, (c) reproduced and deliberately NOT fixed (moved into item 186, pinned), (d) decided (item 73(d): FREEZE), (e) half already closed / the other half fixed, (f) not a defect, folded into item 186.** Every letter now carries a verdict and none is left open: (c), the last one, closed on 2026-09-07 under 186 (16d1a225, #525). This item still does not tick until 186 closes, for the reason the correction at the end of this entry gives. **RE-VERIFIED 2026-09-02 on `89ea9bd6`, letter by letter, by a pass that started from the source rather than from this entry: every verdict above still holds and nothing here needs redoing.** (a) `tests/test_backend_suite_roots_combine.py` plus `backends/python/tests/test_bare_import_pin.py` are green (5 passed), and `pytest backends/go backends/wasm --collect-only` collects 213 tests where the shared basename used to kill collection outright. (b) `tests/test_deploy_118.py` is green (71 passed, 1 skipped), the three mediated-crossing regressions among them, and `reached_emissions` does read `query.Composition` (`src/revl/deploy.py:1509`). (c) was STILL REAL on that sha and was accurately described — `admit_src` was then a first-refusal `Str`-returning function (`selfhost/lower.rvl:3323`) whose every arm was an early `return` with no line on the verdict, so a collecting sink was a rewrite of its shape and of what `tools/build_gate_crate.py` digests. **It has since been done: CLOSED 2026-09-07 (16d1a225, #525) under 186**, which made exactly that rewrite; the pin was deleted with it. (d) `tests/test_goldens.py::test_the_frontend_still_mints_ir_version_1` passes and item 73(d) carries the FREEZE decision. (e) `tests/test_stream_reactive.py` is green (61 passed) and a `Stream.*` host builtin is refused by name on both tiers (`backends/typescript/emit.py:2103`, `backends/java/emit.py:129`). (f) the shape note is in 186's text. Ruff, `tools/check_roadmap_markers.py --check-contradiction` and `tools/build_gate_crate.py --check` are all clean on the same sha. **One correction to the sentence above:** (c) is the only 419 LETTER left open, but (e) left a remainder this item never claimed, the then-missing ts `Stream`/`Subscription` runtime primitive, which was item 130's own slice and landed in #679/#747 rather than a 419 letter, so closing 186 is what ticks this item.
    (a) ✅ **FIXED, and the decision generalized.** *Original:* running `pytest backends/wasm/ backends/python/tests/` in ONE process yielded 91 failed / 155 passed while each root alone was green, because both ship a same-named `emit.py` (and this backend a `runtime.py`) and a bare `import emit` bound whichever directory won the `sys.path` race. **The honest fix is to make the roots CO-IMPORTABLE, not to keep them apart with a documented guard.** Separation is what hid this for months (`backend-python` and `backend-wasm` never combined them), it is unenforceable against the next gate author who writes the obvious command, and a suite that cannot be combined is a suite whose green is conditional on how it was invoked. *That fix already landed:* `backends/python/tests/conftest.py`'s `_load_and_pin` loads each sibling module by PATH under a private name and then UNCONDITIONALLY re-binds the bare name, so it is order-independent; `test_bare_import_pin.py` simulates the poisoning; and CI runs both roots in one process. Verified on this branch: the combined run is 276 passed in BOTH orders. *What this sweep added, because the decision was true of one PAIR and not of the roots:* `backends/go/` and `backends/wasm/` each shipped a `test_router_exec.py`, and with no `__init__.py` anywhere under `backends/` pytest derives a module name from the BASENAME, so `pytest backends/` (the obvious command) died at collection with `import file mismatch` before running a single test. Renamed to `test_router_exec_go.py` / `test_router_exec_wasm.py` (docs updated); `pytest backends/`, all six roots in ONE process, is now green end to end (576 passed, 26 skipped), which is the decision actually holding rather than one pair of it. The structural half is checked by `tests/test_backend_suite_roots_combine.py`: no two collectable test files under `backends/` share a basename, no backend root grows an `__init__.py` (which would silently invalidate that rule), and the set of same-named backend modules is still exactly the one `_load_and_pin` covers, so a NEW collision has to be pinned deliberately instead of being discovered from a combined run months later. It needs no toolchain, so it covers the tiers the `backend-python` job cannot install. The once-seen `test_parallel_259_runtime.py` flake did not recur in any combined run here.
    (b) ✅ **FIXED.** *Original:* `src/revl/deploy.py`'s `reached_emissions` walked component bodies for direct `emit <extern>(...)` call sites, so an emission mediated through a required service's capability-scoped operation was not enumerated, and slice 1's federation admission, which exists to REFUSE a plan that necessarily crosses a non-deferrable irreversible effect, admitted it. Reproduced: `component App requires store: Store { emit store.put("a","b") }` against `emission[host_put] fn put` returned `[]` and was admitted. **A second, worse shape came out of the reproduction and is also fixed:** a provide-method whose body IS the emit (`fn put(k, v) = emit host_put(v)`) lowers to a `return` step (the `emit` marker does not survive lowering), so even a DIRECT irreversible crossing in a provider was admitted. `reached_emissions` now reads `query.Composition` across the activation scope and every provide-method scope, including what a pure fn reaches transitively. CORRECTED 2026-09-29: this sentence said those per-scope facts are the surface `revl audit` prints, and that finding externs by CALLED NAME is sound because `emit` is the only spelling that reaches an emission extern. Both were false. `revl audit` prints `boundary._boundary`, a separate computation. A function value (`let g = charge` then `g(n)`, or `apply(charge, n)`) reaches an emission extern with no call site bearing its name, and the called-name walk admitted it. PR 1459 (merged `54a0aecbb`, 2026-09-27) moved the extern rows onto `Composition.emission_routes`, the checker's call and value channels closed by the G4 fixed point, so a value route is refused and a `deferred` extern reached by any route but a direct `emit` counts as class (c). The per-scope `externs` facts that `revl query` and `erase_report._crossings` read still do not name an extern reached as a value: issue #1458, PR 1544 in review. A mediated crossing is counted only when the key resolves to NO local provider, carries `via: "<key>.<method>"`, and is class (c) by item 245 Decision 2 verbatim ("deferral is an extern-declaration property, not spellable on a service method"). **Counting it unconditionally would have been the wrong guard:** when the provider is local, its own scope already carries the crossing with the extern's TRUE deferrability, so a second synthetic class-(c) row would downgrade a genuinely `deferred` extern and refuse a plan PREPARE can hold, a false refusal, tested for. The refusal message names `via` rather than only the far-side extern, because the author wrote `emit store.put(..)` and cannot find `host_put` by reading their own component (item 274). Three regression tests in `tests/test_deploy_118.py`; `docs/deploy.md` §4 updated. Not mirrored in `selfhost/*.rvl` (checked: the self-host tree is lexer/parser/checker/lower/emit; `deploy.py` has no self-host counterpart), so item 429's rule has nothing to port.
    (c) ✅ **REPRODUCED, MOVED INTO ITEM 186 AND PINNED, THEN CLOSED THERE 2026-09-07 (16d1a225, #525).** Confirmed on this branch: for a program with a duplicate provision on line 4 and a G4 unmarked-emission call on line 5, the reference reports `G2 provision conflict` (earlier LINE) and `admit_src` reports the G4 unmarked-emission refusal (earlier PHASE). Both refusals are true of the program. No permutation of `admit_src`'s phase order fixes it: moving the link before the component loop just inverts the disagreement, so closing it genuinely needs a line number on EVERY gate refusal plus a collecting sink in `selfhost/lower.rvl`, whose `admit_src` is also what `tools/build_gate_crate.py` digests, i.e. item 186's own deferred work rather than a local edit. **What this sweep changed is that the avoidance was no longer folklore:** `tests/test_selfhost_lower.py::test_which_refusal_wins_diverges_when_a_program_has_several` asserted the exact disagreement and said to delete itself and tick this letter when the two agreed. That is what happened. #525 gave the gate an internal `Refusal{tag,msg,line,seq}` and a collecting sink, `admit_src` began reporting the minimum by `(line, seq)`, the two agreed, and the test deleted itself; `tests/test_selfhost_lower.py::test_multi_refusal_programs_agree` pins the agreement in its place. Full text moved to item 186.
    (d) ✅ **DECIDED: see item 73(d), now marked decided: FREEZE, not retire.** The premise was falsifiable and false: the shipped frontend still MINTS `ir_version: 1` (13 of `examples/*.rvl` compile to v1, `user_cache.rvl` included), so the two-dialect `_emit_v1` bodies are the live path for that class of programs, not fixture-propped fossils. Retiring has a precondition: the frontend must stop minting v1 first. Written into docs/conformance.md and pinned by `tests/test_goldens.py::test_the_frontend_still_mints_ir_version_1`.
    (e) ◑ **HALF ALREADY CLOSED, THE OTHER HALF FIXED HERE.** *Did not reproduce as filed:* the ts tier no longer dies on a generic `EmitError: unsupported expression kind 'subscribe'`; `subscribe`/`stream-merge` already refuse honestly on ts and java, naming item 130 §4.6 and the tiers that do lower streams. *But the ACQUISITION was still a silent bad emit:* `let src = effect Stream.source() undo src.close()` (no `subscribe`) EMITTED on both tiers: ts rendered `host.Stream.source()` against a `host` exporting only Pool/Map/Job, java rendered `Stream src = Stream.source();` against a class it never emits, so the program failed at RUN time (ts) or did not compile (java) instead of being refused, on the tier the README calls the portability reference. wasm refuses this exact shape. Both `_expr` host branches now refuse it by name with the same message shape, tested in `tests/test_stream_reactive.py`. Per item 429, checked the self-host source directly: `selfhost/emit_ts.rvl:1118` and `selfhost/emit_java.rvl:740` are the mirrored `host.<fn>` choke points, and the refusal is deliberately NOT ported: those emitters have no refusal channel at all (java's out-of-surface answer is a `<<DEFER-*>>` marker, the wrong shape for a limit the reference REFUSES) and no fixture reaches a `Stream.*` host builtin, so a port would mean inventing a channel for an unreached shape; the decision is recorded in each file's own WHAT IT DEFERS list. **The headline gap itself stands and is NOT claimed closed:** **CORRECTED 2026-09-15: this headline gap has CLOSED and the sentence that stood here was stale.** `backends/typescript/runtime.ts` carries `StreamSource`, `StreamStage`, `Subscription` and the `Stream` host object, the same async-generator mirror the py reference runs, and `backends/typescript/emit.py`'s `_UNIMPLEMENTED_HOST_ROOTS` is empty: `Stream` graduated OFF it rather than being missing from it, which is why `_HOST_ROOTS` still reads `{"Pool", "Map", "Job"}` and why reading that table as evidence of absence is wrong. What slice 2 widened (stage chain, four backpressure policies, a `Paused` state, a clock-driven drain window) all lowers on ts today. Related to 416(a).
    (f) ✅ **NOT A DEFECT: a scoping note, folded into item 186 so it survives this item closing.** `admit_ambient(src, manifest)` as a new entry point, plus a differential-oracle shape that constructs a running manifest on both sides, is now written into 186's own text alongside the "a wave, not a slice; more corpus does not help" judgment. Nothing to reproduce and nothing to fix.

421. (private security advisories, see SECURITY.md) **CAPABILITY / CODEGEN / SEAM AUDIT (2026-09-02, item 416f surfaces).** Eight proven findings, all with executed reproducers. Fix status noted per item.
    **F1 CRITICAL, ✅ FIXED 2026-09-02** (`57c2fc2b`, `fix/f1-capability-token-attenuation`). `_cap_keyed` (`src/revl/lower.py:10210`) now keys the fold element by the DECLARED capability token, read from the same source of truth `parallel._resolve_emission` uses for the same crossings, so `_check_spawn_attenuation` compares capability BOUNDARIES instead of wiring-key spellings. A boundary that no declaration tokens (an `emission` method with no capability list, an unresolvable service) is still named by its G2 wiring key but in its own token namespace, so a key spelling can never be mistaken for a declared token, and a malformed stored spelling degrades to `*`, fail-closed on both sides. The fixture-rename reproducer is pinned in `tests/test_spawn_attenuation.py`. Original finding: `lower.py:9487` `_cap_keyed` DISCARDS the declared capability token and keys the `Cap` by the component's own local `requires` key spelling, so `_check_spawn_attenuation` (`:9791`) compares identifier spellings that name nothing in common. The project's own fixture `examples/rejections/g4_spawn_widens_capability.rvl` is defeated by renaming the child's `requires` key from `kv_b` to `kv_a`: BASELINE refuses, ATTACK is ADMITTED with `"holds": ["kv_a"], "granted": ["kv_a"], "attenuated": []`. Strongest form: a parent holding a PURE non-emission service under key `notes`, child wires `notes` to `emission[fs.write(path="/etc")]`, and the emitted python performs the `/etc` crossing while the G8 audit surface attests `attenuated: ["notes"]`. Violates the guarantee `lower.py:9793` states in its own comment (items 66/294). The data is available and the loss is deliberate: `parallel.py:104` `_resolve_emission` reads the real declared tokens for the same crossings, which is why `disjoint`/`parallel.py` is sound. Only interactive deployments have a second control (`mcp/session.py:37` `_cap_covers`); a compiled artifact has none. `test_spawn_attenuation.py` / `test_parameterized_capabilities.py` / `test_budget_260.py` were 36/36 green while this worked.
    **F2 HIGH, ✅ FIXED 2026-09-02** (`57c2fc2b`, same branch). The ceiling rule is now CONE-SCOPED and EXISTENTIAL (`_ceiling_attenuation_check`, `src/revl/lower.py:10524-10555`): the only held caps that can license a child crossing are those whose resource-only projection covers the crossing's own cone (`cap_order.split_ceilings` plus the stripped-pair `covers` test), and ONE single held cap must license the whole crossing, binding every ceiling at-or-above the child's. The per-token `max()` is gone, and so is the per-parameter `max` over covering caps, which would have been the same unsoundness one level in. Original finding: `lower.py:9789-9795` `_ceiling_attenuation_check` aggregates the parent budget per bare token with `max()`, discarding the resource cone: a parent holding `fs(calls=1,path="/a")` and `fs(calls=100,path="/b")` admits a child granted `fs(calls=100,path="/a")`, a 100x budget on the cone where the parent holds 1. The docstring calls `max` "the conservative first cut"; it is conservative in the unsound direction.
    **F3 HIGH, ✅ FIXED 2026-09-02** (`123f2eaa`, `fix/injective-identifier-mangling`). The reserved-word rename is injective on ALL FIVE tiers now, by the same rule everywhere: escape a name iff the name OR any name reachable from it by dropping trailing `_` is reserved, and escape it by exactly one `_`, which shifts the whole `kw`/`kw_`/`kw__` ladder up one rung and leaves every other name verbatim. Verified present in `backends/python/emit.py:278-289`, `backends/typescript/emit.py:232-243`, `backends/go/emit.py:295-306`, `backends/java/emit.py:289-299` and `backends/rust/emit.py:705-715`. The self-host emitters were ported with it (item 429(b)), but item 429(c) is a DIFFERENT defect, the reserved-word SET rather than the mangling rule, and is NOT closed by this: see its own marker. Original finding: The keyword-mangling rename is NOT injective (`backends/python/emit.py:279`, and the same shape in ts/go/java): `lambda` maps to `lambda_` and a distinct identifier `lambda_` maps to `lambda_`. On PYTHON this silently captures: a function whose revl semantics return `PUBLIC-VALUE` returns `SEKRIT-CANARY` in emitted code. Top-level fns collide the same way, changing the module's exported API; record fields collide while the literal keeps the RAW key. The other tiers break LOUDLY (verified with real toolchains), which is why python is the dangerous one. `lower.py:479` `_safe_name` threads a `taken` set and IS injective; the emitters' binder path has no equivalent. Also a direct counterexample to CrossTier's agreement framing (item 418): same program, different value on python than on five tiers. No test covered it.
    **F4 HIGH, ✅ FIXED 2026-09-02** (`191633bf`, `fix/421-f4-url-credential-capability`). `_authority_split`/`_authority_host` now parse the authority properly (userinfo is everything before the LAST `@`; the authority ends at the first `/`, `?` or `#`; IPv6 literals recognized by their brackets) and `_net_cap` derives the token from the host alone, so the two URLs below no longer collapse. **The fix found a SECOND, independent leak path this finding did not name:** the raw URL including its credential was also echoed verbatim into the generated `.rvl` header comment (`// Server: https://sk_live_SEKRIT:@...`) and into every extern's host-call comment, both of which ride into the compiled IR as the extern's host-body text. So the credential reached the IR by a route that a correct capability-token fix alone would have left wide open. A new `_redact_userinfo` strips userinfo from both. Regression test `tests/test_import_openapi_url_credential_421_f4.py` (11 tests) asserts the key is absent, case-insensitively, from the generated source AND the full compiled-IR dump. Original finding: `import_openapi.py:1140` split `:` before `@`, so a URL credential became the capability TOKEN: `https://sk_live_SEKRIT:@api.example.com/v1` yields `net.sk_live_sekrit`. Verified end to end: the key lands in the generated `.rvl`, the enumerated capability scope, the item-254 compensate audit record, and the compiled IR (it did NOT reach the MCP wire). Second half: the capability names the USERNAME not the boundary, so `https://admin:pw1@bank.example.com` and `https://admin:pw2@attacker.example.net` collapse to the SAME token `net.admin` (covers=True, disjoint=False) — an operator policy on one host authorizes another. The snake-cased spelling is not recognizable as a credential, so a reviewer scrubbing URLs leaves the key in the token.
    **F5 HIGH, ✅ FIXED 2026-09-02** (`fix/421-f5f6-seam-value-leaks`). A provider-side failure is now funnelled through `bridge.seam_failure` -> `confidential.redact_call_text` before it is marshalled: every value the call was made with (nested in a record or a list, numbers included) is replaced by `<redacted:arg>`, and any value a declared `Secret[T]` marking already registered by `<redacted:secret>`, while the exception TYPE and the sentence around the value survive. The consumer's re-raise (`_Client.call`) and the runner log inherit the ONE funnel, so all three sinks close together. Fixed on FOUR tiers with the identical shape and the identical marker: python, typescript (`seamFailure`), go (`bridge.SeamFailure`, hoisting `req.Args` into the error branch) and java (`seamFailure`, hoisting `args` out of the try). **CORRECTION (2026-09-11, `fix/go-seam-registry-stage`): on the GO tier the sentence above overstated the fix — the registered-`Secret[T]` half of the funnel was not there.** `SeamFailure` swept the call's own arguments and nothing else, so a failure quoting a value the composition holds but was not called with (the case this paragraph names as covered) crossed back unredacted. The registry lives in the generated package and the funnel in `bridge`, which the generated package already imports, so the reverse reference is a package cycle; the emitter now installs it as a hook (`bridge.SecretScrub`) from the generated bridge half's `init`, and the funnel runs it as stage 2. Same paragraph, same markers, one tier corrected rather than a second scheme. **CORRECTION (2026-09-11, `fix/421-f5-rust-seam-funnel`): the sentence that stood here about RUST was wrong, and wrong in the way that matters — `placement_runner/src/main.rs` never building an error reply is not a shape with nothing to fix, it is the reason there IS one, and the F5 half of hypothesis H3 was retired on a false premise.** On the other four tiers a failure is a reply the runner builds and the funnel runs there; on rust the only reply is `{"ok": true, "value": ...}` (`handle_conn`), so a `Result`'s `Err` payload IS the value channel and `_bridge_ret_ser` serialized it unchanged — a provider failure quoted the call's own arguments straight back to the consumer, exactly as the original finding describes. The funnel therefore goes in the GENERATED half rather than in the runner: rust has no reflection, so the registry is a process-global the emitted code populates and reads in-line, and the runner holds no registry for a hook to reach — the one structural difference from the go tier, where the hook exists because the generated package already imports `bridge`. The emitter now runs `revl_funnel_err_value` over the DECODED `Err` value's string leaves (stage 1 longest-needle-first over the call's own arguments, then `revl_redact_text` as stage 2), producing the same `<redacted:arg>` marker as the other four tiers; the `Ok` half is untouched, so a `Secret[T]` return still crosses intact and the consumer registers it. Stage 2 is defence in depth on this tier rather than the primary trigger: the checker refuses a declared `Secret[T]` reaching a provide-method return (`G-SECRET-FLOW`), so a failure quoting a registered credential has to have laundered the marking through an unmodelled host body first, while stage 1 — the caller's own bytes crossing back inside a driver's message — is reachable with no author interpolation at all. Regression: `tests/test_rust_seam_failure_scrub.py` (5 tests: the emitted shape, the marker's equality with the other four tiers', byte-identical output for a marking-free document, and a real `cargo test` against the generated dispatch with a non-vacuity check that the host body's own text DOES quote the argument). Bounded honestly: the match is exact, so an argument the host REFORMATS before printing it (truncation, case fold, `%.2f`) is not caught, and a value shorter than 3 characters is left alone because below that a substring match is a coin flip against ordinary English. Regression: `tests/test_seam_value_leaks_421_f5f6.py` (19 tests, canary-absent AND marker-present on every sink, plus false-positive controls) and `backends/typescript/tests/seam_error_arg_leak.test.ts` (9). Original finding: the seam ERROR reply returned raw argument values to the consumer on every tier: `backends/python/bridge.py:432` (its own comment reads `# marshal the failure across the seam (leak 3)`), re-raised `:638`, logged `_process_runner.py:352`; same at `typescript/bridge.ts:456`, `java/.../PlacementRunner.java:353`, `go/.../bridge.go:199`. The forward crossing into a declared `Secret[Str]` receiver is admitted and the reverse is refused by the checker, but the error channel performs the refused reverse crossing unanalysed. Trigger is a plain dict lookup, no author interpolation. Confirmed cross-tier (ts `serve` to py `_Client`). **CORRECTION (2026-09-11, `fix/seam-consumer-error-scrub`): on the TYPESCRIPT tier the CONSUMER half of the sentence above was not true, and it was the half that tier's own tests could not see.** The paragraph says the consumer's re-raise inherits the ONE funnel, and on python it does (`_Client.call` re-raises what `seam_failure` already scrubbed). On typescript `seamCall` did `throw new Error(reply.error)` verbatim (`backends/typescript/bridge.ts:353`): the reply is text THIS process did not write, and this process holds values it has no intention of handing to whatever handler catches the throw, so whether they leaked was a property of the PEER's implementation rather than of this one -- an older revision, or another language's runtime, could hand back whatever it liked. The gap survived the F5 regression suites for a structural reason rather than a careless one: `seamCall` is SYNCHRONOUS (`execFileSync`), so `seam_error_arg_leak.test.ts` and `seam_error_secret_leak.test.ts` both exercise `serve` and the raw wire (`callOnce`) instead, and `makeProxy` -- the path that actually re-raises -- was reached by no test on the tier. The fix runs the SAME `seamFailure` on the consumer side, so it is one funnel applied at two ends rather than a second mechanism: stage 1 the argument needles, stage 2 `redactText` over the values a declared marking registered, the same two markers. The extra pass is safe because it is IDEMPOTENT over a reply the provider already scrubbed (`seamFailure(scrubbed, args) === scrubbed`, asserted directly), and running it is what makes the guarantee this process's own property instead of its peer's. Regression: `backends/typescript/tests/seam_consumer_error_scrub.test.ts` (3 tests: the hostile peer's raw wire is read FIRST and shown to carry both canaries, then the thrown error is asserted to carry neither while keeping the exception type and both markers; the false-positive half, that a value the funnel has no reason to hide survives verbatim; and the idempotence identity). The hostile peer is a SEPARATE process by necessity -- a peer sharing the consumer's event loop deadlocks, because the consumer blocks in `execFileSync` before it ever yields -- which is the same fact that kept the path untested. Bounded honestly: this closes the tier's own re-raise and does not touch the value channel, which still has no funnel on any of the five tiers (the gap note under F6(b)).
    **F6 HIGH, ✅ FIXED 2026-09-02** (`fix/421-f5f6-seam-value-leaks`). The scrub is placed at `runtime._record`, the ONE choke point every trace event passes through, not at each `_record` call site: a trace event is a string the runtime already interpolated into, so `confidential.redact_value` (a VALUE funnel) cannot reach it, and per-printer redaction is the discipline `confidential.py` exists to replace. A new `confidential.redact_text` scrubs it, matching EXACTLY against the values a declared marking registered, so ordinary trace is byte-identical and a secret-free composition pays one empty-set test. That covers every site F6 names AND the siblings it does not (answering "is any other `_record` value secret-typed": `pool.open {url}` / `pool.open refused {url}` / `pool.close {url}` (a connection string is the classic credential carrier and is often a `Secret[Str]` config field); `pool.execute {sql}`; `stream.{kind} fault {reason}`; `stream.drop_newest {item}` / `stream.drop_oldest {evicted}`; `job.run {name}`. The rest interpolate only counters, tags or author-written kind labels). **The fix needed a second half the finding did not name:** the marking was only registered by `replay.redact_args`, i.e. only under `revl run --record` / `--wal` / an MCP `revl_load(record=True)`, while a plain `revl run` prints the SAME console with no recorder attached, which is the console the audit read. So the emitted program now marks it itself, at BOTH ends of the declared marking: `_revl_mark_secret(p)` at the head of a provide method whose service declares that param `Secret[T]` (read off the existing `params[i]["secret"]` stamp), and `@_revl_secret_result` on an extern whose declared return was `Secret[T]`, item 256 §7a's origin, where the value enters the value world, which is the case the receiver-end marking misses (`let t = emit mint(); effect store.insert(t, v)`, the exact repro). The return-side stamp is new IR (`externs[i]["secret_return"]`, additive, set from `taint.py` where the qualifier is stripped), the counterpart of `params[i]["secret"]`, since `ext.returns` no longer mentions `Secret`. **TIERS: py, typescript, go** — every tier whose host objects interpolate a value into a trace at all. ts and go are closed the same way and on the same two IR markings this fix introduced the reading of (`params[i]["secret"]`, `externs[i]["secret_return"]`, plus a config field's `secret` on ts): a funnel at each tier's own single choke point (`runtime.record`, the emitted `hostRecord`), and the emitted program registering the declared value itself at both ends, so the marking fires with nothing attached. Emitted only for a document that declares a `Secret[T]`, so no golden moved and a secret-free composition is byte-identical. **java, rust and wasm are OUT OF SCOPE by construction, not by omission**: java's emitted `Map`/`Pool` and the wasm tier record nothing at all, and rust's `Pool::execute` takes its sql without tracing it, leaving the stream registry as its only trace — which no component body can reach, since a stream item is delivered from the host side. Executable proofs: `backends/go/scenarios/emitted/secret_trace/exec_test.go` on real stc-go and `backends/typescript/tests/host_trace_secret.test.ts` on real cordis, both paired canary-absent/placeholder-present with a false-positive control that an ordinary value is still recorded verbatim; the emitter-level parity and the byte-identical secret-free control in `tests/test_tier_host_trace_secret_421_f6.py`. **Self-host check (item 429), done DIRECTLY on the source rather than through the oracle:** `selfhost/emit_py.rvl` `method_lines` (:1067) receives only the method IR node and `provide_lines` never threads the services table, so the self-host cannot see `params[i].secret`; `_emit_externs` (:687) likewise renders the verbatim body with no return-side marking. Both emitter changes are therefore a REAL divergence for a composition with a `Secret[T]` surface, invisible today only because `tests/fixtures/emit_py_corpus/` contains no `Secret[` at all (grepped), so the differential oracle is green and would have said nothing. Recorded, not ported. Regression: `tests/test_seam_value_leaks_421_f5f6.py`. Original finding: secret-typed values printed verbatim to the operator console by the seam host trace: `backends/python/runtime.py:3559` `_record(f"{self._tag}.insert {key}")` (siblings at 3573/3576/3582, `pool.query {sql}` 3016, `stream.emit {item}` 3204), sink installed `_process_runner.py:247`, forwarded to the conductor's stdout `placement.py:2301-2303`. `Secret[Str]` as a stdlib `Map` key compiles, and the shipped `demo/components/user_cache.rvl:14` idiom is exactly that shape. `runtime.py:449-462` proves the redaction discipline exists in this same file and was not applied here.
    **F6(b), the declared `Secret[T]` config field reaching the checker. ✅ LANDED 2026-09-02 via PR #211.** A component config field declared `Secret[T]` reached the EMITTERS -- every tier reads `config[i]["secret"]` to keep the value out of its run log and its `revl_load` MCP response -- but never reached the checker's ORIGIN LATTICE: a `config.<field>` read was seeded CLEAN alongside a literal, so an operator-supplied credential was invisible to every §7 rule at once, the disclosure sinks and the provide-method return across the service / MCP bridge and the placement seam included. `taint.TaintModel.secret_config` carries the marking off the same declaration the emitters read, and `_FlowChecker.taint_of` mints `confidential` on the read -- the THIRD mint of that origin, beside a `Secret[T]` extern return and a `Secret[T]` parameter, and the only one that had no seed. No rule changed; the existing refusals saw the value as soon as it carried the origin. **Refusal was chosen over a runtime scrub at the marshalling boundary, deliberately.** The language's posture is that a violation is REFUSED, not scrubbed, and here the refusal was also the only tier-independent answer: of the five placement tiers only py and ts register a config value with a redaction registry at load, go registered none, and java/rust/wasm have no such registry at all -- so a marshalling-boundary patch would have needed new runtime surface on four tiers and STILL missed a config field on three of them. Blast radius bounded and checked: no `.rvl` in the tree declares a `Secret[T]` config field, the intended use (handing the credential to the component's own host binding) is not a disclosure crossing and compiled unchanged, and the declared `endorse[confidential]` downgrade was untouched. The go tier's `Load<Comp>` also registers the value with its own funnel now (`revlMarkSecret(cfg.<Field>)`), bringing its host trace to py/ts parity. Regression: `tests/test_secret_flow.py` §7 (7 tests: the refusal on both halves of a `Result` and at a disclosure sink, plus four false-positive controls) and `tests/test_tier_host_trace_secret_421_f6.py` (2).
    **Two gaps this work did not touch, and did not create -- stated so the ✅ above is read for what it covers.** (i) The VALUE CHANNEL has no redaction funnel on any of the five placement tiers: `{"ok": true, "value": ...}` is marshalled unscrubbed, where F5 gave the error channel one. F6(b) removed the DECLARED route into it -- a `Secret[T]` value cannot reach a provide-method return on any tier, because the refusal is in the frontend -- so what is left is a value the checker never saw: one an extern's host body reflects out of its own scope, or an operator value the author did not declare `Secret[T]`. A frontend refusal reaches neither; closing them means giving the value channel the funnel the error channel already has, on five tiers. (ii) java, rust and wasm have no runtime secret registry to extend, so a declared credential handed to the component's own host binding -- the use F6(b) correctly does not refuse, since it is the point of the declaration -- reaches whatever trace those tiers keep, verbatim. py and ts already registered config values; go does now. Both predate this work and are unchanged by it; each is a candidate item rather than an open F-finding, and neither is claimed as closed.
    **F6(c), the RUNNER CONSOLE bypassing the funnel -- found while closing issue #815 item 5 ("no probe-result secret test on any tier"). ✅ FIXED ON THE PY AND TS TIERS 2026-09-10 (`inso1337-fix-issue-815-seam-leak-test-gaps`).** F6 placed the scrub at the emitted program's single trace choke point (`runtime._record` / `runtime.record`), and the placement runner prints its OWN channels around it: `load`, `serve`, `proxy`, `fiber` and `probe` are written by the runner process, not by the runtime, so only the `host |` line ever reached the funnel. A provider whose probe fails therefore printed the failure -- and the value inside it -- verbatim BESIDE a `host` line the same run redacted: observed live on a component with `config { api_key: Secret[Str] }` and an extern raising `KeyError(key + " @ " + url)`, where `[provider] probe | vault.open('alice')| ERROR KeyError: '<canary> @ pg://main'` carried the canary while `host  | Keeper.config` showed `{api_key="<redacted:secret>", url="pg://main"}`. The two halves are independent: the host half was F6 working, and the probe half was a second printer, which is exactly the "per-printer redaction" discipline F6's own placement note says a single funnel exists to replace. py (`_process_runner.py`: `log` funnels `subject` and `detail` through `confidential.redact_text`) and ts (`placement_runner.ts`: through `runtime.redactText`) are fixed; **java was already covered** (`PlacementRunner.log` -> `redactSecrets`, the shape the fix copies), so the finding is a py/ts divergence rather than a tier-wide hole. **go is left open**: its runner log (`backends/go/placement_runner/main.go:101`) prints raw as well, but go's emitted registry exists only for a document that declares a `Secret[T]`, so a funnel there needs an always-emitted no-op form; the prototype run showed the failing-extern path surfacing on a channel the runner does not own. **THE GO HALF IS NOW CLOSED (2026-09-11, `fix/go-seam-registry-stage`)**, and the obstacle named above is what the design avoids rather than solves: `bridge.SecretScrub` is a nil-able hook the generated bridge half installs from its `init` only for a document that declares a marking, and `ScrubText` is the identity while it is nil — so the no-op form is the absence of the hook, with nothing extra emitted and a marking-free placement byte-identical. The runner's `log` closure now scrubs the whole rendered line, which covers `probe`/`load` for the same reason the py and ts funnels sit at one choke point. Residual, unchanged and NOT closed here: a host body that PANICS prints its message from the go runtime itself, before `log` runs and outside every funnel this tier has — the same residual the rust tier carries, and the reason the note above says the failing-extern path surfaces on a channel the runner does not own. Executable proofs: `tests/test_seam_leak_gaps_815.py::test_a_probe_whose_extern_fails_locally_does_not_print_the_secret` (py: a real `revl run --placement --once`, canary absent AND marker present AND the non-secret half of the SAME message still readable, so it cannot pass on an empty console) and `backends/typescript/tests/runner_console_secret_leak.test.ts` (ts: the shipped `log` sliced out of the runner source and executed with the real `redactText` injected).
