    **CORRECTION (2026-09-11, `fix/814-uncaught-funnel-tiers`): two claims in the paragraph above were wrong, and the second was wrong about WHICH tier was the outlier.** First, "java was already covered" overstates it. `PlacementRunner.log` was funnelled, but the JVM's own uncaught-exception channel -- a driver thread's exception escaping to the default handler, which writes `System.err` from the JVM itself -- was behind no funnel at all, so a value the composition held at that moment printed beside the stack trace. Second, rust was not a tier "carrying the same residual" as go; its runner console had NO funnel of any kind. `load`, `serve`, `probe`, the boot-failure line and the panic hook all wrote verbatim, which is the F6(c) hole itself rather than a panic-only footnote, and the reason is structural: F6 put the registry in the EMITTED module, which the runner's own printers cannot reach. The registry therefore MOVED into the runner crate (`backends/rust/placement_runner/src/confidential.rs`), with the emitted module importing it under `_SECRET_MODE` so the two halves share one process-global, and every printer in `src/main.rs` now renders through `confidential::revl_redact_text`. On the same shape go, java and ts gained the process-level disposition they lacked: go a `recover` registered first in `main` (so it runs LAST, after the teardown's own cancel) that scrubs through `bridge.ScrubText` and `os.Exit(1)`s, java a `Thread.setDefaultUncaughtExceptionHandler` writing through `redactSecrets` and `Runtime.halt(1)`, ts a `process.on('uncaughtException'|'unhandledRejection')` pair writing through `redactText` with `fs.writeSync(2, ...)` because a `console.log` line still buffered in a piped stream when `process.exit` runs is dropped. All four EXIT rather than unwinding, so the teardown's own `DOWN` never prints, and none of them is a per-printer patch: each sits at the one disposition its runtime offers. Executable proofs, one per tier, each pairing the redacted line with a non-vacuity arm that un-wires the funnel: `tests/test_go_runner_fatal_funnel.py` (7), `backends/java/test_java_runner_fatal_funnel.py` (6), `backends/rust/test_rust_runner_console_scrub.py` (8 shape + 1 live) and `backends/typescript/test_runner_fatal_funnel_ts.py` (10, including a premise test that the ts registry is call-time only -- unlike java/rust/go it does not register a `Secret[T]` config field at load, so the live trigger has to run a probe first). The one window none of the four closes is stated rather than implied: an `import` that fails is instantiated before any statement of the runner runs, and nothing is registered at that point on any tier.
    **F6(d), the ESCAPED VALUE -- found while closing issue #815 item 3 ("no escaped-secret canary"). ✅ FIXED ON THE PY AND TS TIERS 2026-09-10 (same branch).** Both stages match a value EXACTLY, against text that has already been RENDERED, and `_needles` registered the value's RAW bytes only. Every encoder that renders host text rewrites what it writes -- `repr` of a container and `str(exception)` put the value inside a `'...'` literal (`SEC"RET` becomes `SEC\"RET`), and `json.dumps` puts it inside a `"..."` one on the way to the WAL, the conductor inventory and the seam wire, tabs and newlines escaped as well -- so the raw needle, and with it every argument needle, matched NOTHING at such a sink: a `Secret[Str]` holding a `"`, a `\` or a control character was redacted NOWHERE (the host trace, the WAL, an approval ticket, F5's seam failure reply, since `redact_call_text` shares `_needles`, and the runner console F6(c) had just closed), while the SAME value without the quote was scrubbed everywhere. A password or a DSN with a quote in it is ordinary, so the hole was total for a whole class of secrets rather than partial. The fix is `confidential._renderings`: a registered string contributes the bytes the encoders that render host text actually write (the raw value, a `json.dumps` body with `ensure_ascii`, a `repr` body), and the match stays EXACT and still runs over rendered text -- what changed is that the needle is one of the renderings rather than an assumption about which one the sink would have chosen. `_MIN_MARKABLE` (4) still gates the RAW value and an escape only ever EXPANDS, so one bound covers all three faces; the ts tier mirrors it (`runtime.ts` `renderings()`, deriving the json face from `JSON.stringify` itself), because a polyglot composition has to redact the same bytes whichever tier wrote the line. Live repro before the fix: the same `revl run` printed the canary verbatim on the runner's `probe` channel, next to a `host` line rendering it redacted for the identical value. **java, rust and go are left open and carry the same shape** -- `emit.py`'s emitted `revlRememberSecret`/`revlRedactText`, `emit.py`'s `revl_remember_secret`/`revl_redact_text` and the go preamble all register the raw string; the change is the same addition to each remembering function, deferred here to keep this change confined to the leaks it can prove by execution -- the java arm is at `backends/java/emit.py:_SECRET_PREAMBLE` (`revlRememberSecret` registers `String.valueOf(value)` alone, and `PlacementRunner.render` routes a container through `Json.write`, whose `esc` rewrites `"`, `\` and the short control escapes before any `revlRedactText` sees the line, so the java arm is REAL and not merely theoretical), and the rust/go arms at their preambles. Executable proofs: `tests/test_seam_leak_gaps_815.py::test_one_registration_covers_every_face_a_sink_renders` (the registry itself, no cordis needed) and `::test_no_sink_carries_the_escaped_canary_in_its_escaped_or_raw_form` (a real run, both the raw and the `json.dumps` form absent), plus `backends/typescript/tests/secret_escaping.test.ts` (all three faces, a false-positive control that an unregistered string which merely LOOKS escaped is untouched, and the secret-free pass-through). **The java, rust and go arms are now closed too** (`fix/421-f6d-escaped-secret-faces`). All three register the raw face first, then the escaped one, so the `REVL_MIN_MARKABLE` / `_MIN_MARKABLE` bound still gates the RAW value alone -- an escape only ever expands, so one bound covers every face. Where the encoder is reachable from the emitted program the face is taken FROM it rather than restated, so it cannot drift: rust's `revl_renderings` derives from `serde_json::to_string` (the wire encoder) and `format!("{:?}", text)` (the host's own diagnostics), and go's `revlRenderings` from `json.Marshal` itself (the runner's probe channel and the seam wire). Java's encoder lives in `PlacementRunner.Json.esc`, which an emitted unit cannot reach, so `revlRenderings` restates its five characters and the coupling is pinned by a textual drift check that reads both tables and compares them. **The go arm also carried a compile defect**: the emitted registry calls `json.Marshal`, and go has TWO independent import blocks -- a document with a top-level extern routes through one and a document with components and nothing top-level through the other -- so `encoding/json` had to be added to both, and a v3-typed-core document declaring a `Secret[T]` did not compile before that. Executable proofs, each with the pre-F6(d) registration shown to leak the escaped face on the same run: `backends/go/test_secret_registry_go.py` (the emitted registry sliced out of the program and run against a real `json.Marshal`), `backends/java/test_secret_registry.py` (the emitted unit compiled beside the runner's real `Json` class and run, so the check cannot pass against a restatement), and `backends/rust/test_rust_secret_registry.py` (the emitted crate run under `cargo test` against the real `serde_json` and `{:?}`).
    **F6(e), the CONTAINER's OWN face -- the go half of F6(d) was incomplete, and the gap was structural rather than an oversight. ✅ FIXED 2026-09-10 (`fix/421-f6-secret-value-faces`).** F6(d) pinned go's `revlRenderings` to `json.Marshal` and that coupling is sound, but the STRING it is handed was `fmt.Sprintf("%v", v)`, and `%v`-then-`json.Marshal` is a transform pair only for a scalar `Str` -- `%v` of a string IS the string, so `json.Marshal` is its json escape. For ANY container (`Secret[List[Str]]`, `Secret[Bytes]`, `Secret[Map[Str,Str]]`) `%v` is Go's DEBUG rendering (`[104 101 108 108 111]`, `map[k:v]`), so the registered needle was the json escape of the debug form: longer than the value, differently punctuated, and a substring of nothing any sink writes. The sink is the runner's own probe channel -- `backends/go/placement_runner/main.go:190-193` marshals each probe argument with `json.Marshal` over a `[]json.RawMessage`, `:198` logs `"probe"`, the label and `"=> "+string(encoded)`, and `:101-114` funnels the whole line through `bridge.ScrubText` -- so the container's json body and every leaf of it crossed verbatim, and `fn put(token: Secret[List[Str]]) -> Str` compiles while no `G-SECRET-*` guarantee refuses it. **py was never exposed and rust agrees by accident:** `backends/python/confidential.py:402-441` `_needles` RECURSES into containers (bytes → decoded and repr; int/float → `str`; list/tuple → per item; dict → values only; records via `_members`) and bounds each needle on its own text at `_MIN_MARKABLE = 4`, and rust's `Display` for a container is valid Rust syntax that `serde_json` also produces, so `revl_renderings` happened to agree with the wire; go's flat registry was a real COVERAGE divergence from py's recursion, and this closes it. **CORRECTION (2026-09-11, F6(g) below): the rust half of that sentence is wrong, and wrong at the level of whether the artifact exists — there is no wire for rust to agree with, because a container `Secret[T]` does not compile at all.** `revl_mark_secret<T: std::fmt::Display>` and `revl_secret_result<T: std::fmt::Display>` have no `Display` impl for `Vec<u8>` (`Bytes`) or `Vec<String>` (`List[Str]`), so a `Secret[Bytes]` or `Secret[List[Str]]` composition failed the build with `E0277` at whichever door it reached. "rust agrees by accident" described a compilation that never happened. The fix walks the value itself: `revlIsContainer` gates a recursive `revlRegisterValue` → `revlRegisterText` → `revlRegisterFace` chain that registers each reachable node's `%v` face AND its `json.Marshal` face, bounds each rendering on its own text exactly as `_needles` does, dedupes and re-sorts longest-first (interface/pointer unwrapped with a nil guard; a `Uint8` slice also contributes its decoded byte string; map VALUES only, matching py's documented rationale that keys are author field names; struct fields only where `CanInterface()`; depth capped at 8). The walk is gated on containers so the scalar path stays byte-for-byte what F6(d) pinned, and `reflect` is now imported wherever the registry is emitted, added to BOTH of go's import blocks and guarded against the lifecycle and `needs_reflect` cases that already import it. **Both non-vacuity controls bite:** stripping `range revlRenderings(text)` leaks the escaped scalar face on the same run, and stripping the walk leaks `items`, `items-leaf`, `bytes` (the base64 the wire carries) and `bytes-decoded` while `items-fmt` stays covered by the outer loop -- which is precisely why the gap survived a green suite, since the runner does not write that form. The pre-fix registry shows the same four leaking on an identical driver. **The self-host divergence F6 already recorded extends here:** `selfhost/emit_py.rvl` has no container walk either, so the divergence is now two emitter changes rather than one. Regression: `backends/go/test_secret_registry_go.py`. **CORRECTION (F6(f) below): "py was never exposed" is true of the CONTAINER-COVERAGE gap this entry closed and false of the BYTE LEAF inside it -- `_needles` recursed into a `bytes` and registered two forms that were already renderings, so neither got an encoded face.**
    **F6(f), the BYTES branch's OWN faces -- F6(d) fixed the `str` branch and did not reach the branch beside it, and on the ts tier a byte payload registered nothing at all. ✅ FIXED 2026-09-11 on BOTH tiers (`fix/bytes-secret-encoder-faces` py, `fix/bytes-secret-registration` ts).** F6(e) built a container walk that hands each reachable leaf to the registry, and the leaf it hands a byte payload to is `_needles`'s `bytes` branch, which did `into.add(form)` for `(value.decode("utf-8","replace"), repr(value))` while the `str` branch directly above it did `into.update(_renderings(value))`. Those two byte forms are not raw values -- `repr(bytes)` escapes every non-printable and every non-ASCII byte as `\xNN` -- so they are already RENDERINGS, and registering a rendering as though it were the raw value is exactly the F6(d) shape: the needle is the escaped text, the sink renders it once more, and an encoder escapes a backslash. A `Secret[Bytes]` holding a `"`, a `\` or any non-ASCII byte therefore crossed every sink that encodes before it scrubs, verbatim in escaped form, while the SAME bytes without such a byte were scrubbed everywhere. The decisive sink is `src/revl/_process_runner.py:180`'s `_funnel_line(f"[{name}] HALTED {json.dumps(inventory)}")`, which json-encodes FIRST and scrubs second, so the registry has to match the encoded line; `json.dumps` of a `bytes` raises, so a json sink can only ever carry bytes in DECODED form, which is precisely why the decoded form needs its own encoded face. The fix bounds the RAW form and expands each face from it -- `for form in (value.decode("utf-8","replace"), repr(value)): if len(form) >= minimum: into.update(_renderings(form))` -- the `_MIN_MARKABLE = 4` gate stays on the raw text because an escape only ever expands. **The ts half was larger and unconditional.** `rememberSecret`'s `typeof value === "object"` walk does `Object.values(value)`, which for a `Uint8Array` yields the byte NUMBERS, and `String(byte)` is at most three characters -- under `MIN_MARKABLE = 4` -- while `Array.isArray` is false for a typed array, so the array branch never caught it first: a `Secret[Bytes]` on this tier registered NOTHING, and the host trace interpolates values into template literals where `${uint8array}` is `Array.prototype.join(",")`, so `record(\`stream.emit ${item}\`)` (`runtime.ts:1575`), `record(\`${this.label}.insert(${key}, ${value})\`)` (`:701`), `record(\`${this.label}.query(${sql})\`)` (`:406`) and `record(\`job.run ${name} start\`)` (`:466`) each wrote the comma-joined decimals verbatim on every run. The ts fix extracts a `registerText` helper so the byte path reuses the scalar path's bound and escape faces rather than restating them, and adds an `ArrayBuffer.isView(value) && !(value instanceof DataView)` branch registering the three faces the payload actually wears -- `bytes.join(",")` (the template-literal face, this tier's `%v`), `new TextDecoder().decode(bytes)` (the decoded text a host prints) and `JSON.stringify(value)` (the value's OWN json face: a `Buffer` writes `{"type":"Buffer","data":[...]}` where a plain `Uint8Array` writes `{"0":...}`, and a sink marshals whichever it was handed). `DataView` is excluded because it has no `join`. That is go's canonical three faces off a `[]byte`, which F6(e) already registers; there is no base64 face because no ts bridge or runner carries a byte payload that way (`canonicalBytes` exists only for the correlation seal). **The other two tiers are closed or fail-closed, and the reasons differ.** java was recorded here as "NOT exploitable and needs no fix": `revlMarkSecret(Object...)` calls `String.valueOf(value)`, whose `byte[]` overload is the identity hash (`[B@hash`, matching nothing), and "more decisively" `PlacementRunner.render` falls through to `Json.write(BridgeCodec.encode(v))`, which descends into `List`, `Map`, `Record`, ADT variants and reflective data fields but NOT into a `byte[]`, so a byte payload encodes to `{}` and never reaches a line. **The second clause was wrong and the row is retracted -- java IS exploitable, and F6(h) below is where it is fixed.** The encoder really is blind to `byte[]`; the leak never needed it, because the `String` the seam funnel scrubs is composed by the provider's OWN host body (`new String(raw, UTF_8)`), so the wire's clean json body was the one byte-shaped channel anyone asserted on and it hid the sink that mattered. rust is fail-closed: `revl_mark_secret<T: Display>` has no `Display` for `Vec<u8>`, so a `Secret[Bytes]` there does not compile rather than not registering, and the residual is a silent non-registration to weigh separately from a leak -- **CORRECTED and fixed by F6(g) below, which is where the "does not compile" observation is followed to its conclusion.** **Both non-vacuity controls bite, and the ts one shows the live leak:** `tests/test_seam_leak_gaps_815.py::test_one_registration_covers_every_face_a_bytes_sink_renders` uses the canary `b'secret\xc3\xa9"key'` and fails on the pre-fix `_needles` while passing after, and reverting the ts byte branch fails three of the five new cases in `backends/typescript/tests/secret_bytes.test.ts` with the trace reading `stream.emit 67,65,78,65,82,89,45,52,50,49,45,70,54,69` and `blob=CANARY-421-F6E` where the fixed registry writes `<redacted:secret>`. The gap survived a green suite on both tiers for the same reason F6(d) did: the py suite's byte canaries were ASCII and escape-free, and the ts suite had no byte-payload case at all.
    **F6(g), the rust NON-SCALAR doors -- the rust row F6(f) recorded as "fail-closed" was a BUILD DEFECT, and it was never `Bytes`-specific. ✅ FIXED 2026-09-11 (`fix/bytes-secret-encoder-faces`, the same PR as the py half of F6(f)).** F6(f) wrote that `revl_mark_secret<T: Display>` "has no `Display` for `Vec<u8>`, so a `Secret[Bytes]` there does not compile rather than not registering, and the residual is a silent non-registration to weigh separately from a leak." The first clause is right; what it was read as is not. The front end ACCEPTS `Secret[T]` for every `T` probed (`Str`, `Int`, `Int32`, `Bool`, `Bytes`, `List[Str]`, `Map[Str,Str]` all emit), so a `Display` bound on the doors means the compiler silently accepts a program class whose artifact cannot be built, and the failure lands on the author at `cargo build` rather than on any runtime boundary the threat model talks about. **Not `Bytes`-specific:** the bound is generic over the DECLARED type, so `Vec<String>` (`List[Str]`), every `HashMap` (`Map[..]`), the emitted records and ADT variants (which derive `Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize` and never `Display`) and cordis `Value` fail it identically -- at all three doors: the config door (`revl_mark_secret(&config.<f>)` at the head of the plugin closure), the two receiver doors (a `Secret[T]` provide-method parameter), and the origin door (`revl_secret_result(<call>)` for an extern whose declared return was `Secret[T]`; `taint.py` strips the qualifier before lowering, so `secret_return` is the only surviving record of it). The fix routes by declared type through `_secret_mark_call`/`_secret_result_call`: `Str`/`Int`/`Int32`/`Float`/`Bool` keep the `Display` door untouched, so every existing golden and the self-host mirror stay byte-identical for a scalar `Secret[T]`; `Bytes` takes a concrete `revl_mark_secret_bytes`/`revl_secret_result_bytes` (decoded text via `String::from_utf8_lossy`, the value's own `serde_json` array, and the `Debug` form -- go's F6(e) byte-slice face set, off the same `revl_remember_secret` gate); and every other declared type takes `revl_mark_secret_encoded`/`revl_secret_result_encoded`, bound `T: std::fmt::Debug`. **`Debug` and not `Display` for that fallback** because it is the one formatter EVERY lowering implements -- `cordis::Value` included, whose `Debug` is hand-written (`cordis-rs-0.6.2/src/value.rs:85`) with no `Display` anywhere in the crate -- and it is also the only form a sink can write such a value with, so the registered face is the one a trace would carry. **That second clause is WRONG, and item 421 F6(i) is the entry that refutes it** -- kept here rather than deleted because the correction is the finding: `Debug` is the only form the DOOR can render the container with, but it is NOT the only form a sink writes, because a sink handed one ELEMENT of the container writes the element, which is a different string, and `revl_redact_text` matches an exact needle. F6(g) therefore closed the BUILD defect and left the COVERAGE one standing, which is the same distinction F6(e) drew on go. (F6(i) also renames the second helper `_secret_result_call` to `_secret_result_lines`, since it now emits a block rather than an expression.) The byte door is concrete `&[u8]` rather than a generic bound because its json face needs `T: Serialize`, which `Value` is not, so a generic door could not carry that face at all. Regression: `backends/rust/test_rust_secret_registry.py` pins both door shapes (`test_a_non_scalar_secret_takes_the_door_its_declared_type_needs`, `test_a_scalar_secret_still_takes_the_display_door`) and builds and RUNS the emitted crate (`test_a_non_scalar_secret_composition_builds_and_is_scrubbed`), asserting the decoded, json and `Debug` faces are all scrubbed, the value still returned unchanged, and every face flowing verbatim once the registry is forgotten. **Non-vacuity bites in both directions:** with the dispatch forced back to the single `Display` door the emitted crate fails to build again (`E0277`, one per door, for `Vec<u8>` and `Vec<String>` alike) and both door-shape tests fail. **Severity is LOW/MEDIUM and the class is a build defect, not a disclosure:** nothing leaks, because nothing runs -- but the front end accepted the program, which is the acceptance half of the same `Secret[T]` surface F6(f) closed on py and ts. The self-host divergence is unchanged: `selfhost/emit_rust.rvl` ports no `_SECRET_MODE` (item-429(d)), so the two dispatch helpers are recorded in `tests/fixtures/selfhost_uncovered_lines/` under the existing item 421 F6 rust reason rather than closed by a corpus document, which would flip the byte-agreement oracle red instead.
    **F6(h), the java tier's `Secret[Bytes]` -- F6(f) recorded java as "NOT exploitable and needs no fix", and the reason it gave was wrong in the half that mattered. ✅ FIXED 2026-09-11 (`fix/java-bytes-secret-faces`).** F6(f) offered two reasons and they do not have the same standing. The first is the real root cause and it is CORRECT: `revlMarkSecret(Object...)` reaches `String.valueOf(value)`, whose `byte[]` overload is the identity string `[B@hash`, which no sink this tier writes, so a `Secret[Bytes]` registered a needle that could never match. The second -- "more decisively", that `PlacementRunner.render` falls through to `Json.write(BridgeCodec.encode(v))`, which descends into `List`, `Map`, `Record`, ADT variants and reflective data fields but NOT into a `byte[]`, so "a byte payload encodes to `{}` and never reaches a line" -- is true about the ENCODER and irrelevant to the leak, because the leak never needed the encoder. The decisive sink is the one F6 already funnelled: `PlacementRunner.seamFailure`'s second stage, `redactSecrets(text)`, reached from `BridgeClient.call`'s `reply.get("error")`. The `String` it scrubs was composed by the PROVIDER'S OWN HOST BODY -- `new String(raw, UTF_8)` in the failing extern's java block, the plainest thing an author writes -- so the registry is asked to match decoded text against a registry that holds `[B@hash`, and the runner console via `log()` is the same shape. `BridgeCodec.encode` being blind to `byte[]` is exactly what made the wrong reading look confirmed: it kept the wire's json body clean, and the wire was the only byte-shaped channel anyone asserted on. **Proved by running two real JVMs across a real socket seam, not by reading the emitted source:** the `Secret[Str]` control prints `vault refused key <redacted:secret> at pg://main`, and the same composition with `Secret[Bytes]` printed `vault refused key SEKRIT-JAVA-BYTES-CANARY-421-F6 at pg://main` -- the credential minted by an extern (the `revlSecretResult` origin door, `emit.py:3061`) and quoted by a DIFFERENT binding's failing body, so it was HELD and the funnel's argument stage could not see it by construction. Any container holding one (`Secret[List[Bytes]]`, `Secret[Map[K,Bytes]]`, a record with a `Bytes` field) reaches the same fallthrough, because F6(e)'s walk bottoms out at the leaf the byte branch owns. **Fix:** a `byte[]` branch registering the two forms a byte payload actually wears in host text, each bounded on its own length at `REVL_MIN_MARKABLE` like every other needle -- the DECODED text (`new String(raw, UTF_8)`; malformed input becomes U+FFFD, the same replacement the py tier asks for) and the debug list (`Arrays.toString(raw)`, py's `repr`) -- with the bound/escape/re-sort tail extracted into a single `revlRememberText` so a scalar and a decoded payload register through one funnel, the same shape F6(f) gave ts's `registerText`. It deliberately does NOT mirror py's omission of `str(value)`: java's `[B@hash` is identity, not content. `argNeedles` has no `byte[]` case either, but java's `BridgeCodec.decode` never rebuilds one, so that stage is unreachable for bytes and is left alone rather than given dead code. **Control:** a document declaring no `Secret[T]` is byte-identical (the preamble is emitted only in secret mode, and `tests/fixtures/emit_java_corpus/` declares no `Secret` anywhere, so no golden moves). **Both non-vacuity controls bite, and the second is what makes this a WALK and not just a leaf case:** stripping the branch's two faces prints the held payload AND the container's leaf on an identical two-JVM run, while short-circuiting the `Collection` walk prints the container's leaf ALONE with the scalar beside it still redacted -- the two paths are covered independently. Regression: `backends/java/test_secret_registry.py`, which RUNS the two JVMs and greps what they printed. **Residual, stated and not fixed here, and it FAILS CLOSED rather than disclosing:** the java `byte[]` CONFIG door is broken separately. The plugin constructor a `Secret[Bytes]` config field emits takes a real `byte[]` (`public KeeperPlugin(String url, byte[] key_material)`), but `PlacementRunner.instantiate`'s `coerce` handles `long`/`int`/`double`/`boolean`/`String` and returns every other value unchanged, and a config document can only supply a `String` or a json array for the field. The value therefore arrives uncoerced and `Constructor.newInstance` raises `IllegalArgumentException: argument type mismatch` (verified on a real JVM) -- BEFORE the constructor body runs, so `revlMarkSecret` is never reached, nothing is registered, and nothing is printed. The consequence is a field that can never be supplied from a config document, not a value that escapes: a functional gap that belongs to the config door, and the reason it is recorded here rather than filed. `selfhost/emit_py.rvl`'s missing container walk (F6(e)) is likewise unaffected by this java-side change. **With this the five placement tiers are closed on the byte-payload face: py and ts by F6(f), go by F6(e), rust by F6(g), java here.**
    **F6(i), the rust CONTAINER's leaves -- F6(g) closed the build defect on the non-scalar doors and registered the container's own `Debug` face, and a sink writes a different form than that. ✅ FIXED 2026-09-11 (`fix/rust-container-secret-faces`).** The encoded door F6(g) introduced (`revl_mark_secret_encoded`/`revl_secret_result_encoded`) registers ONE rendering: `format!("{:?}", value)`, the container's own debug form. `revl_redact_text` matches an EXACT needle, so that face covers a trace that prints the WHOLE container and nothing else -- while the plainest thing an author writes at a sink is one ELEMENT of it (`stream.emit(leaves[0])`, `record(format!("{}", map[\"key\"]))`), which is a different string and crossed every funnelled sink verbatim: the host trace, the WAL record, F5's seam failure reply. The F6(g) rationale is where the gap was read past -- it argued `Debug` "is also the only form a sink can write such a value with", which is true of the FORMATTER and false of the SINK. This is exactly the assumption F6(e) disproved on go one entry earlier, unported to this tier: go walks the value at RUNTIME (`revlRegisterValue`'s reflect walk, `depth > 8`), and py's `_needles` recurses `list`/`tuple` and `dict` VALUES, so rust was the one tier of the three whose registry stopped at the container's own face. **Not `Bytes`-specific, and not reachable only through an extern return:** every non-scalar declared `Secret[T]` takes the encoded door -- `Secret[List[Str]]`, `Secret[Map[Str,Str]]`, `Secret[Opt[Str]]`, a record, a `Result` -- at all three doors (the config door at the head of the plugin closure, the two receiver doors for a provide-method parameter, and the origin door for an extern whose declared return is `Secret[T]`). **Fix: the leaves are resolved at the DOOR, not in the helper.** Rust has no reflection, so a runtime walk of the go shape is not available; what the door has and the helper does not is the DECLARED surface type, so `_secret_face_lines` recurses that type string at emit time and emits one registration statement per reachable leaf, each through the door that leaf's OWN declared type takes (a `Bytes` leaf therefore gets the F6(g) byte trio rather than the scalar door). `List`/`Opt`/`Map`/`Result` decompose structurally (`.iter()` / `.as_ref()` / `.values()` / `match .. as_ref()`), a record walks its fields through `_ident`, and anything the walk cannot decompose returns no statements, leaving the container face as the whole coverage -- the same kind of bound go's walk had when this was written (a `depth > 8` cap, since replaced by a path bound), and a strictly NARROWER one than py's, which registers any list or dict it happens to hold. **Map walks VALUES only,** matching py's `_needles` and go's `revlRegisterValue`: a map's keys are the author's field names, not the caller's data. Two door shapes had to change with it. `_secret_mark_call` appends the walk FLATTENED onto one line, because the receiver door's registration string is both appended standalone and INLINED into a method head, so a multi-line walk would emit invalid Rust in one of the two positions. `_secret_result_call` becomes `_secret_result_lines` and emits a block instead of an expression: the origin door is the block's TAIL expression, so the value is bound once (`let _revl_v = {call};`), its container face registered, the walk run over `_revl_v`, and `_revl_v` returned as the tail. **Proved by a real `cargo` build and run, not by reading the emitted source:** `test_a_returned_containers_leaf_is_scrubbed_on_its_own` compiles the emitted crate and drives the origin door for `Secret[List[Str]]`, `Secret[Map[Str,Str]]` and `Secret[Opt[Str]]`, asserting the LEAF ALONE is scrubbed for each shape plus a non-vacuity control, and `test_the_container_door_emits_a_walk_over_every_reachable_leaf` pins the emitted shape of the receiver and config doors (inline walk, `.values()` for Map, `if let Some(..) = _revl_v.as_ref()` for `Opt`, and the scalar doors byte-unchanged). **Non-vacuity bites in both directions:** against the pre-fix emitter both new tests fail -- the cargo one on the live leaf assertion, the shape one on the missing walk -- and the whole `backends/rust/` suite is 155 passed with the fix. **Only the origin door is driveable end-to-end from a `#[cfg(test)]` harness:** the config and receiver doors are inline in a plugin closure (`KeeperConfig` / `ctx.effect`), so their walk is pinned by the emitted-shape test while the origin door carries the live proof. **A direct call to the HELPER is deliberately unchanged** (`revl_mark_secret_encoded(&list)` still registers the container face alone): the walk lives in the door, which is where the declared type is, and that is the difference between this fix and go's. The self-host divergence is unchanged and now covers the new helper: `selfhost/emit_rust.rvl` ports no `_SECRET_MODE` (item-429(d)), so `_secret_face_lines` and `_secret_result_lines` are recorded in `tests/fixtures/selfhost_uncovered_lines/` under the existing item 421 F6 rust reason rather than closed by a corpus document, which would flip the byte-agreement oracle red instead. **Severity HIGH and the class is a DISCLOSURE, not a build defect** -- unlike F6(g), which was the same surface failing to compile: the program builds, runs and prints the credential. Regression: `backends/rust/test_rust_secret_registry.py`. **Parity across the tiers (checked 2026-09-29, issue #1572).** The leaf walk this entry gave rust is what the other registry tiers already do at run time: the py tier's `_needles` (`backends/python/confidential.py`) recurses lists, tuples and dict values; the go runtime's `revlRegisterValue` (`backends/go/emit.py`) walks the value by reflection, bounded by the path rather than a depth (`backends/go/test_secret_registry_go.py::test_the_walk_is_bounded_by_the_path_and_not_by_a_depth`); the ts runtime's `rememberSecret` (`backends/typescript/runtime.ts`) recurses arrays, a `Map`'s keys and values, and a record's values; and the java runtime's `revlRememberSecret` (`backends/java/emit.py`) walks by identity path, pinned by `backends/java/test_secret_registry.py::test_with_the_container_walk_stripped_the_leaf_leaks`. The wasm tier was measured on its own (issue #1577), because it has no secret registry and no redaction funnel. Of the host-visible sinks its runner exposes (the `[run]` lines of `backends/wasm/run_harness.py`, the `PASS`/`FAIL` lines of `backends/wasm/lifecycle_harness.py`, the runtime's own log, which neither harness prints, and the record-mode durable WAL), only the WAL reads module memory into text. On the live runtime a leaf DID reach it: a witnessed extern that takes `v: Secret[Str]` and hands it back as a plain `Result[Str, E]` witness wrote a `Secret[List[Str]]` element into the WAL file verbatim, and the `Secret[Str]` scalar control too. The other doors carried nothing: a config field is refused for every shape, a provide-method parameter's witness is parked in the runtime accumulator and never framed, and `Secret[Map[Str, Str]]` does not lower on this tier at all. The fix is the fail-closed one: `_refuse_unscrubbed_secret_referent` (`backends/wasm/emit.py`) refuses that shape by name wherever an activation body registers it, in every emit mode, and a witness declared `Secret[...]` still emits and frames the placeholder. A scrub was not chosen because the only scrub this tier can do is that emit-time placeholder, and forcing it onto a witness the author did not declare confidential would make the descriptor unreplayable without saying so. Regression: `backends/wasm/test_secret_leaf_sinks_1577.py`, which runs every door and shape on the live runtime and pins the sink inventory.
    **F6(j), the rust leaf walk's RECURSION -- F6(i)'s walk recursed the DECLARED type and revl admits recursive datatypes, so a cyclic record had no base case. ✅ FIXED 2026-09-11 (`fix/rust-container-secret-faces`, the same PR as F6(i)).** `_secret_face_lines` walks the declared type, and its `kind == "record"` branch recursed on each field's declared type with no visited set and no depth bound, so the walk has no base case on a cycle -- and revl admits recursive datatypes: `_recursive_boxed_edges` (`backends/rust/emit.py:7365`) exists precisely to `Box`-indirect record and enum cycles so the artifact is finite-sized and the drop check terminates, and its docstring names the self-host AST `Expr` (a case carrying a per-case struct that again holds `Expr` fields) as the shape it is for. **The front end ACCEPTS such a program and the emitter then died on it**, which is a build defect on legal source rather than a disclosure: nothing leaks, because nothing is emitted, so the class and severity are F6(g)'s and not F6(i)'s. Proved at the front door -- with the cap disabled `emit` raises `RecursionError` on a composition declaring `type Node = { val: Str, next: Opt[Node] }`, and that same program passes the front end without a diagnostic, so the author meets an emitter traceback rather than a refusal. **Fix:** `_SECRET_WALK_MAX_DEPTH = 8` plus a `levels` counter threaded through every recursive call, mirroring the go tier's `revlRegisterValue`, which bounds the identical shape at runtime with `depth > 8`. `levels` is deliberately separate from `depth`, which stays the indentation and leaf-name counter, so every non-recursive shape emits byte-for-byte what F6(i) emitted and every golden and the self-host mirror are untouched. Past the cap the container face is the whole coverage for that subtree, the same bound an undecomposable shape already leaves -- a purely cyclic pair with no scalar field anywhere therefore registers nothing beyond the container face, which is correct because there is no leaf to register. **Regression:** `test_the_leaf_walk_terminates_on_a_recursive_record` (the helper, runs everywhere: it terminates, stays bounded, and still registers the non-recursive leaf) and `test_a_recursive_record_composition_emits` (the front door). Non-vacuity: with the cap disabled the first raises `RecursionError` and the second kills `emit` after the front end accepted the program. **Ledger:** the two cap statements are unreachable by the rust corpus -- no corpus document declares a recursive record, and adding one would flip the byte-agreement oracle red for the same item-429(d) reason F6(i) records -- so `_secret_face_lines` is re-recorded 35 to 37 uncovered statements under the existing item 421 F6 rust reason, with an F6(j) sentence added to that reason.
    **F6(k), `Opt` is not indirection -- the boxing pass treats every generic wrapper as already indirect, which is right for `List` and `Map` and wrong for `Opt`. ◑ FOUND 2026-09-11, NOT FIXED, recorded so the shape is on file.** `_recursive_boxed_edges` (`backends/rust/emit.py:7365`) builds its adjacency from `types` and counts an edge as a cycle edge only when the field's or case's declared type is a KEY of `types`; a generic wrapper (`List[..]`, `Opt[..]`, `Map[..]`) is not a key, so it is assumed to break the cycle already. That assumption is correct for `Vec<T>` and `HashMap<K,V>`, which are heap-backed, and WRONG for `Option<T>`, which stores its payload INLINE. `type Node = { val: Str, next: Opt[Node] }` is therefore an infinite-size type that the pass never boxes. **Evidence, a real toolchain:** the emitted crate fails to build with `RC 101`, `error[E0072]` and `error[E0391]: cycle detected when computing layout of 'Node' ... which requires computing layout of 'core::option::Option<Node>' ... which again requires computing layout of 'Node', completing the cycle`. **Pre-existing, and not caused by F6(i) or F6(j):** the identical probe on the tree without those changes fails the same way, `RC 101` with `E0072`, `E0277` and `E0391` -- one error MORE, because that tree still carries F6(g)'s `Display` bound on the doors. **Why it is recorded rather than fixed here:** the front end accepts the program and the failure lands on the author at `cargo build`, which is the class F6(g) fixed on the doors, but this fix belongs in the boxing pass rather than at a door: `Opt[<type in types>]` has to become a cycle edge requiring `Box` exactly as a direct record field does, while `List` and `Map` stay non-edges. That moves the emitted layout of every `Opt`-reached recursive type and needs its own goldens and its own self-host pass, so it is out of scope for a door-level fix. It also bounds F6(j): the `Opt`-reached recursive shape can never reach the walk, because its artifact does not build in the first place. Filed as issue #919, which carries the repro and the boxing-pass scope.
    **F6(l) LOW, ✅ FIXED 2026-09-11 (`fix/py-confidential-walk-cycle`): the py tier's three value walks recursed without a visited set.** `confidential.register_secret_tree`, `confidential.redact_value` and `confidential._needles` each descend a declared value's container graph, and each descended unconditionally: a value whose container graph re-enters itself drove the walk until the interpreter's recursion limit. A `@py` body is verbatim python, so `a = ["leaf"]; a.append(a); return a` is enough, and a host object carrying a back-reference (a parent pointer, a self-referential record) does it with no author intent at all; the front end admits `Secret[List[Str]]`, and the tier refuses cyclic *modules* (the import-cycle and G3 dependency-cycle checks) while admitting every cyclic *value*. Four doors reach a walk, and each one fails closed. `runtime.secret_result` -- the `@_revl_secret_result` wrapper on a `Secret[T]`-returning extern -- raises `RecursionError` out of the call. `runtime.mark_secret`, emitted at the head of every provide method whose declared parameter is stamped `secret`, calls `register_secret_tree` outside any `try`, so the method aborts before its body and registers nothing. `bridge.seam_failure` renders the crossing through `redact_call_text`, and because the raise sits inside the `return {"ok": False, "error": ...}` expression it escapes `_invoke`'s `except Exception`, so the structured seam-failure reply is lost rather than delivered. And `replay`'s `record_emission` renders an undeclared argument through `redact_args`, so the emission is never recorded and the crossing never fires. All four abort *before* the value crosses, so the defect is availability and diagnosability rather than disclosure: nothing unredacted is written or sent, and `redact_text` -- the WAL renderer -- sees only registered leaf faces, which a cyclic graph still reaches. The fix is a PATH, not a depth. `_entered(value, path)` keys a per-walk `set` of `id()`s and each container branch returns early on a back-edge, because a depth cap terminates the walk but also drops the leaves of any legal value deeper than the cap, a confidentiality regression in exactly the walks that exist to prevent one. `redact_value` returns a `RECURSIVE` placeholder at the back-edge and discards its own id once the node is done, so a DAG shared by two siblings still renders in full at both; it deliberately does not fall back to `repr(value)`, which would carry a container's leaves verbatim. Red state proven by neutering `_entered`; `tests/test_redaction_cycle_guard.py` (9 tests) pins termination, the `RECURSIVE` rendering, and leaf retention on the sibling DAG. The same walk on the ts and java tiers is F6(m).
    **F6(m) LOW, ✅ FIXED 2026-09-11 (`fix/py-confidential-walk-cycle`): the same unbounded registration walk on the ts and java tiers.** F6(l) is one defect with three spellings, and the survey that closed it is recorded here so the tiers stay comparable. `typescript/runtime.ts`'s `rememberSecret` descended `Array` elements and `Object.values` with no bound, so a cyclic value reaching `markSecret` or `secretResult` threw `RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded` before the crossing; `java/emit.py`'s `revlRememberSecret` walked `Optional`, `Collection`, `Map.values()` and `Record` components with no bound and threw `StackOverflowError`, which is the more interesting shape of the two because a `StackOverflowError` is an `Error` and nothing on the java side converted one: `java/emit.py` emits no `catch (Exception)` and no `catch (Throwable)` at all, its only `Error`-aware brackets are the two compensation guards (`catch (RuntimeException | Error err)` and `catch (RuntimeException | Error failure)`), and the funnel that does exist -- the placement runner's `catch (Exception ex)` -- is an `Exception` catch that an `Error` does not match, so the fault left the generated code as an `Error`. Both now thread an identity `seen` set through the walk and return early on a back-edge, the same path-not-depth rule as py and for the same reason. Identity rather than equality is required rather than stylistic: a java `Record` and a ts object graph can hold two equal-but-distinct nodes that must both render, and hashing a container to test membership is what the walk exists to avoid. The rust and go tiers bound their own registration walks by depth (`_SECRET_WALK_MAX_DEPTH = 8` and `depth > 8` respectively), the shape F6(j) settled on for rust, so this entry covers the two tiers that had no bound at all. A depth bound is not a substitute for this rule, though: it is the same confidentiality regression in miniature, because it drops the leaves of any legal value deeper than the cap, and on go it was also the walk's only termination, so a self-referential container was a `fatal error: stack overflow`. Go's walk is bounded by path here, in F6(n).
    **F6(n) LOW, ✅ FIXED 2026-09-11 (`fix/go-secret-walk-path`): the go half of F6(l)/F6(m), and the counterexample to F6(m)'s closing sentence.** F6(m) exempted the go and rust tiers on the grounds that they "bound their own registration walks by depth" (`depth > 8`, `_SECRET_WALK_MAX_DEPTH = 8`). A depth bound is not a bound of the kind this walk needs: it does terminate the recursion, but it also stops REGISTERING, so the leaves of any legal value deeper than the cap are never registered as their own text and cross verbatim in every sink `revlRedactText` covers. `revlRegisterValue` (`backends/go/emit.py`) walked Interface/Pointer/Slice/Array/Map/Struct with `depth+1`, returned at `depth > 8`, and rendered `fmt.Sprintf("%v", rv.Interface())` at every level it passed. A `map[string]any` spends TWO frames per level -- the map, then the `interface{}` holding the next one -- so the cap was reached at four map levels, not eight. **Evidence, end to end against the real emitted registry:** a `Secret[Map[Str, Any]]` service parameter emits `revlMarkSecret(payload)`, so the marked value is an ordinary author-reachable argument; nested 1/2/3 maps deep its leaf was redacted, and nested 4 and deeper it printed bare (`leaf=deep-canary-9999`) in the probe channel, the seam wire and the runner console alike, the registered needle count saturating at 15. **Second arm, availability:** because `depth > 8` was the walk's only termination, a self-referential container reached `fmt.Sprintf("%v", ...)` and died with `fatal error: stack overflow` in `fmt.(*pp).printValue` -- and Go's stack overflow is not a panic, so `recover()` never runs and the process is lost. `@go` bodies are verbatim Go and admitted, and `m := map[string]any{}; m["self"] = m` is a legal one, so the cycle is reachable from admitted source; a recursive DECLARED type is not, since `Opt[Node]` emits the non-pointer `RevlOpt[Node]` and the crate does not compile -- the go analogue of F6(k), which is what ruled that route out. **Fix:** `revlRegisterValue(rv, seen map[revlWalkKey]bool) bool` is bounded by the PATH it is on rather than by a depth, the rule F6(l) settled: each container marks `revlWalkKey{ptr, typ}` with `defer delete(seen, key)` -- path membership rather than a global visited set, so a DAG still renders at both of its siblings -- and returns `true` on a back-edge. `revlRememberSecret` now branches on `revlIsContainer` FIRST and never calls `fmt.Sprintf("%v", ...)` on a container at all, and the cyclic answer is propagated to ancestors so none of them renders the subtree that could not be rendered. Needles now grow 7/10/13/16/19/22/28/40 for 1/2/3/4/5/6/8/12 maps, so no leaf is dropped, and a cyclic map prints its sentinel instead of crashing. Four tests in `backends/go/test_secret_registry_go.py` pin the emitted shape, the depth sweep, the cycle, and non-vacuity (a path-length cap standing in for the depth cap makes the leaf leak again). **Rust is the remaining half:** `_SECRET_WALK_MAX_DEPTH = 8` is the same shape and is NOT addressed here.
    **F6(o), the rust leaf walk's DEPTH bound -- F6(i)'s walk stopped at `levels > 8` and returned `[]`, so the leaves of any declared shape nested deeper than the bound were never registered as their own text and crossed verbatim in every sink `revl_redact_text` covers. ✅ FIXED 2026-09-11 (`fix/rust-secret-walk-path`).** The rule this breaks is F6(l)/F6(m)'s, the one the python tier's fix settled: a registration walk must be bounded by the PATH of types it is on and never by a depth, because a depth bound is a confidentiality regression in exactly the walk that exists to prevent one -- it terminates the recursion, but it also stops REGISTERING. F6(m) exempted rust and go as tiers that "bound their own registration walks by depth"; that exemption was wrong for both, and F6(n) closes the go half. Here the same `if levels > _SECRET_WALK_MAX_DEPTH: return []` that gave F6(j) its base case WAS the walk's confidentiality bound, so the fix for the availability defect was itself the disclosure. **Reachability, at the front door:** a `Secret[Map[Str, ...]]` extern nested nine declared levels deep is ADMITTED and emits with ZERO `revl_mark_secret` -- eight levels emits one -- so the author gets no diagnostic and the leaf is registered nowhere. **Evidence, a real toolchain:** an emitted crate whose function returns a nine-level (and a twelve-level) declared map leaves the bottom leaf unredacted, `revl_redact_text` returning the canary verbatim and the cargo test exiting `RC 101`; at one and eight levels the same crate redacts. **Fix:** `levels` becomes `path: frozenset[str]` of declared type NAMES, with `if declared in path: return []` and `path = path | {declared}`, and all three recursive call sites thread `path` instead of `levels + 1`; `_SECRET_WALK_MAX_DEPTH` is deleted. A path set is what F6(j) actually needed -- a declared type that re-enters itself is the only shape with no base case -- and it leaves every legal shape's leaves registered at any depth. `path` is deliberately PER-BRANCH and not a shared visited set: two sibling fields of the same declared type are two distinct values and both must register, which a global set would silently drop while still terminating. `depth` is retained unchanged as the indentation and leaf-name counter, so the emitted output for every non-recursive shape is byte-identical and no golden and no self-host mirror moves. **Regression:** `test_the_walk_reaches_the_leaf_at_any_declared_depth` (the emitted shape, parametrized 1/8/9/12, runs everywhere: the walk registers the innermost leaf and exactly one registration, whatever the depth), `test_a_leaf_past_the_old_bound_is_still_scrubbed` (the `#[needs_cargo]` build-and-scrub harness at 9 and 12 levels, the arm that was RED), `test_two_sibling_fields_of_one_type_are_both_walked` (guards against a global visited set replacing the path), and the F6(j) pair reworded to the path rule, which still pins termination. Non-vacuity: `test_with_a_bound_on_how_deep_the_walk_goes_the_leaf_leaks` re-wraps the walk with the old `levels > 8` rule and shows the innermost registration disappear, and with the emitter reverted the 9- and 12-level arms of both new tests fail while the 1- and 8-level arms pass (4 failed, 5 passed). **Ledger:** one statement arrives with the path check, so `_secret_face_lines` is re-recorded 37 to 38 uncovered statements under the existing item 421 F6 rust reason, with an F6(o) sentence added to that reason.
    **F6(p), the rust leaf walk's VARIANT cases -- F6(i)'s walk decomposed the four generic heads and `record` and stopped there, so a `Secret[Variant]` registered the container's own `Debug` face and NOTHING inside it. ✅ FIXED 2026-09-11 (`fix/rust-secret-walk-path`, the same PR as F6(o)).** This is F6(i)'s own gap on the one declared shape F6(i) left out, and F6(i)'s argument carries over word for word: `revl_redact_text` matches an EXACT needle, so the container face covers a trace that prints the WHOLE value, while the plainest thing an author writes with an ADT is ONE CASE'S PAYLOAD -- `match step { Step::Final(v) => sink(v) }` -- which is a different string and crossed every funnelled sink verbatim: the host trace, the WAL record, F5's seam failure reply. **Rust was the outlier of the three tiers, again:** go reaches the payload through the struct a variant emits (`StepFinal{Value: ..}`, so `revlRegisterValue`'s `reflect.Struct` arm descends into it) and py reaches it through `_members`'s `__slots__` probe, which is exactly the shape an emitted ADT case class has; rust has no reflection and the emit-time walk had no case arm, so its registry stopped at the container's own face. F6(g) had already named "the emitted records and ADT variants" together, in the one context where it considered them -- the `Display` bound -- and F6(i) ported the fix to records only. **Reachability, at the front door:** `type Step = Final(Str) | Retry` with `extern pure fn step() -> Secret[Step]` is ADMITTED and emits with ZERO `revl_mark_secret`; the door falls back to `revl_secret_result_encoded`, which registers `format!("{:?}", _revl_v)` alone. **Evidence, a real toolchain:** an emitted crate whose function returns that `Secret[Step]` redacts `format!("{:?}", s)` and hands back the canary VERBATIM from `revl_redact_text(v)` for the `v` the author matched out of it, `RC 101`; with the arm in place the same crate redacts the payload. **Fix:** a `kind == "variant"` branch beside the `record` one in `_secret_face_lines`, emitting `match &{expr} { <Enum>::<Case>(_revl_leaf<depth>) => { <walk of the payload> } ... _ => {} }`. Three details are load-bearing. The scrutinee is `&{expr}` and not `{expr}`: the origin door binds `let _revl_v = ...` and RETURNS it, so a `match` on the owned value would move it. The arm's case name goes through `_ident(.., "case name")` to match the DEFINITION, which mangles it. And the `_` arm is not decoration: it keeps the match exhaustive over the nullary cases and over the re-entrant payloads `path` cuts, and a case whose payload walk is empty is skipped rather than emitted as an empty arm. A payload that is itself a container is walked through the same arms, so `Wrap(List[Str])` registers its element and not the vector. **Regression:** `test_the_walk_decomposes_a_variant_by_its_cases` (the emitted shape, runs everywhere: the `match`, the case arm, the nested `List` payload's own loop, and the catch-all), `test_the_variant_arm_is_taken_through_a_record_field_too` (`match &_revl_v.step`, the ADT-typed member of a record), and `test_a_variant_case_payload_is_still_scrubbed` (the `#[needs_cargo]` build-and-scrub harness, the arm that was RED). Non-vacuity: `test_a_variant_with_no_payload_has_no_leaf_to_register` pins both directions -- a nullary-only variant returns `[]` and leaves the container face as the whole coverage, so an arm-per-case walk that registers nothing cannot pass, and a payloaded one returns the exact arm. With the emitter reverted all four fail (4 failed, 27 deselected) while the crate still builds, which is what makes this a disclosure and not a build defect. **Ledger:** the branch adds statements to `_secret_face_lines`, so the item 421 F6 rust reason gains an F6(p) sentence and the count moves with it.
    **F6(q), the java tier's DECLARED shapes -- F6(h) recorded java as "FIXED ... descends into `List`, `Map`, `Record`, ADT variants", and that sentence describes the ENCODER (`BridgeCodec.encode`), not the registry. The registry descended through `java.lang.reflect.Record` alone, and this tier declares every user record as `public static final class` and every variant case as `final class {Case} implements {Name}` inside a `public sealed interface` -- neither is a java `record`, and no `toString` is emitted either. ✅ FIXED 2026-09-11 (`fix/java-secret-walk-shape`).** The predicate was false for every declared shape, so `revlRememberSecret` fell through to `revlRememberText(String.valueOf(value))` and registered the identity string `Name@hash` -- a needle no sink this tier writes -- while a `Secret[Str]` beside it redacted. Only the built-in `Result` is a real java record (`record Ok<T, E>(T value) implements RevlResult<T, E> {}`), which is why the `instanceof Record` branch was live and why F6(h)'s own probe never saw this: it isolates the defect to the predicate, not the branch. This is F6(h)'s `[B@hash` finding one level up, and F6(i)'s rule again: the container's own face is not the leak, its LEAVES are, because `revlRedactText` matches an EXACT needle and `match step { Final(v) => sink(v) }` writes a different string. **Reachability, at both doors:** `type Payload = { key: Str }` with `extern emission fn mint(u: Str) -> Secret[Payload]` is ADMITTED and emits `revlSecretResult(_revl_secret_mint_record(u))`; a service method parameter declared `Secret[Payload]` emits `revlMarkSecret(rec);`. Both registered a face nothing matches. **Evidence, a real JDK:** an emitted unit marked with a record, a variant payload, a nullary case and a record one container level down hands all four leaves back VERBATIM from `revlRedactText`, and the same unit crosses the provider->consumer seam with the three leaves in the seam-failure reply and both consoles; with the fix every one redacts, and an unmarked value beside them survives, so the harness is not vacuous. **Fix:** the generated shapes declare their members instead of being rediscovered -- a `RevlSecretShape` interface carrying `java.util.List<Object> revlSecretMembers()`, emitted into the registry preamble, implemented by every declared record class and every variant case (a payload case lists `this.value`, a nullary case lists nothing), with a walk branch `if (value instanceof RevlSecretShape shape)` placed BEFORE the `instanceof Record` branch. Decomposing the shape is what gives the walk a needle per LEAF, and because the branch recurses through `revlRememberSecret` it reaches a shape nested in a `List`, an `Optional` or a `Map` with no extra code. Emitting a `toString` instead would NOT have closed this: it registers the container's own face, which the fallback already covered, and leaves the leaves where they were. The `_SECRET_MODE` guard on both the `implements` clause and the members method is what keeps a secretless document byte-identical, and it keeps the interface itself out of a document that has no registry to call into. **Regression:** `test_the_emitter_declares_every_generated_shape_to_the_walk` (the guard, the marker on the record and on both cases including the nullary one, three members methods, and the `instanceof Record` branch still beside it), `test_no_sink_carries_a_record_or_variant_secret_across_the_seam` (a live Keeper/Portal seam run on the stub runtime: three canaries absent, the sentence around them marked and still readable, no over-redaction), `test_the_registry_covers_a_declared_shape_at_every_nesting` (record, variant, list-of-record, variant-list, optional-of-record, marked through the same entry point the doors use), `test_a_secretless_document_carries_no_shape_machinery` (the partition), and the non-vacuity pair `test_with_the_shape_walk_stripped_the_leaves_leak` / `test_with_the_shape_walk_stripped_only_the_shapes_leak`, which short-circuit the guard and show the leaves print. `java-record` is a positive control in both probe tests: `Bag` is a real java `record`, so it goes through the reflective branch and must stay redacted with the shape branch stripped -- that pins the new branch as an EXTENSION of the walk and not a replacement for it. With the emitter reverted, 4 of the 5 new tests fail (the absence-only one passes by design) and the seam reply carries all three canaries while `for <redacted:arg>` beside them stays redacted. **Ledger:** `_emit_v3_secret_members` (6 statements) and the `_SECRET_MODE` branches of `_emit_v3_types` (3) are recorded as uncovered, because no java corpus document declares a `Secret[T]` and `selfhost/emit_java.rvl` has no secret-redaction port (item-429(d)). **CORRECTION, same PR: the shape branch took no cycle bound, and that reintroduced F6(m) one branch away.** The first cut called `revlRememberSecret(member)` and did not `seen.add(value)`, so every shape restarted the walk with a FRESH set. `type Chain = { key: Str, kids: List[Chain] }` is heap-backed, so a self-referential value is legal and it builds; `revlMarkSecret(first)` where `first.kids` holds `second` and `second.kids` holds `first` raised `StackOverflowError` BEFORE any crossing -- and a `StackOverflowError` is an `Error`, not an `Exception`, so nothing on this tier converted one and the walk registered NOTHING. That is the same defect, the same fault type and the same consequence F6(m) recorded for the `Optional`/`Collection`/`Map`/`Record` branches this one sits beside, and F6(j) for rust. The branch now takes the bound the others take: `if (!seen.add(value)) { return; }` and `revlRememberSecret(member, seen)`. **Proved on a real JVM:** the probe prints `MARKED OK` and `val=<redacted:secret>` with the bound, and `THREW java.lang.StackOverflowError` without it. `test_a_declared_shape_that_reenters_itself_is_bounded_by_path` asserts BOTH keys of a two-node cycle are held -- bounded by PATH, the second is reached *through* the first -- and `_run_shapes` asserts the JVM exit code, so the overflow is itself a failure. That test rides its own probe main rather than joining the nesting probe: with the shape branch short-circuited the walk falls to `String.valueOf(value)`, whose identity string calls the GENERATED `hashCode()`, and `Chain.hashCode()` recurses `kids` through `revlHash` with no cycle bound, so the stripped run dies in the equality machinery instead of printing a leak. **That is recorded here as a pre-existing, shape-independent property of the generated equality surface (`revlEq`/`revlHash`, `emit.py:1491`/`:1503`), not as part of this fix:** an `a == b` on a self-referential declared record reaches `revlEq` -> `equals` -> `revlEq` on the cyclic `List` with no visited set either, and it is not secret-specific. The `Chain` declaration is deliberately never referenced by the document: the walk has to be handed a member list by every generated shape, reachable or not.
    **F6(r) LOW, ✅ FIXED 2026-09-11 (`fix/ts-secret-walk-map`): the ts tier's `Map`, and the one container its registry walk could not see at all.** F6(l) bounded `runtime.rememberSecret` by path and F6(f) taught it the byte faces, and the walk that came out of both dispatches on `ArrayBuffer.isView`, then `Array.isArray`, then `Object.values(value)`. A revl `Map` is this tier's built-in JS `Map` — `emit.py` lowers `Map.new()` to `new Map()` and renders the type as `Map<K, V>` — and **a `Map`'s entries are not OWN enumerable properties**, so `Object.values(map)` is `[]` and the walk registered nothing at all. The receiver marking is real, which is what makes the gap reachable rather than theoretical: `emit.py:1492` writes `host.markSecret(token)` at the head of every provide method whose service declares a `Secret[T]` parameter, so a `Secret[Map[Str, Str]]` parameter is an ordinary author-reachable argument. **Evidence, against the real emitted unit:** a `Secret[Map[Str, Str]]` receiver emits `store(token: Map<string, string>) { host.markSecret(token) … }`, and `markSecret(new Map([['token', CANARY]]))` left `CANARY` bare in `redactText` — through `Map.get`, through `JSON.stringify(map)` (`{"token":"…"}`) and through the `Map(1) { 'token' => '…' }` rendering alike, while the list, variant-payload and record-field controls beside it all redacted. **Why this tier alone:** py's `register_secret_tree` handles `dict` via `value.values()`, java's `revlRememberSecret` has had a `java.util.Map` branch (`record.values()`) since F6(h), and go's `revlRegisterValue` walks `reflect.Map` — so ts was the only tier whose registry could not reach a map's values. **Fix:** a `value instanceof Map` branch that walks `value.values()` and returns, placed after the cycle guard so it takes the same path bound every other container branch takes. **Values only, and for the reason the record walk states:** a map's KEYS are field names the author wrote, and registering them would redact the field name out of every later trace — the same rule py's `dict` arm and java's `Map` arm follow. No container face is registered because a `Map` has none worth having (`String(map)` is `[object Map]` and `JSON.stringify(map)` is `{}`); the registered values are what the renderings that matter are built from, so the `Map(n) { 'k' => 'v' }` form is covered by the value needle rather than by a face of its own. **Scope note:** `Map` is the only such container on this tier — revl declares `List`, `Opt`, `Map`, `Result` and `Async`, and only `Map` is a JS `Map` (`Result` is the tagged plain object `{ kind, value }`, which `Object.values` already reaches). Four toolchain-free checks in `backends/typescript/test_secret_map_receiver.py` pin the emitted shape, the marking site, and non-vacuity (an unmarked method and a secretless document carry no marking), and six runtime assertions in `backends/typescript/tests/secret_map.test.ts` pin the walk: a map value, every value rather than the first, a map nested inside a list and a record and another map, a self-referential map, and the two controls — the keys survive and an unmarked value still crosses. Red before the fix: 4 of the 6 fail with the canary bare. **Lettering:** F6(q) is the java half of this same family (`fix/java-secret-walk-shape`, PR #925), so the row above this one is `p` and the gap at `q` is deliberate rather than a lost record.
    **F7 MEDIUM, ✅ FIXED 2026-09-02** (`fix/421-f7-temporal-residue`). A compensation activity's error is now redacted before it reaches any of the three sinks, and the redaction is EMITTED INTO THE GENERATED CODE (not applied python-side at emit time), since `emit_temporal.py` renders TypeScript source strings rather than running a shared runtime file. Each `saga.push` site (the `emit`-compensate write-ahead and both `effect`/`let-effect`-undo forms) now also pushes `args: () => [...]`, a LAZY thunk over the runtime values (`name`/`var`/`config`/`field` leaves) the compensation call touches; lazy matters for the write-ahead referent-seeded form (`undo r_close(h)`), whose `h` is still `undefined` at the moment the push statement runs and is only assigned after the guarded acquire lands, so capturing it eagerly would have frozen it at `undefined` and redacted nothing. The catch site (`backends/typescript/emit_temporal.py`, was `:486-487`) now reads `error: redactResidueError(e, step.args())`. `redactResidueError`/`argNeedles`/`REDACTED_ARG` are emitted once in the module preamble, a direct mirror of `backends/typescript/bridge.ts`'s `seamFailure`/`REDACTED_ARG` from F5 (same marker `<redacted:arg>`, same longest-needle-first substring scrub, same "exception TYPE and surrounding sentence survive, only the caller's own bytes are gone" shape), reused rather than a second scheme. Verified end to end (not just the static shape) with a generated module run for real against a stub Temporal SDK and a host `r_close` that echoes its argument into a thrown Error's message: the canary is absent from and the `<redacted:arg>` marker present in all three sinks, `recordResidue`'s argument, `ApplicationFailure.details` **specifically confirmed** (the sink that PERSISTS IN TEMPORAL HISTORY for the namespace retention period), and the residue query. An operator reading any of the three sinks afterward still sees the compensation's NAME, its residue `kind`, and the failing error's TYPE and surrounding sentence (`Error: r_close failed for handle <redacted:arg>`) — enough to act on — with only the argument value itself gone. Bounded exactly as F5/F6: a value under 3 characters is left unmatched, and host text that reformats an argument (truncation, case fold) is not caught, since the match is exact. Regression: `backends/typescript/test_temporal_target.py::test_residue_error_static_shape_no_longer_bare_string_of_e` and `::test_residue_error_redacts_host_text_from_all_three_sinks_end_to_end` (shells out to `node`, skips cleanly if absent). Golden `backends/typescript/golden/temporal_booktrip.ts` regenerated: the `SagaStep` type gained `args: () => unknown[]`, the preamble gained the redaction helpers, both `saga.push` sites gained `args: () => []` (empty — that fixture's compensate args are literal strings, not bound variables, so nothing to redact), and the catch line's `error:` field changed. **Self-host check (item 429), done DIRECTLY on the source rather than through the oracle:** `selfhost/emit_ts.rvl` has no Temporal-rendering mode at all — it emits only the cordis-v4 target, the same one `backends/typescript/emit.py`'s default (non-`temporal`) path emits — so there is no mirrored `saga`/`recordResidue`/`ApplicationFailure` logic anywhere in `selfhost/` to diverge or to port; recorded as a deliberate no-op, not an oversight. Original finding: `backends/typescript/emit_temporal.py:486-487` `error: String(e)` copied unbounded host error text into three sinks (`recordResidue`, `ApplicationFailure.details`, and the residue query). `ApplicationFailure.details` persists in Temporal history for the namespace retention period and the query is readable by anyone with namespace query permission. Aggravating: `Secret[Str]` erases to `string` in `RevlActivities`, so the implementer gets no warning (pre-emitter, target-independent).
    **F8 MEDIUM, ◑ WIRED FOR LOCAL UDS SEAMS 2026-09-02; NETWORK SEAM PEER ADMISSION 2026-09-02** (`9137843d`, `fix/421-f8-correlation-guard-wiring`; `fix/correlation-guard-network-seams`). `placement.py` now mints a per-process peer identity (the declared `[tls] identity` where the process has one, else its process name), a fresh 32-byte per-process secret, and a `composition_id` (`attest.canonical_hash(ir)`); each local consumer's proxy entry carries `{composition_id, peer_identity, secret}` and each provider's serve entry a `{composition_id, peers}` table built from exactly the consumers that reach it. `_process_runner.py` builds the `CorrelationGuard` from that table and passes `correlation=` to `bridge.serve`; the consumer seals a fresh `Correlation` per call with a fresh `idempotency_key`, so a captured-and-replayed request collides with the one already admitted. The transport already supported all of this and was simply never called with it. **The regression test is the load-bearing part:** `tests/test_deploy_118.py::test_the_ordinary_run_placement_path_leaves_a_live_correlation_guard` boots through the real `run_placement()` entry point, hand-constructs NO guard, then opens a raw Unix socket and sends a bare pre-118-shaped request, asserting the dispatch is REFUSED (`REJECT_MALFORMED`) rather than merely that a guard object exists. A test that hand-built a guard would have reproduced exactly the gap that let this ship dead. **NETWORK (TCP+mTLS) SEAMS: the honest answer, not the same guard.** The correlation guard cannot be lifted onto a network seam, and the reason is structural rather than an omission: its secret is minted per boot by ONE conductor and delivered only inside its own children's specs, so an item-151 cross-composition consumer (live in `tests/test_two_composition_gate.py::test_provider_placement_boots_standalone`) can never hold one. Demanding a sealed envelope there refuses the legitimate caller rather than the stranger, which is regression (i) below in a new costume; distributing the secret is Slice 2's replicated control plane. **The dedup ledger, however, does NOT go with the secret — that was wrong and is now fixed (2026-09-03).** Dedup needs a key naming one crossing and an identity to scope it by; the HMAC exists to bind a CLAIMED identity to a real caller, which is load-bearing on UDS only because `bridge.peer_identity` is None there. On TCP+mTLS the handshake already bound the identity with a CA-signed key, so `deploy.TransportReplayGuard` keys freshness on `(TRANSPORT identity, composition_id, generation, idempotency_key)` — the first member read off the peer certificate, never off the payload a replayer controls — and needs no shared secret and therefore no distribution channel. It is installed by `placement.py` on EVERY network seam and refuses no legitimate caller: a network consumer (including an item-151 one under another conductor) stamps an UNSEALED envelope from its own certificate, and a crossing declaring no `idempotency_key` is dispatched exactly as before (item 309: nothing declares it re-deliverable). The ledger is BOUNDED (`deploy.BoundedReplayLedger`: 1024 keyed crossings per identity x 64 identities, LRU on both, evictions counted), because a provider answers a peer for weeks; past the window the oldest scope is forgotten and that crossing's replay would be admitted again, which is stated rather than hidden — refusing everything once full would be a self-service DoS, not fail-closed. What mTLS DOES prove, per session and with a CA-signed key, is which identity is calling; what was missing is a closed set to check it against. So a network provider now takes a DECLARED `[processes.<p>] peers` allowlist, enforced in `bridge.serve(peers=)` once per connection against the certificate identity, before a request is read (`deploy.PeerAllowlist`). It holds NAMES, not secrets, which is precisely why it holds across a composition boundary and needs nothing from the caller's bridge — a py consumer, a node consumer and a stranger composition are judged identically. Declared, never derived: a placement cannot enumerate other compositions' consumers, and a derived set would lock them out; this placement's own network consumers are unioned in so naming an external peer cannot cause a self-inflicted outage. **The achieved level is now reported per seam** (`deploy.SeamAdmission`, `bundle.verify`'s vocabulary): `sealed` (UDS, per-peer secret + replay ledger), `peer-bound` (network, allowlisted identity AND keyed crossings replay-checked over a bounded window), `peer-pinned` (allowlisted identity, NOT replay-checked) or `UNVERIFIED` with the reason, printed by the conductor and named on the runner's own `serve` line. A weaker property is never shipped under the stronger word, and a seam that cannot say who may call it says so instead of staying quiet. Tests: `tests/test_seam_admission.py` (27), including a real TCP+mTLS `bridge.serve` where a peer holding a valid certificate from the same CA is refused before dispatch while a declared peer round-trips; a captured request re-delivered on a fresh connection refused `duplicate-envelope` with the service dispatched exactly once; a cross-composition caller (foreign `composition_id`, no `auth` tag) admitted and its own replay refused; a captured envelope replayed under another certificate refused `peer-identity-mismatch`; and `test_the_ordinary_run_placement_path_leaves_a_live_replay_guard`, which boots a real network placement through `run_placement()`, builds no guard of its own, and replays a captured request against the published port. **Node consumers now seal (2026-09-03).** `typescript/bridge.ts` grew the `correlation` parameter (`makeProxy`/`sealCorrelation`), sealing the same HMAC over the same canonical envelope bytes (`JSON.stringify` with sorted keys == `attest._canonical_bytes`), and `placement_runner.ts` passes the proxy entry's block, so `_CORRELATION_SEALING_TIERS` is `{py, node}` and a py provider with a node consumer is guarded instead of disarmed. Proven end to end, not by inspection: `tests/test_ts_correlation_seal.py` spawns a real node process against a real py provider running the real guard — a node-sealed crossing is ADMITTED, an exact replay of one is refused `duplicate-envelope` (reachable only if the tag verified), and sealing under the wrong secret is refused `forged-envelope`. `ci/placement_smoke.sh`'s py<->node seam boots green with the guard installed; with the runner's `correlation` argument removed it fails `correlation refused: malformed-envelope`, which is regression (i) reproduced on demand. Original finding: The item-118 `CorrelationGuard` is NEVER WIRED by the shipped runner: `_process_runner.py:335` calls `bridge.serve(...)` with no `correlation=`, and `grep -c correlation` is 0 in both `placement.py` and `_process_runner.py`; the only call sites are in `tests/test_deploy_118.py`. `typescript/bridge.ts:419` has no such parameter at all. On a real TCP+mTLS seam a stranger peer with any CA-signed cert dispatched, and exact replays dispatched. The guard's implementation is well built; it is simply dead code in production. **TWO REGRESSIONS FROM THE FIRST WIRING, BOTH FIXED (2026-09-02).** (i) The guard was installed regardless of CONSUMER TIER, but only the python bridge SEALS a correlation envelope. A java, go, node or rust consumer therefore sent a pre-118 request and the guard correctly refused it as `malformed-envelope`, breaking every cross-tier seam: the py-java placement smoke went red in CI and py-java placements did not work at all. The first wiring was verified py-py, where both ends seal, so this was invisible to it. Fixed with `_CORRELATION_SEALING_TIERS = frozenset({"py"})` in `placement.py`: a provider is guarded only when EVERY local consumer runs on a tier that can seal, and serves unguarded otherwise. Never build a guard on an assumption that does not hold for every caller it will judge, which is the same rule the network-seam scoping already applied. (ii) `do_swap`'s successor `serve` spec was built fresh from socket/keys/methods and carried NO correlation block, so every swap successor booted UNGUARDED, including a same-tier py->py swap where the guard was valid and all consumers still qualified. A guard that is never installed refuses nobody, so this failed SILENTLY: "guarded" meant "guarded until the first swap of that provider", and `revl swap` is ordinary use. The predecessor's block is now carried onto the successor when the successor tier can run the guard. The peer table stays valid across a swap because a swap re-points EXISTING consumers and never attaches a new one, so who may call is unchanged and only where they call moves. Gating on consumer tier at spec-build time is sufficient: `[tiers]` expansion resolves before the per-process loop, item-151 remote consumers can only dial a network provider (unconditionally unguarded), and swap never adds a consumer.
    **HYPOTHESES.** H1: `lower.py:2703-2704` checks the key type AFTER qualifiers are stripped, so `Secret[Str]` is accepted as an `idempotent(key:)` parameter and the raw secret becomes the idempotency key (static half executed: the IR shows `"idempotent": true` on a `Secret[Str]` param); `Correlation.idempotency_key` is HMAC'd but not encrypted and `DedupLedger` retains it. H2: `bridge.py:697` `repoint` never calls `_require_network_contract` unlike `proxy_component`, so a repoint could carry a seam from TCP+mTLS onto plaintext UDS. H3 (EXECUTED 2026-09-11, `fix/421-f5-rust-seam-funnel`): rust tier equivalents of F5/F6 (`placement_runner/src/main.rs:85`, `components.rs:276`). CONFIRMED for the F5 half and fixed: the rust runner builds no error reply, so a `Result`'s `Err` payload rides the value channel and `_bridge_ret_ser` serialized it unredacted -- the F6 half (the registry) was already closed on this tier. H4: item 294 slice 1 is static only, `cap_order._canon_path` disclaims symlinks/case/Unicode, so `path=` is a declaration with no runtime enforcement.
    **NEGATIVE RESULTS worth keeping.** The `cap_order.py` ALGEBRA is sound and resisted attack (path canonicalization, component-wise `_leq_path`, parameter-drop widening refused, `*` fail-closed, alias normalization, closed registry); the defect is entirely the `lower.py` bridge. Temporal's compile-time emission surface is clean by exhaustion over all 585 lines. OpenAPI headers/cookies are hard-refused and capability-token INJECTION is unreachable.
