Growth had converted "dies at call 65" into "grows without bound": the 65536-call run above was 67.6 MB of live memory for a workload whose working set is one 1 KiB string, and a component instance is long-lived by design (the component CLI re-instantiates per `--invoke`, which is exactly why this has to be measured on the core module). The fix is the **per-call arena** this item proposed, with ONE correction to the shape: the mark is NOT recorded on entry. The host places the arguments through `cabi_realloc` BEFORE the call, so an entry mark leaves the argument buffer — the larger half of what a call spends — above it and unreclaimed. The floor is instead the constant `$__hp` starts at (`$__canon_arena_base`), and every canonical export rewinds to it on the way out, so the arguments go with the scratch. RE-MEASURED through the REAL canonical export (`probe_heap.py` fact 3; facts 1 and 2 replay the per-call ALLOCATION and structurally cannot see a rewind that lives in the wrapper), `echo` with a 1 KiB argument, baseline before vs after: **1040 to 8 B after 1 call, 66056 to 8 after 64, 1056776 to 8 after 1024, 67633160 to 8 after 65536**, and `memory.size` **1033 pages to 1** across the same 65536 calls. FLAT, which is what this item asked for. Fuel cost: **ZERO**, re-bisected either side — `echo` 67 at 16 B, 256 B and 4 KiB alike, `greet` 16645/17125, `ids` 136 at n=4 and 8296 at n=1024, `dbl` 11, every one of them unmoved — because wasmtime charges no fuel for a `global.get`/`global.set`. Size cost: **+29 B** core.wasm (`echo` 3669 to 3698, `greet` 3713 to 3742, `listid` 3801 to 3830). SOUNDNESS IS CHECKED, NOT ASSUMED. A rewind is only correct for a module that parks nothing in the heap across calls, and `canonical._arena_safe` reads that off the emitted module: a live-across-calls heap value has to be reachable from a module-level cell, this tier's only such cell is a global, immutable globals are const-initialised (the return area and the literal segments, both BELOW the floor), i64 globals hold `Int` values rather than addresses, and a mutable i32 global cannot be holding an address if every write to it stores an `i32.const`. The activation cursors (`$__step`, `$__dstep`) and the commit flag pass that test; a module-level binding of a compound and the witnessed-accumulator head `$__mw_head` do not, and those modules keep the old never-reclaim heap rather than getting a wrong one. A `(start ...)` function is refused for the same reason. ORDERING: the rewind is a POINTER move and never a write, and it is the last statement before the wrapper's tail, so the result the host has yet to lift is still in memory; the first byte that can overwrite it belongs to the NEXT call, which a single-threaded instance cannot start before the host has finished lifting this one. Regression: `backends/wasm/test_heap_reclaim_432e.py` (12 tests: four on the `_arena_safe` verdict including both refusals, four on the emitted shape, four executed — the flat `$__hp`, the one-page high-water mark over 65536 calls, a 200000-byte single call still growing and still answering, and repeated round-trips at five lengths, since a rewind hands the same addresses out call after call and a stale read would surface as a WRONG ANSWER rather than a trap). Goldens: the three that carry the boundary (`canonical_echoer`, `canonical_aggregates`, `canonical_service` `.core.wat`); `functions.wat` did not move, the change being entirely in `canonical.py`. SELF-HOST: no port — `cabi_realloc`, `__canon_*` and `$__canon_arena_base` occur ZERO times anywhere under `selfhost/`. Regression: `backends/wasm/test_heap_growth_432e.py` (7 tests: both re-bisected proofs plus both refusal paths). Self-host: ported, `selfhost/emit_wasm.rvl` `helper_preamble()` embeds the preamble verbatim and now carries the identical bytes (checked directly against `_helper_funcs()`, 20365 B either side, not only through the oracle). Goldens moved by the same 34-added/6-removed hunk in all four (`functions.wat`, `canonical_echoer/aggregates/service.core.wat`): the two new functions ahead of `$alloc`, and `$alloc`'s body. `Beacon.wat`, `Auditor.wat` and `Pulse.wat` regenerated byte-identical (they carry no memory preamble). ORIGINAL FINDING: **the emitted heap is one fixed 64 KiB page that never grows and never frees, so a long-lived instance dies.** `emit.py:1938` and `emit.py:5138` emit `(memory (export "memory") 1)`, `$alloc` (`emit.py:2773`) only bumps `$__hp`, and neither it nor the exported `cabi_realloc` ever calls `memory.grow` (`grep -c memory.grow` over `emit.py` and `canonical.py` is 0). Two executed proofs in `bench/codegen/wasm/probe_heap.py`: a single `echo` call with a **40000-byte** `Str` traps with `memory fault at wasm address 0x10000 in linear memory of size 0x10000`, the host's own write of the argument already being out of bounds; and replaying the per-call allocation on the core module, **63 calls with a 1 KiB string exhaust the instance** (32 calls leave the heap at 33280 of 65536 B, 64 traps). This is a correctness cliff as much as a perf one, and it is the reason it is ranked here rather than last. FIX: `memory.grow` in `$alloc` and in `cabi_realloc` when the bump would cross the current limit, which is a bounded change; reclaiming per-call scratch is the larger follow-on and (a) plus (c) remove most of what would need reclaiming.
    (f) **The 8-byte canonical return area is bump-allocated on every call although it is constant for the module.** `canonical.py:640` emits `(local.set $area (call $alloc (i32.const N)))` per canonical export. The component host reads the return area before it can re-enter the instance, so one module-level cell serves every call on a single-threaded instance. Measured: **13 fuel per call**, constant, plus 8 bytes of otherwise unbounded heap growth per call removed, which compounds with (e). Small on its own; free to take alongside (e). **FIXED 2026-09-02** (same commit). One `$__canon_ret_area` global, sized to the widest result any export in the module returns and carved off the bottom of the bump heap so it can never be handed out again. RE-MEASURED 13 fuel per call on `echo` (58 for the lift alone, 45 with the area, 49 with the item-432(a) bounds check), and the 8 bytes per call of unreclaimed heap growth are gone. `scalar` stays at 11 fuel, confirming NEGATIVE RESULT (1): a direct-returned `Int` never touched a return area.
    (g) **No constant folding: `7 * 6` emits two `i64.const` and a `call $int_mul` with its overflow test.** `emit.py:2207` routes every `+ - *` through the checked helper regardless of whether both operands are literals the emitter can evaluate. Measured on `fn scaled(n: Int) -> Int { return n * 1000 + 7 * 6 }`: **47 fuel down to 29** when the constant multiply is folded, so **18 fuel per folded checked op**. Lowest expected win in this item and listed for completeness; **FIXED 2026-09-03** (`fix/113-wasm-perf-remainder`, PR #259, `backends/wasm/emit.py` `_fold_int_const`, applied in `_bin_expr` and in `_un_expr`'s subtract-from-zero). RE-MEASURED on the committed harness with a new `programs/constfold.revl` and a `const_fold` variant: **47 to 29 fuel**, exactly the audit's **18 fuel per folded checked op**, and the variant is now a no-op against the fixed emitter. The operands are matched as EMITTED WAT rather than as IR nodes, which makes the fold compositional for nothing: `2 * 3 * 4 + 5 - 6` collapses to one `(i64.const 23)`, and `-5` folds through the unary subtract-from-zero. **The proposed OVERFLOW SHAPE was measured and REJECTED.** This item proposed making an overflowing constant expression a compile-time refusal. That would make the wasm tier alone refuse a program every other tier compiles and traps in at run time, and `docs/arithmetic.md` pins the TRAP; so an overflowing fold is DECLINED instead and the checked helper stays, keeping the trap exactly where it was. `9223372036854775807 + 1` still emits two constants and `(call $int_add)`; `4611686018427387904 * (0 - 2)`, which is exactly `Int.MIN` and therefore representable, folds. `/` and `%` are not folded: they are single native instructions whose zero-divisor and `MIN / -1` traps this tier deliberately leaves to wasm. SELF-HOST: PORTED (`selfhost/emit_wasm.rvl` `fold_const`, whose overflow guards are written so the CHECK itself cannot overflow — this tier's `+ - *` trap, so the fold cannot be decided by computing the answer first), and `tests/fixtures/emit_wasm_corpus/constfold.rvl` puts the folds, the declined overflows and the exact-`Int.MIN` product under the byte-agreement oracle rather than trusting a corpus that never contained a constant expression (the item-429 trap).
    **NEGATIVE RESULTS, measured and fine.** (1) The **scalar** boundary is already tight: `dbl(21)` costs 11 fuel end to end and no fix in this item moves it, so `Int`/`Bool` params and results need no work. (2) `$str_concat` itself is well written, one allocation and two `memory.copy`; the defect in (d) is the emitted chain around it, not the helper. (3) The checked-arithmetic helpers `$int_add`/`$int_sub`/`$int_mul` are the branch-free shapes an author would write, and cost nothing beyond the fold in (g). (4) `$__canon_lower_str` is already zero-copy: it writes a pointer and a length and copies nothing. (5) Dead-code pruning changes **no** fuel number anywhere in the bench, confirming (b) is purely a size finding and that the emitted live path is not paying for the dead prelude at runtime. (6) The record lift/lower helpers do one copy per field with no redundant round trip. (7) `(local $__revl_tmp i32)` is declared in every emitted function and genuinely used in the larger goldens (52 references in `canonical_aggregates.core.wat`), so it is only wasted on trivial bodies and is not worth gating.
    **A MEASUREMENT NOTE worth keeping for anyone re-running this.** `wasmtime run --invoke` meters INSTANTIATION as well as the call, and a module carrying `(data ...)` segments pays about **16385 fuel** to instantiate. Probe: adding two 12-byte data segments to an otherwise identical module moved a 16-byte `echo` from 380 to 16765 fuel. A ratio of raw fuel totals therefore understates the per-call ratio for any program with string literals. The harness reports the baseline-minus-variant delta and the per-byte slope instead, both of which are instantiation-independent.
    **ITEM CLOSED 2026-09-03 (PR #259, `fix/113-wasm-perf-remainder`).** Every finding in this item is landed and re-measured on the committed harness: (a), (c), (f) and (e) earlier, and (b), (d) and (g) here. On the bench programs the emitted `core.wasm` is now **612 to 1193 B where it was 3675 to 3830 B**, every emitted function is reachable from an export, a 9-part template costs **4 fuel per argument byte instead of 20**, and a constant `+ - *` costs nothing at run time. Two of the item's own PROPOSED SHAPES were rejected on inspection rather than implemented — (b)'s per-helper token gating, which cannot see a helper wanted only by another helper and would have swept the core before the canonical wrappers that call into it, and (g)'s compile-time refusal of an overflowing constant, which `docs/arithmetic.md` contradicts — and (d)'s stated self-host blast radius was wrong (the self-hosted wasm emitter has no template interpolation at all). The harness's variants are all checked no-ops against the fixed emitter now, so `run.py`'s `saved` columns read 0 and every win lives in the **baseline** row.
433. (issue #70) **JAVA CODEGEN PERFORMANCE AUDIT (2026-09-02). ALL TEN FINDINGS ARE NOW MEASURED ON A REAL JVM; SEVEN WERE FIXED AND TWO DID NOT SURVIVE MEASUREMENT (`fix/433-java-codegen-perf`).** **THE HEADER THIS ITEM SHIPPED WITH WAS WRONG AND IS CORRECTED HERE.** It read "NO JDK ON THE AUDIT MACHINE, SO EVERY FINDING BELOW IS STATIC", on the evidence that `java -version` and `javac -version` both fail with "Unable to locate a Java Runtime". They do — but that is the macOS `/usr/bin/java` STUB, which exists on PATH (and so satisfies `shutil.which("java")`) precisely when no JDK is selected. There IS a working JDK on this machine, **openjdk 26.0.2 at `/opt/homebrew/opt/openjdk`**, keg-only and therefore not on PATH. The riders below had already caught this once; the perf half of the item never did. With `export JAVA_HOME=/opt/homebrew/opt/openjdk; export PATH="$JAVA_HOME/bin:$PATH"` the audit's own harness `bench/codegen/java/run.py` runs, and every F-number below now carries an EXECUTED allocated-bytes-per-op number instead of a construction-site count. THE LESSON, since it is the second time on this item: a probe that answers "the tool is missing" is a claim about the PROBE until the tool has been looked for where package managers actually put it. **A FINDING THAT WAS INVISIBLE TO THE STATIC AUDIT AND FELL OUT OF THE FIRST `javac` RUN:** the emitted unit for ANY routed require DID NOT COMPILE. `_emit_java_router_class` throws `CordisException` on the empty live set and on the unreachable tail, but `_core_imports` added that import only for a `fail` STEP, so `backends/java/scenarios/router.rvl` emitted 6119 bytes of Java that javac rejects with "cannot find symbol: class CordisException". Every router assertion in the suite was a substring match on source text, so nothing caught it. Fixed; the scenario now compiles (12023 class bytes). **MEASUREMENT NOTE, UNCHANGED:** the harness reports ALLOCATED BYTES PER OP (`com.sun.management.ThreadMXBean.getCurrentThreadAllocatedBytes`), a count that does not move when another process takes the CPU, and deliberately reports no duration at all. Numbers below are the minimum over 15 samples after warmup on openjdk 26.0.2, emitted-vs-hand-written, N as each case's `Drive` declares it. Timings were also not taken for a second, independent reason: the audit machine was running about a dozen other agents, and on the JVM machine load distorts JIT compilation as well as wall clock, so an interleaved A/B ratio would not have cancelled it either. The deliverable is therefore a static audit plus a runnable harness, `bench/codegen/java/`, which reports ALLOCATED BYTES PER OP (a count, unaffected by load) and compiled class-file sizes, and deliberately reports no duration at all. Six cases, each an emitted program paired with the Java a competent Java developer writes for the same semantics, with a `setup()` that asserts the two sides agree before anything is sampled. `run.py --static` needs no JDK; the default mode exits 77 when none is present.
    **✅ FIXED 2026-09-02 on `fix/433-java-codegen-perf`. REPRODUCED the same way: `!(nan == nan)` and `negzero == 0.0` EXECUTE green on python and rust; java emitted `return java.util.Objects.equals(a, b);` for `fn feq(a: Float, b: Float) -> Bool`, which is the inverted answer by JDK definition (inspection, no JVM). The fix is a `revlEq` helper emitted with the module, the same shape TypeScript's carries: a `Double` pair compares as primitives, a `List`/`Map`/`Optional` recurses so a Float nested inside one keeps the rule, and every other value keeps the structural `Objects.equals` it already had. Gated on the document containing an equality, so a program without one emits byte-for-byte as before. (R3) FIXED. A component that stayed on the legacy `_emit_component` path emitted a provider class with NO config fields, so a provide-method body reading `config.prefix` emitted a bare `prefix` that is not in scope. VERIFIED BY INSPECTION ONLY, since checking "does not compile" needs `javac` and there is none here: the emitted `public static final class HelloG implements Greeter { HelloG() {} public String greet() { return prefix; } }` declares no `prefix` and nests in `Components` as a `static` class, so the name resolves nowhere and javac raises "cannot find symbol". The cause was wider than `_component_needs_modern` (`:2864`) not considering config reads: `_emit_component_modern` did not carry config into its provider class either, so forcing the modern path alone still emitted the same unbound name. Both halves are fixed, and the threading is gated on a provide method actually reading config so every other component emits unchanged. (R4) FIXED. `_emit_java_router_class` mutated `cursor` and a plain `HashMap served` with no synchronization while `backends/java/placement/PlacementRunner.java:380` serves each bridge connection on `new Thread(() -> serveConn(ch), "bridge-conn")`, and the go router already takes `r.mu.Lock()` for the whole of its select. `revlSelect` is now `synchronized`, the exact mirror of go's whole-function mutex, since every read and write of both fields happens inside it. Same hazard item 397 closed for the host `Map`. **WHY THE CONFORMANCE SUITE DID NOT CATCH R1 AND R2. THE CORPUS ALREADY HAD THE CASES; THE GATE THAT RUNS THEM ON JAVA IS SWITCHED OFF EVERYWHERE.** `IEEE_FLOAT` in `tests/test_cross_tier_execution.py` has asserted `7 / 2 == 3.5` and `0.0 / 0.0 != 0.0 / 0.0` since the Float work landed, and java FAILS BOTH: the pre-fix emitter renders them `java.util.Objects.equals((7L / 2L), 3.5d)`, where `7L / 2L` is Java integer division and boxes to a `Long` no `Double` equals, and `!java.util.Objects.equals((0.0d / 0.0d), (0.0d / 0.0d))`, where `Double.equals` compares `doubleToLongBits` and so calls NaN equal to itself. Either assertion fires the first time java executes that file. java never has. Its probes sit behind `@pytest.mark.skipif(not os.environ.get("REVL_CROSS_TIER_SLOW"))`, and **`REVL_CROSS_TIER_SLOW` is set nowhere in this repository**: not in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, not in the `Makefile`, not in `ci/`. `pytest tests/` runs in the `frontend` and `frontend-cordis` jobs, neither of which installs a JDK or exports the variable, so every `*_slow` test in that file (java AND rust) has executed in no CI job and on no machine that did not set it by hand. A second gate stacks on top: `RUNNERS["java"]` returns `skip: no working JDK`, and the test turns that into `pytest.skip`, so "nothing checked it" is recorded as a pass. The file's own header claimed "CI runs the full matrix", which was simply untrue; that line is now corrected in place. So this was the "java is excluded from the comparison" branch, excluded in practice by a dead env-var gate rather than by a missing tier. The corpus gap is real but small and confined to ONE case: `IEEE_FLOAT`'s "negative zero equals zero" is spelled `0.0 - 0.0`, which is POSITIVE zero in IEEE, so no probe anywhere ever produced a real `-0.0` and R2's second half was uncovered even in principle. `tools/conformance.py` and `tests/test_conformance_validate.py` could not have caught either rider in any configuration: the first asks "did the emitter raise?" and the second asks "does the emitted code compile?", and R1 and R2 emit cleanly and compile cleanly, they just compute a different number. That is this file's own recurring lesson one level up. HOLE CLOSED, in the order that matters. (a) The load-bearing fix is two STATIC guards that need no toolchain and therefore run in the `frontend` job and on any laptop, `test_java_true_division_widens_both_operands` and `test_java_equality_goes_through_the_revl_helper`, the same role the rust `PartialEq` and TypeScript `revlEq` guards already play; these are what would have caught both riders here. (b) A new executed probe, `"true division and IEEE float equality"`, adds the missing negative zero (`scale(0.0, 0.0 - 1.0)`, a genuine `-0.0`) plus `Int / Int`, `Float / Float` and NaN under both `==` and `!=`, with the divisor and both compared floats arriving as PARAMETERS so a literal zero divisor is not a checker rejection and Go cannot constant-fold the case away. (c) `tools/conformance.py` gains `expr/true division` and `expr/float equality`, so both constructs also reach every tier's real compiler in the `conformance` job (`docs/conformance.md` regenerated; both are `lim` on wasm, which has no Float, beside the existing `type/Float service` row). (d) STILL OPEN, and the real repair: export `REVL_CROSS_TIER_SLOW=1` for `tests/test_cross_tier_execution.py` in the `conformance` job, which is the one place node, rust, java, go and wasmtime all exist at once. That was NOT done on this branch, deliberately: switching it on today turns the java record-`equals` gap below into a red build for a defect outside this item's scope, so it should land immediately after that gap is closed. Until then, treat a `*_slow` skip as "unmeasured", never as "passing". **SELF-HOST (item 429's standing rule, checked in the sources, not inferred from the oracle).** `selfhost/emit_java.rvl` carries R1, R2 and R3: the widened `/`, `revlEq` with its `uses_equality` gate and helper block, and `reads_config`/`provider_config_fields` plus the `needs_modern` arm. R4 has nothing to port, because routers are DEFERRED in that slice (`selfhost/emit_java.rvl:69`) and it emits no router class. `selfhost/lower.rvl` needed no change: `/` already carries `operands` from the reference lowering and R1 was purely an emitter defect. The byte-equality oracle gains `tests/fixtures/emit_java_corpus/comp_config_provide.rvl` so R3's port is actually covered rather than passing by absence. **ADJACENT DEFECT FOUND WHILE REPRODUCING. FIXED 2026-09-02 on `fix/433-java-record-equality`, which branches from this riders branch because its `equals` must match `revlEq`'s IEEE rule.** v3 record types emitted as `public static final class <T>` with public final fields and a canonical constructor and NO `equals`/`hashCode` (`_emit_v3_types`), and each SEALED-VARIANT case class had the identical defect, nullary and payload alike. So both inherited `Object.equals`, which is reference identity, and `revlEq`'s `java.util.Objects.equals` fallback dispatched straight into it: `{ id: 1, name: "a" } == { id: 1, name: "a" }` was FALSE on java and TRUE on python, typescript, rust and go, against syntax-2.0 3.4. `revlEq` could not have papered over it even in principle, since its fallback IS the inherited method. THE FIX: each record class and each case class now carries a generated `equals`/`hashCode`. A primitive component (long/int/double/boolean) compares with the primitive `==`, never `Double.compare` or `Double.equals`, which are the same `doubleToLongBits` trap R2 was; a reference component routes through `revlEq`, so a nested record recurses into the inner class's own generated `equals` and a Float inside a List/Map/Opt keeps the IEEE rule. A matching `revlHash` mirrors `revlEq` arm for arm, because `Objects.hashCode` disagrees with it wherever a Float is involved: it separates 0.0 from -0.0, and the collection classes delegate to the element's own `hashCode`. Both helpers keep the `_uses_equality` gating idiom, emitted exactly where they are called, so a record of nothing but Int/Float/Bool emits byte-identically to before and `backends/java/golden/user_cache.java` does not move at all. NaN DECISION, STATED BECAUSE IT HAS A COST: there is deliberately no `if (this == o) return true` fast path, so the answer never depends on aliasing, which matches rust's derived `PartialEq`. IEEE equality is not an equivalence relation, so a value carrying a NaN is not equal to itself and does not satisfy `Object.equals`'s reflexivity requirement; such a value is therefore not a well-behaved hash key on this tier. `HashMap`/`HashSet` short-circuit on `key == k`, so a lookup with the identical reference still finds it, but a structurally identical NaN-carrying lookup never will, and `List.contains`/`indexOf`/`Set.remove` can miss. Matching every other tier is the requirement and this is what it costs on a host whose collections assume an equivalence relation. **EVIDENCE: EXECUTED ON A REAL JVM, and this item's own header is wrong about that.** There IS a working JDK on this machine, openjdk 26.0.2 at `/opt/homebrew/opt/openjdk/bin`. It is keg-only, so it is not on PATH, and the `/usr/bin/java` macOS stub that IS on PATH errors with "Unable to locate a Java Runtime" while still satisfying `shutil.which("java")`. That stub is what produced this item's "NO JDK ON THE AUDIT MACHINE" header and R1/R2/R3's inspection-only labels; the perf findings F1-F10 genuinely were static, but the correctness claims did not have to be. With `PATH=/opt/homebrew/opt/openjdk/bin:$PATH` the divergence was REPRODUCED AND FIXED BY EXECUTION: the pre-fix emitter fails `PROBES["structural equality"]` on the JVM with `java.lang.AssertionError` at `testRecordsCompareByValue`, and the post-fix emitter passes it, along with the new `PROBES["declared value types compare structurally"]` and the riders' `PROBES["true division and IEEE float equality"]`. `pytest tests/test_cross_tier_execution.py -k java` with `REVL_CROSS_TIER_SLOW=1` is 33 passed, 0 skipped, and `test_emitted_code_survives_its_toolchain[java]` runs green. The same probe runs green on python, go and typescript. THE NaN CONSEQUENCE WAS MEASURED, NOT ARGUED: `x.equals(x)` is FALSE for a NaN-carrying record, so reflexivity is gone; `HashMap.get` still finds it by the identical reference (HashMap short-circuits on `key == k`) but returns null for a structurally identical key; `HashSet` will hold two "equal" NaN values at once; and `ArrayList.contains`, which has no such short-circuit, returns FALSE for a value that is literally in the list. Every non-NaN value round-trips correctly, including a distinct-but-equal nested record and a -0.0/0.0 pair at the top level and inside a List, which is `revlHash` doing its job. GUARD: four new STATIC guards that need no toolchain and run in the `frontend` job, verified to FAIL against the pre-fix emitter and pass after: `test_java_declared_value_types_carry_a_structural_equals` sweeps every emitted value class rather than naming one, so a future third shape cannot opt out; plus the Float IEEE, nested-record and nullary-case guards. SELF-HOST: ported to `selfhost/emit_java.rvl` per item 429's standing rule, checked in the source, and the byte-equality corpus agrees again on `records.rvl`, `adts.rvl` and `comp_host_map_generic.rvl`. STEP (d) ABOVE IS NOW UNBLOCKED: this was the gap that made exporting `REVL_CROSS_TIER_SLOW=1` in the `conformance` job turn red. **STILL OPEN, one level further out, and EXECUTED so the next agent does not have to re-derive it:** `revlEq` compares `Bytes` (`byte[]`) with `Objects.equals`, which is ARRAY IDENTITY. Compiled and run on openjdk 26.0.2, `beq(x, y)` for two equal but distinct `byte[]{1,2,3}` is FALSE and `beq(x, x)` is true, where python's `bytes` compare structurally (`bytes([1,2,3]) == bytes([1,2,3])` is True) and rust's `Vec<u8>` does the same. A record with a `Bytes` component inherits the hole uniformly (`blob(x).equals(blob(y))` is likewise false), which is exactly why it was left alone here instead of being widened into this fix: one hole reported once beats a record that compares Bytes differently from top-level `==`. Reachability is currently narrow, since no literal or builtin produces a `Bytes` value and it can only enter through an extern signature, which is presumably how it went unnoticed. It wants its own item: a `byte[]` arm in `revlEq` (`java.util.Arrays.equals`) and in `revlHash` (`java.util.Arrays.hashCode`), plus a cross-tier probe, which needs a per-tier extern body to construct the value.
    **NEGATIVE RESULTS, examined and FINE.** The modern component path (`_emit_component_modern`) emits close to what a Java developer writes: final fields, constructor injection, one `ctx.get` per required key hoisted out of the provider, no per-call resolution, no defensive copies, and `fx.track(Disposables.of(() -> ...))` per effect, which is one capturing lambda for an inverse that genuinely must capture. `Math.addExact`/`subtractExact`/`multiplyExact` are the correct lowering of trapping `Int` arithmetic per `docs/arithmetic.md` and are HotSpot intrinsics, so the hand-written equivalent is the same code. `?? ` lowering to `orElseGet(() -> ...)` allocates nothing per call, because a non-capturing lambda is cached by `LambdaMetafactory`; `orElse` would be marginally cheaper for a literal right operand but the laziness the comment cites is real for a non-literal one, so this is not worth a special case. `revlMapKeys`'s comparator is a non-capturing lambda, so hoisting it buys nothing, and its sort is load-bearing for canonical `Str` order past U+FFFF; only the trailing `List.copyOf` of a list no caller holds is removable, two objects and one O(k) copy per call, which is bench case `map_keys` and ranks below F6. `revlSlice`'s `List.copyOf(xs.subList(..))` is what a competent developer writes, since returning the view would retain the parent list. The host `Map` runtime is a `ConcurrentHashMap` with O(1) insert/get/remove and an atomic `putIfAbsent`, correct and appropriately typed. The `RevlFrame` teardown runtime allocates one capturing lambda per registered inverse, which is unavoidable, and its `ExecutorService` is created lazily and only on an abort that has compensations, so it costs nothing on the clean path. `Pool` is O(n) on release with a sort, but it is a deterministic in-memory simulation with pool sizes in single digits and no hot path, so it is not worth touching. Emitted `if`/`while`/`for` control flow, `List.of` for list literals, record and sealed-interface emission, and the `switch` pattern lowering for `match` are all what a Java developer writes.
    **HYPOTHESES, RESOLVED 2026-09-02 on `fix/433-java-codegen-perf`.** All three are now settled by execution, and the settling changed two answers. F6's class-file cost was a hypothesis ("presumably costs class-file bytes in proportion to source bytes") and is now MEASURED with javac: the proportion holds and is if anything understated — 42 to 66 percent of the compiled unit on the three files the audit named. F1's object count for `String.format`, read off the `Formatter` source rather than measured, was CONSERVATIVE: the real gap is 508968 against 40040 allocated bytes per op. The blanket claim that "every allocation figure in this item is a static count of construction sites" is exactly what made F5 wrong, because construction SITES are not BYTES: F5's ten-sites-against-three became 221224 bytes against 286760, in the emitter's favour. `bench/codegen/java/run.py` did replace all ten with `getCurrentThreadAllocatedBytes` deltas, as it was written to. Relates to 397 (the host-Map thread-safety fix whose reasoning F3's rider extends), 391 (the standing self-host port question, which F1, F2, F3, F5 and F6 each raise for `selfhost/emit_java.rvl`), and 327 (per-backend drift, which R1 is an instance of).

435. (issue #115) **TYPESCRIPT CODEGEN PERFORMANCE AUDIT: THE ASYNC COLOUR IS APPLIED PER-FUNCTION, NOT PER-EXPRESSION, SO EVERY `match` IN AN ASYNC BODY BUYS MICROTASK TURNS IT NEVER USES; AND TWO STDLIB LOWERINGS ARE QUADRATIC (2026-09-02).** Audit of the code the TypeScript backend GENERATES, against what a competent TS developer writes by hand for the same revl program. Harness committed at `bench/codegen/typescript/` (`run.mjs`, `cpuprof.mjs`, `emit.py`, `src/*.rvl`, `emitted/*.ts`, `cases/*.ts`, `results/`) and re-runnable standalone. **No timings were taken and none should be quoted from this item**: the audit machine was carrying about a dozen concurrent agents plus six other perf audits, so every number below is load-independent by construction: microtask turns, Promise allocations, code points materialised, array elements copied, and CPU-profile sample shares. Executed on node 26.7.0 against real cordis; every case IMPORTS the emitted function and runs it (nothing is transcribed), declares the emitted fragments it was written against, and `run.mjs` reports `STALE` rather than a number if the emitter has since changed shape. No emitter was edited.

    **✅ FIXED 2026-09-05 (`f3640460`); the finding as filed is kept below for the record, WAS OPEN AS RE-VERIFIED ON `2d2fa07f`.** **(a) THE HEADLINE, AND IT IS AN ASYNC-COLOUR BUG. `_v3_match_expr` colours the match by the ENCLOSING FUNCTION, not by whether any arm suspends.** `backends/typescript/emit.py:2453`, `a = "async " if ctx.in_async else ""`, plus the two `(await ({a}({bind}) => ({body}))({tmp}.value))` branches at :2475 and :2498. `ctx.in_async` is true for the whole body of an async-coloured `fn`, so a match whose every arm is a bound variable or a literal still renders as an awaited `async` IIFE wrapping one awaited `async` arrow per bound arm. The arm arrow for `Final(a) => a` is emitted literally as `return (await (async (a) => (a))($revl_match_1.value))`: a Promise allocated around an already-computed string, then awaited. The comment at :2450 calls the extra wrapper "harmless (tsc does not require an `await`)", which is true of type-checking and false of execution. **Measured, per match evaluation: 2 excess microtask turns and 4 excess Promise allocations** (`bench/codegen/typescript/cases/match_sync_arms.ts`; emitted 5 turns / 9 promises, hand-written 3 / 5). The recursive-agent case (`cases/match_async.ts`, 12 matches per call) shows the same 2 turns / 4 promises per match, 24 turns and 48 Promise allocations over one call. CPU profile at equal iteration counts: emitted 730 samples with 16.2% self-time in `EMITTED (anonymous)` frames that the hand-written version does not have at all, and 20.7% in GC against 14.2%; hand-written 367 samples for the identical workload. **PROPOSED FIX, and the cheap one captures all of it.** Emit `async` on the IIFE and on an arm arrow only when the RENDERED body contains `await`, since the emitter already knows this, since `_expr` decides where to insert every `await` and suppresses the await-seed inside arrows (`ctx.in_async and not ctx.in_arrow`, :635), and drop the matching `await` at the call site when it does not. A de-coloured variant that keeps the IIFE and the arm arrows exactly as they are today and changes nothing but the keyword **measures identical to the full hand-written rewrite (3 turns / 5 promises)**, so the whole win is available without touching the lowering's structure. The larger rewrite (lower a match in return position to a plain `switch` with the scrutinee in a local) additionally removes 3 closure allocations per evaluation but buys no further turns. **Cross-tier precedent: the python tier already solved this**, `backends/python/emit.py:2196` `_match_expr(..., awaited=True)` switches from a one-shot lambda to a walrus bind precisely so the arm body's `await` lands in the enclosing frame instead of a wrapper (item 263). The TS tier is the outlier. **Blast radius:** `backends/typescript/golden/async_agent_loop.ts` regenerates; `selfhost/emit_ts.rvl` mirrors this shape byte-exactly (its own header names "the async `match` shape (Opt IIFE + tagged switch, arm-arrows + inner calls awaited)"), so the port is mandatory or `tests/test_selfhost_emit_ts.py` goes red. **RE-CHECK 2026-09-02:** `_v3_match_expr` is at `backends/typescript/emit.py:2465` and still opens `a = "async " if ctx.in_async else ""` at `:2481` (cites `:2453`); the comment above it still argues the wrapper is harmless.

    **✅ FIXED 2026-09-05 (`f3640460`); the finding as filed is kept below for the record, WAS OPEN AS RE-VERIFIED ON `2d2fa07f`.** **(b) SAME ROOT CAUSE, SECOND SITE: a function value whose body only forwards an already-async call is still emitted `async`.** `emit.py:899/921`, `is_async = bool(node.get("async"))` / `prefix = "async " if is_async else ""`. `fn run(prompt) = agent_loop(prompt, (msgs) => model.complete(msgs))` emits `(async (msgs: any) => (ctx.model.complete(msgs)))`. The rendered body contains no `await`, so the `async` adds a resolution hop over the promise the body already returns, and the closure is rebuilt on every call although it captures only `ctx`, which is fixed for the whole activation. **Measured, per operation call: 2 excess microtask turns and 2 excess Promise allocations** (`cases/async_arrow.ts`; emitted 6 / 9, hand-written 4 / 7). Again a de-coloured variant that still allocates the arrow per call measures identical to the hand-written version, so the ticks are the colour and the closure is only allocation. Same fix as (a): the `async` keyword follows the rendered body, not the declared type. Hoisting the arrow to the activation is a separate, smaller, second change. **Blast radius:** `golden/async_fn_values.ts`; `selfhost/emit_ts.rvl` (its header lists "the async ARROW (`async (…) => …`)" and notes "an async arrow flattens its own Promise"). **RE-CHECK 2026-09-02:** `backends/typescript/emit.py:900` `is_async = bool(node.get("async"))` and `:922` `prefix = "async " if is_async else ""` are unchanged (cites `:899/921`).

    **✅ FIXED 2026-09-05 (`cef42ac3`); the finding as filed is kept below for the record, WAS OPEN AS RE-VERIFIED ON `2d2fa07f`.** **(c) `revlLen` / `revlCharAt` / `revlCharCodeAt` / `revlSlice` rebuild the whole code-point array on EVERY call, so an index scan is quadratic.** `emit.py:2964-2990`, `_REVL_STR_HELPER`: `revlLen` is `Array.from(x).length`, `revlCharAt` is `Array.from(s)[Number(i)]`. Code-point rather than code-unit indexing is correct (docs/strings.md) and is not the problem; materialising the array per call is. Compounding it, the `while` lowering (`_v3_stmt`) re-evaluates the loop condition each iteration, so the idiomatic `while (i < s.length())` calls `revlLen` n times. **Measured, exactly, by intercepting `Array.from` and totalling the code points it produces** for `count_digits` over an n-code-point ASCII string: n=200 -> 80,200; n=400 -> 320,400; n=800 -> 1,280,800; n=1600 -> 5,121,600. Growth is 2n^2 + n against a hand-written n. CPU profile of the emitted scan: **94.8% of self-samples in `revlLen` (47.3%) and `revlCharAt` (45.6%), and 0.5% in `count_digits` itself**. Essentially all of the program's instructions are in the helper, not in the user's logic. **PROPOSED FIX, two tiers.** Cheap and helper-local: memoise the last decoded code-point array (one string, one array) inside the helper block, which collapses all four counts to exactly n with no change to any lowering; measured in `cases/string_scan.ts` as the `memo` column. Caveat worth stating: the memo keys on string identity, so a caller alternating between two equal-but-distinct strings thrashes it, and the guard should be written to fail cheap. Proper: lower an index scan over a `Str` to a single decode, and hoist a loop-invariant `length()` call on a binding the loop body never assigns (a one-line LICM that is trivially sound here because the receiver is a parameter). **Blast radius:** the helper text is emitted into every module that uses a `Str` method, so every golden containing `_REVL_STR_HELPER` regenerates; `selfhost/emit_ts.rvl` carries the helper text and must be ported. **RE-CHECK 2026-09-02:** `_REVL_STR_HELPER` is at `backends/typescript/emit.py:2992` now (cites `:2964-2990`) and `revlLen` is still `Array.from(x).length`.

    **✅ FIXED 2026-09-05 (`84d236fc` / `fc4a5ba9`); the finding as filed is kept below for the record, WAS OPEN AS RE-VERIFIED ON `2d2fa07f`.** **(d) `xs = xs.push(e)` lowers to a whole-array spread, so building an n-element list copies n(n-1)/2 elements.** `emit.py:2277`, `if method == "push": return f"[...{target}, {args[0]}]"`. Value semantics make a copy the honest default, but in the overwhelmingly common `xs = xs.push(e)` the source binding is dead the instant the assignment lands, so the copy is never observed. **Measured, exactly, by intercepting the array iterator** (which forces spread onto its slow path and makes each copied element observable): n=50 -> 1,225 elements copied; n=100 -> 4,950; n=200 -> 19,900; n=400 -> 79,800. Hand-written `xs.push(e)` copies zero. **PROPOSED FIX:** rewrite `x = x.push(e)` to a destructive `x.push(e)` when `x` is a local binding that has not escaped since its last assignment. The safe conservative form is "inside a pure `fn` (G6 already forbids the interesting aliasing), `x` is a `var` local, and `x` is not passed as an argument, returned, or captured anywhere in the loop body". Do NOT apply it to a binding whose old value is still live. **Blast radius:** any golden building a list in a loop; `selfhost/emit_ts.rvl` builds lists this way itself, so the self-host source is one of the beneficiaries. **RE-CHECK 2026-09-02:** `backends/typescript/emit.py:2305-2306` still returns `[...{target}, {args[0]}]` for `push` (cites `:2277`).

    **(a) AND (b) DONE (2026-09-02), and the audit's cheap fix is the whole win it predicted.** The colour is now decided PER EXPRESSION, from the RENDERED text, in `backends/typescript/emit.py`. `_renders_await(text)` is the entire predicate (the literal `"await "`: every `await` the emitter writes is followed by a space, and `selfhost/emit_ts.rvl` has to mirror it with no regex engine); it errs only towards KEEPING `async`, which is the pre-435 shape, so a false positive costs a microtask turn and never correctness. **(a)** `_v3_match_expr` colours each binding arm-arrow by that arm's own rendered body and the IIFE by the assembled body, dropping the matching `await` at each call site; the IIFE and the arm arrows are structurally UNCHANGED, exactly the variant the audit measured. `match-sync-arms-in-async-fn` goes 5.0 turns / 9.0 promises to **3.0 / 5.0**, equal to hand-written and to the de-coloured variant: **excess 2 turns / 4 promises to 0 / 0**. `match-in-async-fn` is unchanged at 4.2 / 8.3 because every one of its 12 matches takes the arm that genuinely awaits; its residual 2 / 4 per match is the IIFE-plus-arm-arrow structure itself, which only the deliberately-not-taken `switch` rewrite removes. One guard the audit did not name: inside an ARROW the colour stays unconditional, because the await-seed is suppressed there (`in_async and not in_arrow`) so an arm body can be an un-awaited Promise with no `await` in its text, and the arm arrow's `async`/`await` pair is what resolves it. **(b)** the arrow branch drops `async` when the body renders no `await` AND the body IS that un-awaited emission Promise (`_v3_emission_call_node`), narrower than "no await in body", because an `async` arrow over a PLAIN value returns `T` where the slot's type says `Promise<T>`, while `async (p) => e` and `(p) => e` over an emission call are both `(p) => Promise<T>` and stay assignable. `async-arrow-value` goes 6.0 / 9.0 to **4.0 / 7.0**, equal to hand-written: **excess 2 turns / 2 promises to 0 / 0**. Per-call closure allocation is untouched, as the audit ranked it. **Goldens: two lines.** In `golden/async_agent_loop.ts` the sync `Final` arm becomes `return ((answer) => ({ kind: "Final", value: answer }))($revl_match_1.value)` while the awaiting `NeedTool` arm and the IIFE keep their colour; in `golden/async_fn_values.ts` `(async (msgs: any) => (ctx.model.complete(msgs)))` becomes `((msgs: any) => (ctx.model.complete(msgs)))`. `npm run typecheck` (tsc over `golden/**`) is green, which is the type argument executed rather than asserted. **Self-host ported** (`renders_await`, `emission_call_node`, `arm_is_async`, `wrap_match` in `selfhost/emit_ts.rvl`) and checked against the SOURCE, not just the oracle: `emit_ts_corpus/async_module_switch.rvl` already carried a sync `Err(e) => e` arm beside an awaiting `Ok` arm, so (a) is covered, but NO corpus fixture put an emission call in an arrow body, so `async_arrow_emission.rvl` was added for (b): item 429's trap, where a green oracle only means neither side was asked. **Exit conditions (1), (2), (4) and (5) are met**; (3), the quadratic string helpers, is NOT done and neither is (d), the `push` spread.

    **(c) DONE (2026-09-02), and it is the helper-local tier the audit priced, with the whole win it predicted.** `revlLen` / `revlSlice` / `revlCharAt` / `revlCharCodeAt` now share ONE code-point decode, `revlCps`, memoised in `backends/typescript/emit.py`'s `_REVL_STR_HELPER`. **No lowering moved**: the `while` condition still calls `revlLen(s)` on every iteration, exactly as the audit found it, and that call is now O(1), which is what makes the cheap tier sufficient and the LICM/single-decode rewrite unnecessary for this defect. Code points materialised by `Array.from` for `count_digits`, the audit's own measure: n=200 **80,200 -> 200**; n=400 320,400 -> 400; n=800 1,280,800 -> 800; n=1600 **5,121,600 -> 1,600**. Quadratic (x4.0 per doubling) to LINEAR (x2.0), and equal to hand-written and to the `memo` column at every size. The CPU profile inverts with it: the emitted scan goes from 730 samples with 94.8% of self-time in `revlLen` + `revlCharAt` to **6 samples**, against 1205 for the hand-written arm, which now decodes more than the emitted one does because it re-decodes per call. Microtask turns and Promise allocations are unchanged at zero excess, as they were before the fix: (c) was never a tick finding. **TWO slots, not the audit's one.** The audit named the thrash caveat and said the guard should fail cheap; a single slot thrashes on a PAIRWISE scan (compare / merge / diff two strings), which is common enough to matter, and a thrashing cache is exactly the pre-435(c) cost. Two slots make that shape linear too, and since the miss path IS the old code the cache can never cost more than the compare that missed. Note the audit's caveat is stated for reference equality; JS `===` on strings is VALUE equality with a pointer check first, so equal-but-distinct strings hit. The price is that the module retains at most two strings and their arrays for its lifetime. **`revlIndexOf` deliberately keeps its own `Array.from`**: its argument is a fresh substring per call, so routing it through the cache would evict a live scan's entry to store something nothing asks for twice. **Test EXECUTES, it does not read.** `backends/typescript/tests/str_scan_memo.test.ts` runs the emitted module with `Array.from` intercepted and counts, over a new committed fixture pair (`tests/fixtures/str_scan.rvl` + `.ir.json`, generated module committed); every expected count is read off the input string rather than pinned, and the astral cases (`count_digits('😀1😀2😀3')`, `common_prefix` over a surrogate pair) pin that the memo did not quietly turn code-point indexing into code-unit indexing, which is the one way this change could have been silently wrong. **Goldens:** `golden/async_agent_loop.ts` and `golden/fr3_json.ts` regenerate, plus the three generated fixture modules that carry the helper. **Self-host ported** (`revl_str_helper` in `selfhost/emit_ts.rvl`) and checked against the SOURCE, not just the oracle: `emit_ts_corpus/strings.rvl` emits the helper block, so the byte-agreement in `tests/test_selfhost_emit_ts.py` (33 passed) genuinely covers the new text — item 429's trap avoided without a new fixture this time. **`run.mjs`'s two closing verdict lines are now DERIVED from the measured column** rather than asserted in prose, because the string one had just become false. **Exit conditions (1) through (5) are now all met.** **(d), the `push` spread, is STILL OPEN** and `run.mjs` still reports it QUADRATIC (x4.0 per doubling, 79,800 elements copied at n=400). It is deliberately not done here: the sound rewrite needs an escape/uniqueness fact about the receiver that this tier cannot see, and item 436 F1 argues the same marker belongs in the FRONTEND, where ts/rust/go/java/wasm and py all get it once instead of six times. A TS-local approximation would have to be mirrored into `selfhost/emit_ts.rvl` as well, doubling the surface on which an aliasing mistake would silently violate value semantics.

    **(d) DONE (2026-09-03, PR #262), by READING item 445's frontend marker instead of re-deriving the proof here.** The audit named the fix and deliberately did not make it: the sound rewrite needs an escape/uniqueness fact this tier could not see, and the conservative form it sketched ("inside a pure `fn`, `x` is a `var` local, and `x` is not passed as an argument, returned, or captured anywhere in the loop body") would have been the fourth independent copy of one aliasing rule. Item 445 put that fact on the IR. `backends/typescript/emit.py` now reads `"unique": true` off the `assign` step (`_ts_inplace_write` / `_ts_inplace_stmt`, four lines in `_v3_stmt`) and renders four shapes destructively: `xs = xs.push(v)` to `xs.push(v)`, `m = m.set(k, v)` to `m.set(k, v)`, `m = m.remove(k)` to `m.delete(k)`, and `p = { p | x = e }` to `Object.assign(p, { x: e })`, which builds the whole override object BEFORE writing any of it so a swap stays simultaneous exactly as the `{ ...p, .. }` spread was. **Measured, `node --expose-gc bench/codegen/typescript/run.mjs`, elements copied building an n-element list: 1225 / 4950 / 19900 / 79800 at n = 50 / 100 / 200 / 400 -> 0 / 0 / 0 / 0, equal to hand-written.** Microtask turns and Promise allocations are unchanged at 2 / 3 per run and were already equal to hand-written: this shape never bought a turn, which is why (a) and (b) were the findings that did. What landed is stronger than the exit asked, because the frontend answers PER WRITE over a forward dataflow with a fixpoint on the loop back edge, needs no special case for `return`, and carries a whole-program retention summary; and it is not restricted to a pure `fn` or to a loop body, because the question does not depend on either. **The conservative fallback.** The site the audit named (`if method == "push": return f"[...{target}, {args[0]}]"`) is untouched and is still the default: absence of the marker means "not proven" and keeps the copy, which is always correct. The lowering also requires a value shape it can render, so an unmodelled value, a `concat`, or a `push` whose argument count does not match its arity is emitted normally. `"unique": "copy"` is DECLINED on this tier: it is conditional on a birth copy at the `let` the frontend stamps `unique_birth` on, whose values are `"List"` and `"Map"` — and `"Map"` covers both a revl `Map` (a JS `Map` here) and a record update (a plain object here), so the copy has no single spelling and guessing is not on the table for an aliasing fact. The python tier honours it because both are a `dict` there. **Verified by EXECUTION, not only by spelling**, because a wrong answer here is a wrong VALUE and not a crash: `backends/typescript/tests/unique_writes.test.ts` runs the emitted module (7 cases — the swap is a swap, snapshots handed to another list stay distinct, flow-sensitively reborn chunks stay separate objects while their pushes ARE in place, a binding born off the caller's list leaves the caller's list alone), and `backends/typescript/test_unique_writes_ts.py` pins the spellings toolchain-free in both directions (12 cases, including that stripping `unique`/`unique_birth` from a lowered document restores the previous output exactly). `selfhost/emit_ts.rvl` is ported, and `tests/fixtures/emit_ts_corpus/unique_writes.rvl` was added because `transforms.rvl` exercises `push` alone and `set`, `remove`, the record update and the negative case would otherwise have been invisible to the byte-agreement oracle (item 429's trap). No golden and no committed generated module changed: neither set contains a marked self-rebind.

    **NEGATIVE RESULTS, executed and fine, recorded so nobody spends a session on them.** (1) The **`revlI64` overflow guard** around every Int operation, **`revlEq` on scalars** instead of `===`, **record literal construction and field reads**, and the **character-class predicate IIFE** `((_rc) => "0" <= _rc && _rc <= "9")(c)` are all cheap: `cases/scalar_ops.ts` measures zero excess microtask turns and zero excess Promise allocations against hand-written equivalents that strip each artefact, and the predicate IIFE never appears in a CPU profile. V8 inlines all four. (2) The **per-call `ctx.effect(() => {...})` in an emission provide-method** (`emit.py:1080`) allocates one fresh body closure per call (confirmed executed: 10 `put()` calls produce 10 `ctx.effect` calls with 10 distinct function objects), but the disposer it returns genuinely captures the call's arguments and cannot be shared, and a hand-written version that hoists only the body closure measures **zero** excess turns and **zero** excess Promise allocations. Not worth a change. (3) The **`{ key, method, args, site }` crossing record** is built once per activation for a `let-effect` bracket, not per call, so it is not on any hot path; the per-call `compensationMethod` crossing is only reached by `emit ... compensate ...`, which is a boundary crossing whose cost dwarfs the literal. (4) **`apply` is made `async` and its `ctx.effect` awaited only when a body step actually awaits** (`emit.py:1933`, item 131). That colouring is per-step and correct, and is NOT an instance of the (a)/(b) defect.

    **Exit:** (1) a match whose arms contain no `await` emits no `async` and no `await`, verified by a test asserting the emitted text for a sync-armed match inside an async-coloured `fn`; (2) `bench/codegen/typescript/run.mjs` reports zero excess microtask turns for `match-sync-arms-in-async-fn` and `async-arrow-value`; (3) the `Array.from` code-point count for `count_digits` is linear in n; (4) `selfhost/emit_ts.rvl` ported and `tests/test_selfhost_emit_ts.py` green; (5) goldens regenerated. **A note for whoever re-runs this:** the harness deliberately prints no durations. `node --expose-gc bench/codegen/typescript/run.mjs --timing` adds interleaved A/B ratios and should be run only on an idle machine; it prints a banner saying so.

436. **CODEGEN PERFORMANCE AUDIT OF THE PYTHON BACKEND: THE EMITTER TURNS EVERY ACCUMULATION LOOP QUADRATIC, AND SEVEN SMALLER PER-EVALUATION TAXES (2026-09-02).** An audit of the code `backends/python/emit.py` GENERATES, against the Python a competent developer writes by hand for the same semantics. Harness committed at `bench/codegen/python/` (`run.py`, `micro.py`, `copycount.py`, `handwritten.py`, `programs/*.rvl`); `PYTHONPATH=src python bench/codegen/python/run.py` reproduces every number below. **NO TIMINGS WERE TAKEN and none are quoted**: the audit ran with about a dozen other agents on the machine, where even an interleaved A/B ratio is noise because the two arms sample different load. Every figure here is deterministic and load-independent: executed bytecode instructions (`sys.settrace` with `f_trace_opcodes`), Python frames entered (`sys.setprofile`), `tracemalloc` peak, and container ELEMENTS COPIED, the last counted by re-executing the emitted module with its AST mechanically rewritten so every copying construct reports how many elements it moved (`copycount.py`, whose rewrite is asserted value-preserving). A timing pass is left ready behind `--time` / `--scale` / `--curve` and should be run on a quiet machine.
    **✅ LANDED 2026-09-02 (`fix/436-python-codegen`), see the fix note below.** Issue #71 tracks the rest of this item. **F1 HIGHEST. Persistent-collection accumulation is O(n^2), and `ops` cannot see it.** `xs.push(v)` emits `(xs + [v])` (`emit.py:588`), `m.set(k,v)` emits `{**m, k: v}` (`:644`), and a record update emits `{**p, 'x': …}` (`:1206`/`:2445`). In the ordinary `var out = []; for (…) { out = out.push(…) }` loop that is one full copy per step. Measured element copies, emitted vs hand-written: `build` 125250 vs 500 at n=500 and 500500 vs 1000 at n=1000 (x4.0 per doubling against x2.0, so quadratic against linear, a **500x gap in copied elements at n=1000**); `maps.fill` and `records.shift_all` identical shape and identical numbers. This is not a synthetic case: `stdlib/list.rvl`'s `list_map`, `list_filter`, `list_dedup` and `list_sort` are all written as push loops, so **`xs.map(f)` on the py tier is quadratic** (`transforms.doubled`, 500500 copies vs 1000 for `[f(x) for x in xs]`), and `list_sort` becomes CUBIC: its copies grow x7.9 per doubling where the hand-written same-algorithm sort grows x4.0. Worth recording separately: the opcode counter reports `build` at **0.96x**, i.e. the emitter's largest defect is INVISIBLE to any interpreter-level profiler, because `out + [x]` is one bytecode instruction that copies `len(out)` pointers in C. A cProfile-driven audit would have missed this entirely. **RE-CHECK 2026-09-02:** every cited site is byte-identical on main (`backends/python/emit.py:588`, `:644`, `:1206`, `:2445`).
    *Fix.* The general one is a frontend uniqueness marker: at the `x = x.push(v)` / `x = x.set(k,v)` shape, where the receiver local is provably unaliased (never bound to another name, never captured by an arrow, never passed or returned before the assignment), mark the IR node `unique` and let every backend lower it in place (`out.append(v)` / `m[k] = v` on py). A py-only alternative needing no analysis is a refcount-guarded copy-on-write helper, the same trick CPython applies to `s += t`; that one is a HYPOTHESIS, not measured. *Blast radius:* moves goldens for every program containing `push`/`set`/record-update; needs the self-host port in `selfhost/emit_py.rvl`; the marker (if done in the frontend) is visible to ts/rust/go/java/wasm, which is an argument for doing it there rather than six times.
    **✅ CLOSED 2026-09-02 (`fix/436-python-codegen-perf`), see the fix note below.** **F2 HIGH. `Map.remove` rebuilds the whole map, through a generator, per removal** Issue #71 tracks this finding. (`emit.py:658`, `(dict((kk, vv) for kk, vv in m.items() if kk != k))`). Two defects stacked. The rebuild is O(n) per removal, so a removal loop is quadratic: 124750 element copies vs 1000 hand-written at n=500, x4.0 per doubling. And the rebuild uses `dict(<generator>)` rather than a dict comprehension, which costs 48 vs 39 ops and **4 Python frames vs 1** per call. Because the copy here IS at Python level, it is the one quadratic the opcode counter does see: `drop_keys` measures **461.94x ops and 17526x calls**, the largest single-case ratio in the suite. *Fix:* dict comprehension immediately (one-line, tiny golden delta); the quadratic itself is F1's marker again. *Blast radius:* goldens for any program using `Map.remove`; self-host port. **RE-CHECK 2026-09-02:** `backends/python/emit.py:658` still emits `dict((kk, vv) for kk, vv in ...)`.
    **🟡 HALF CLOSED 2026-09-02 (`fix/436-python-codegen-perf`, the scrutinee closure) and 2026-09-03 (`fix/436-python-codegen-rest`, the two payload binds that need no binder at all). WHAT IS LEFT is a payload bind READ FROM INSIDE A LARGER BODY; see both fix notes below.** Issue #71 tracks this finding. **F3 HIGH. `match` builds and calls two closures at every evaluation** (`emit.py:2256` and `bind_payload` at `:2199`). `match o { Some(v) => v, None => 0 }` emits `(lambda match: ((lambda v: v)(match) if match is not None else 0))(o)`: one function object for the scrutinee bind, one more for the taken arm's payload bind, and two frames entered, per evaluation. Measured 15 vs 5 ops and **3 vs 1 frames** per match; in a loop over 400 optionals, 1068 vs 2 calls (534x) and 3.57x `tracemalloc` peak. `match` is the idiomatic way to consume `Opt` and `Result`, so this is on every such path. **The fix is already in the file**: `_match_expr` takes an `awaited` flag and, when set, emits a walrus bind instead (`((match := scrut), body)[1]`) precisely because a lambda cannot host an `await`. Using the walrus form unconditionally, or better `(body if (match := scrut) is not None else other)` which needs no tuple either, removes both allocations and both frames. *Blast radius:* goldens for every program with a `match`; self-host port; no semantic change (the sync and awaited forms are documented as otherwise byte-identical in intent). **RE-CHECK 2026-09-02:** the anchors moved. `bind_payload` is at `backends/python/emit.py:2218` (cites `:2199`) and the `match` arm is at `:2432` (cites `:2256`); the non-awaited branch at `:2224` still returns `(lambda {bind}: {body})({payload})`. **ACCEPTED RESIDUAL — issue #71 CLOSED on this basis 2026-09-08 (not fixed).** The remaining tax is one interpreter frame per payload bind read from inside a larger body. The walrus that removes it writes the bind through to an enclosing local of the same name, so it is sound ONLY when that name is bound nowhere else in the function (no `let`, `for`, arrow param or sibling/nested arm bind of it — the item-163 shadow/nested/arrow shapes). The REFERENCE emitter can decide that from one module-level binder tally set at each function entry: a small, clean change, verified out-of-tree to emit the walrus `(((v := match), <body>)[1] …)` for a unique bind, keep the lambda for the three item-163 shapes, and execute to the same value. But `selfhost/emit_py.rvl` has NO module state, so the byte-agreement oracle (`tests/test_selfhost_emit_py.py`, which diverges immediately on `control.rvl` / `branches.rvl` / `services_match.rvl` the moment the reference flips) forces the same tally to be THREADED through that emitter's `expr` (112 call sites) and `cexpr` (52 call sites) recursions plus their helpers, and mirrored as a `collect_bound_names` walk over the `value_*` IR API — a ~180-edit deep-recursion signature refactor graded byte-exact over the whole corpus including the arbitrary whole-tree survey fixtures. Disproportionate self-host churn for one frame per match; accepted. Pinned at its accepted value by `tests/test_71_codegen_perf_findings.py::test_accepted_residual_match_payload_bind_keeps_its_lambda` and the unique-bind case in `tests/test_163_match_payload_bind_scope.py::test_a_bind_read_inside_a_larger_body_keeps_its_binder`; a future removal must flip both deliberately.
    **🟡 HALF CLOSED 2026-09-02 (`fix/436-python-codegen-perf`): the helper FRAME is gone, the dynamic `isinstance` is not.** **F4 MED/HIGH. Every record field read is a helper call with an `isinstance` in it** (`emit.py:2394` fn bodies, `:1173` component bodies). `p.x` emits `_revl_field(p, 'x')`, which is 13 vs 3 ops and **2 frames vs 1** against the `p['x']` a developer writes; a two-field loop over 400 records measures 2.81x ops and 1602 vs 2 calls. The `isinstance(v, dict)` branch exists because "record literals are dicts, ADT payloads are objects", but note what the audit found while checking that: `type Row = { … }` emits a `@dataclass Row` that is **never instantiated anywhere** (a record literal of that type emits a plain dict), so for declared record types the dataclass is dead code and the dynamic branch is answering a question the type already answered. *Fix:* a frontend marker on a field node whose target type is a declared or structural record (exact precedent: the existing `sized_length` and `opt` markers on the same node), lowering to `target['name']`. *Blast radius:* large golden delta; self-host port; the `_revl_field` helper stays for the genuinely dynamic case (a host object returned by an extern). **RE-CHECK 2026-09-02:** the two sites are `backends/python/emit.py:2393` and `:1172` (cites `:2394`/`:1173`, off by one). **RE-CHECK 2026-09-03:** the `@dataclass` half of the argument is gone with F9 — a declared record type now emits a decorator-free shape class — and the finding is UNCHANGED by that, because the dynamic `isinstance` is still answering a question the type already answered. What F9 removed was the misleading signal, not the dispatch. **ACCEPTED / DEFERRED RESIDUAL — issue #71 CLOSED on this basis 2026-09-08.** The helper frame is gone; the surviving `isinstance(p, dict)` field-read dispatch cannot be removed by a codegen change, because a field read off a record-typed value must still tolerate a host object an extern handed back under that type (`src/revl/typecheck.py`: an extern's return provenance is deliberately opaque, on the G8 audit surface not the checked one). Removing it needs a value-PROVENANCE fact on the field node (the value was BUILT here, as a dict — precedent: the existing `sized_length`/`opt` markers), which is a FRONTEND change and its own tracked follow-up, out of scope for a codegen-only close. Pinned at its accepted value by `tests/test_71_codegen_perf_findings.py::test_accepted_residual_record_field_read_dispatches_dynamically`.
    **✅ CLOSED 2026-09-02 (`fix/436-python-codegen-perf`), see the fix note below.** Issue #71 tracks this finding. **F5 MED. Every bounded arithmetic operation costs a Python frame** (`emit.py:2302`/`:2307`). `a + b` on `Int` emits `_revl_i64(a + b)`, and the calls nest: `total += i * i - i` emits `_revl_i64(total + _revl_i64(_revl_i64(i * i) - i))`, three frames for one statement. The arith benchmark measures **8002 vs 2 calls** for 2000 iterations. In ops the gap is small (14 vs 13 for the inline form) because the helper body is counted either way; the real cost is the frame, which `ops` under-prices, so the honest statement is "one extra Python frame per bounded `+`/`-`/`*`" rather than a multiplier. *Fix:* emit the check inline as an expression, `(_r if -(2**63) <= (_r := a + b) <= 2**63 - 1 else _revl_i64(_r))`, keeping the helper only for the trapping path; the emitter already uses a walrus for exactly this reason in `is_alpha`/`is_alnum` (`:632`). *Blast radius:* very large golden delta (bounded arithmetic is everywhere); self-host port; nested uses need distinct walrus temps, which is the one real implementation risk. **RE-CHECK 2026-09-02:** `backends/python/emit.py:2302` unchanged.
    **✅ CLOSED 2026-09-02 (`fix/436-python-codegen-perf`), see the fix note below.** **F6 MED. Eight builtins build a fresh lambda and immediately apply it, at every evaluation.** Issue #71 tracks this finding. `%` (`:2322`), `div_trunc` (`:664`), `div_euclid` (`:669`), the four `checked_*` divisions (`:696`), `Str.indexOf` (`:604`), `Str.split` (`:608`) and `Str.to_int` (`:683`) all emit `(lambda _a, _b: …)(x, y)`. Each evaluation is one function-object allocation plus one frame, where a module-level `def` emitted once in the preamble is one frame and no allocation, and an inline conditional is neither. Measured: `%` 17 vs 15 ops and 2 vs 2 frames (the hand-written comparator keeps a helper, so the saved item is the allocation); `Str.indexOf` 16 vs 10 ops and 2 vs 1 frames; `Str.split` 14 vs 8 and 2 vs 1. Whole-program: `divmod.churn` 1.92x ops and 3.00x calls, `strings.split_join` 3.17x ops. *Fix:* hoist each into a gated preamble helper, exactly as `_revl_div` and `_revl_ftoa` already are, or inline the ones that fit an expression. *Blast radius:* goldens for programs using those builtins; self-host port; the gating machinery (`_uses_*`) already exists. **RE-CHECK 2026-09-02:** all eight `(lambda _a, _b: ...)` sites unchanged (`backends/python/emit.py:664`, `:669`, `:705`, `:708`, `:2322` and the `Str` ones).
    **✅ LANDED 2026-09-02 (`fix/436-python-codegen`), see the fix note below.** Issue #71 tracks the rest of this item. **F7 MED. `??`, `?.` and `?.()` render their left operand TWICE, which is a correctness defect before it is a performance one** (`emit.py:2295`/`:1182`, `:2454`/`:1241`, `:2458`/`:1248`). `lookup(k) ?? 0` emits `(0 if lookup(k) is None else lookup(k))`. Executed with a call-counting extern: **200 extern invocations per 100 uses** where a developer writes one. Any left operand that is a call, an index or an interpolation is evaluated twice; for anything with an observable effect that is a semantic bug, not a tax. Measured 31 vs 19 ops and 3 vs 2 frames. *Fix:* the walrus, `(v if (v := <lhs>) is not None else <rhs>)`, again with in-file precedent at `:632`. *Blast radius:* goldens wherever `??`/`?.` appear; self-host port. **py is the OUTLIER here, not the shared shape**: the ts tier emits JS's own `??`, and the rust tier emits `unwrap_or_else` with a comment explaining that the right operand must stay lazy (`backends/rust/emit.py:4311`); only py duplicates the left. *Noted while checking that, as a HYPOTHESIS and not executed:* the go tier emits `revlOptOr(left, right)` (`backends/go/emit.py:3797`), and Go evaluates call arguments eagerly, so `a ?? f()` on go would call `f()` even when `a` is present, which is the laziness the rust lowering deliberately preserves. Worth a cross-tier test either way. **RE-CHECK 2026-09-02:** unchanged; this is a CORRECTNESS defect first, so it should not be scheduled as performance work.
    **✅ CLOSED 2026-09-02 (`fix/436-python-codegen-perf`), see the fix note below.** **F8 LOW/MED. `Some(x)` allocates a function object to apply the identity** Issue #71 tracks this finding. (`emit.py:2285`/`:1155`): a bare `Some` renders `(lambda _v: _v)`, so `Some(x)` becomes `(lambda _v: _v)(x)`, measured 7 vs 1 ops and 2 vs 1 frames. `Opt[T]` is `T | None` at runtime, so the argument itself is the answer. *Fix:* special-case a `call` node whose callee is `var Some` and emit the argument. *Blast radius:* small and local; goldens only where `Some(` appears; self-host port. **RE-CHECK 2026-09-02:** `backends/python/emit.py:1155` and `:2285` both still return `(lambda _v: _v)`.
    **✅ CLOSED 2026-09-03 (`fix/436-python-codegen-rest`), see the fix note below.** **F9 LOW. A declared record type emits a `@dataclass` that nothing ever constructs**, plus `from dataclasses import dataclass` and `from typing import …`, in every module that declares one. Load-time only, but it is also what makes F4's dynamic field read look necessary when it is not. **RE-CHECK 2026-09-02:** `@dataclass` still emitted at `backends/python/emit.py:2088` and `:2109`.
    **✅ LANDED 2026-09-02 (`fix/436-python-codegen`), see the fix note below.** **FLAGGED IN PASSING, NOT A PERFORMANCE FINDING.** `s?.length()` where `s: Opt[Str]` emits `(None if (s) is None else (s).length())`. Executed: `AttributeError: 'str' object has no attribute 'length'`. The `optcall` path (`emit.py:2458`, and `:1248` in component bodies) never routes through `_render_builtin`, so every stdlib method reached through `?.` emits a host method that does not exist. `tests/fixtures/emit_py_corpus/optionals.rvl` contains exactly this line and the corpus proves byte-agreement with the self-host emitter without ever executing it, which is item 429's category (i) again: both sides agree and both are wrong. **RE-CHECK 2026-09-02, AND IT IS STILL A CORRECTNESS DEFECT, NOT A PERFORMANCE ONE:** `backends/python/emit.py:2455-2458` (and `:1242` in component bodies) still emits `(None if ({target}) is None else ({target}).{method}(...))` with no route through `_render_builtin`, and `tests/fixtures/emit_py_corpus/optionals.rvl:1` still holds the `s?.length()` line the corpus pins as correct.
    **NEGATIVE RESULTS (what was measured and is FINE).** The `Str` builtins that map to a native operation are byte-for-byte the hand-written spelling and cost the same: `length`, `slice`, `charAt`, `charCodeAt`, `codepoint_at`, `concat`, `repeat`, `join`, `startsWith`, `endsWith`, `to_str`. The character classifiers `is_digit`/`is_alpha`/`is_alnum`/`is_space` are already inline with a walrus binding, no lambda and no call, which is precisely why F5/F6/F7 read as oversights rather than policy: the emitter demonstrably knows the technique. `Map.lookup`/`has`/`size`/`keys` lower to `.get`/`in`/`len`/`sorted`, identical to hand-written. `_revl_div` and `_revl_ftoa` are preamble helpers defined once and called once per use, which is the correct shape and the model F6 should follow. Control flow (`while`, `for`, `if`, `break`, `continue`), indexing, and `Int32` bitwise `&`/`|`/`^`/`>>`/`~` (native, no re-wrap) are all hand-written-equivalent. An arrow binds one lambda per enclosing call, not per element, so `xs.map(f)` builds `f` once. String interpolation in a fn body renders a `'a=' + str(x)` concatenation with no runtime indirection. `records.shift_all` peak memory is 0.98x, confirming the record-update problem is copies, not live set. On the COMPONENT surface the per-activation `class _Calc` is built once per activation rather than per request, and the per-request closures (`_effect_N`, the `undo` and `compensate` lambdas) capture request state, so a hand-written cordis-py plugin would build the same objects; no waste found, but note this one is a code-shape review and NOT a measurement, because cordis-py is not installed in the interpreter the audit ran under, so no component could be activated. The whole `test`/`fault_test`/`lifecycle test` emission surface, async coloring, realm placement and spawn were not examined at all.
    **LANDED 2026-09-02 (`fix/436-python-codegen`): F7, the FLAGGED-IN-PASSING `?.` crash, and F1.**
    *F7 and the crash, one commit.* `??`, `?.` and `?.()` now bind a non-trivial left operand with a walrus and read the bound name in both the presence test and the result, so `lookup(k) ?? 0` runs its extern 1000 times per 1000 uses where it ran 1500. A bare local or literal keeps the plain double render, which costs nothing and leaves the common spelling unchanged. The temp is named after the OPT-CHAIN HEIGHT of the whole node, which is strictly greater than that of every opt node beneath it: `join` and `indexOf` render their ARGUMENT before the receiver, so a chain nested in an argument would otherwise clobber the binding above it, and the height is a function of the node alone so nothing is threaded through the expression walk. The `optcall` path now routes through `_render_builtin`, which is where it always belonged (the checker types an optcall through `builtin_check`, so the method is a stdlib builtin and there is no host-method row to reach); `to_int` is the one row that dispatches on a receiver type the optcall node does not carry, so it decides at runtime. `tests/fixtures/emit_py_corpus/optionals.rvl` now EXECUTES rather than only emitting, which closes this instance of item 429's category (i), and it covers the nested-argument temp collision.
    *The go hypothesis F7 filed is CONFIRMED, and it is the mirror of the python defect, not the same one.* Executed, not read: `backends/go/emit.py:3888` still emits `revlOptOr(left, right)` for the v3 tier, Go evaluates both call arguments before entering the function, and a 1000-iteration `lookup(k) ?? fallback(k)` built with `go run` against a counting `fallback` reports **1000 invocations where 500 are needed** (the 500 present cases must not call it at all). The returned value is correct, so nothing but an effect makes it visible. python over-evaluated the LEFT operand; go over-evaluates the RIGHT, which is exactly the laziness `backends/rust/emit.py`'s `unwrap_or_else` comment says it is preserving on purpose. NOT FIXED HERE, deliberately: the go tier already contains the right shape in its other dialect (the v2 component `??` at `:3550` emits an inline `func() T { _v, _ok := ..; if _ok { return _v }; return <right> }()`, which IS lazy), so the fix is to use that shape or a `revlOptOrElse` taking a thunk, but it moves go goldens and needs the `selfhost/emit_go.rvl` port checked, and item 434's agent is in that file. Reproducer: any `fn resolve(k: Int) -> Int { return lookup(k) ?? fallback(k) }` with a counting `@go` extern.
    *F1, one ownership analysis rather than a rewrite per builtin.* A local may be written in place exactly when the object it names is reachable through no other name. Two clauses carry that: it must be BORN in this fn (a fresh literal, or another name materialised as ONE defensive copy at birth where the persistent form made one per write), and it must never ESCAPE (a call argument, a list or record element, an arrow capture, a `for` iterable, a `??` operand and a plain alias all retain; a parameter is never a birth, because it is the caller's object). Element copies at n=1000, emitted against hand-written: `list_build.build` **500500 to 1000** (hand-written 1000), `transforms.doubled` **500500 to 1000**, `maps.fill` **500500 to 1000**, `maps.drop_keys` **499500 to 2000** (2000), `records.shift_all` **503500 to 4000** (4000). Five of the six copy probes now match the hand-written column exactly and grow x2.0 per doubling instead of x4.0. This also takes F2's quadratic (`drop_keys` was the 461.94x ops case), though F2's `dict(<generator>)` spelling is untouched where the rebuild still happens.
    *Left, deliberately.* `transforms.list_sort` is unchanged, and so is `list_dedup`; both need the refinements filed as item 445. F2's dict-comprehension spelling, F3 (`match`'s two closures), F4, F5, F6, F8 and F9 are untouched. `Str` accumulation is out on this tier for the reason the go tier hit on the same defect: a python `str` cannot be mutated, and `concat`'s receiver type is not known at that node. The analysis lives in `backends/python/emit.py` rather than the frontend, so the go tier's `_v3_self_rebind_locals` (item 434) is the same rule written a second time; see item 445 for the argument to lift it. All of it is ported to `selfhost/emit_py.rvl` and checked directly against the source, per item 429's standing rule; `fn_stmt` there takes the analysis as two parameters because it sees one step while the proof needs the whole body.
    **LANDED 2026-09-02 (`fix/436-python-codegen-perf`): F5, F4's frame, F3's scrutinee closure, F6, F2's spelling and F8.** Every number below is the same `PYTHONPATH=src python bench/codegen/python/run.py` table, taken on `origin/main` and on the branch. **NO TIMING IS QUOTED, and the reason is not caution but the reading**: the host was at load average 38 on 10 cores while this ran, which is the condition the harness README says makes even an interleaved A/B ratio noise. The headline is `calls`, Python frames entered: **58284 across the 17 cases, down to 16485**, against 13614 for the hand-written comparators. Twelve of the seventeen cases now enter EXACTLY as many frames as the hand-written arm.
    *F5, and the one honest regression in the set.* `a + b` on `Int` emits `(_bi if _REVL_I64_MIN <= (_bi := a + b) <= _REVL_I64_MAX else _revl_i64(_bi))`; the helper stays as the trapping tail, so the raise and its message are still written once, and it is still gated by `_uses_bounded_int`. One temp name serves any nesting depth, because the walrus sits in a conditional expression's CONDITION, which python evaluates before either branch: an inner operation has finished reading `_bi` before an outer one rebinds it, and the outer branch reads what the outer bind just wrote. `arith.sum_squares` **8002 frames to 2** (hand-written 2), `list_build.build` 402 to 2, `maps.fill` 402 to 2, `maps.probe` 402 to 2, `transforms.list_sort` 30090 to 9685 (hand-written 9685, so 3.11x to 1.00x). **It costs about two executed opcodes per bounded operation**, which is why `arith` moves 1.22x to 1.29x in `ops` and `maps.fill` 3.41x to 3.56x: the inline form loads two module constants where the call loaded one global. That is the trade the finding predicted (`ops` under-prices a frame), and it is recorded here rather than buried, because a reader comparing the two `ops` columns will otherwise think something broke.
    *F4's frame, not F4's `isinstance`.* `p.x` emits `(_fv['x'] if isinstance((_fv := p), dict) else getattr(_fv, 'x'))`, and the `_revl_field` preamble helper, which was the ONE helper emitted unconditionally into every module, is gone with it (`_revl_opt_field` and its `_uses_opt_field` gate go too). Per read that is 13 ops and 2 frames down to 11 ops and 1 frame, against 2 and 1 hand-written. `records.total` **1602 frames to 2** and `records.shift_all` 802 to 2. What is LEFT is exactly the dynamic dispatch the finding said only a type could remove: `records.total` is unchanged at 2.74x `ops`. So the frontend marker F4 proposed is still worth its own item, and the frame it would have paid for is no longer part of its case.
