    *F3's scrutinee, not F3's payload.* The bind rides the FIRST arm's test, `((lambda v: v)(match) if (match := o) is not None else 0)`, and the `(<bind>, <body>)[1]` tuple is kept only for a leading wildcard arm, which has no test to carry it. `match` is a revl keyword, so no user binding can collide, and a nested match may reuse the name because the outer chain has finished reading it before any body runs. `matching.sum_present` **1068 frames to 268** and `classify` 5 to 3. **The PAYLOAD bind keeps its lambda, and this is a correctness result, not an oversight**: `let v = 5; let r = match o { Some(v) => v * 2, None => 0 }; return r + v` compiles today, so an arm bind may SHADOW an enclosing local, and a walrus payload bind would write through to it. The lambda is what makes the shadow work. Two more cases break the naive walrus: a nested match binding the same name inside an arm body, and an arrow inside an arm body capturing the bind (with a walrus every closure would share one cell). Deciding it needs the set of names bound in the ENCLOSING FUNCTION, which neither `_match_expr` nor its self-host mirror is given, so it is a threading change and not a spelling one. **Note while there: the `awaited` payload binder, which has spelled itself with a walrus since item 263, carries that shadowing bug today.** It is not exercised by any fixture and was not fixed here; it belongs with whatever closes the sync half.
    *F6, all eight, as gated preamble `def`s.* `%`, `div_trunc`, `div_euclid`, the four `checked_*` divisions, `Str.indexOf`, `Str.split` and `Str.to_int` (plus the `?.`-reached variant of the last) now lower to `_revl_rem`, `_revl_div_trunc`, `_revl_div_euclid`, `_revl_checked_*`, `_revl_index_of`, `_revl_split`, `_revl_str_to_int` and `_revl_opt_to_int`, each emitted only where the IR uses it. One frame and no function-object allocation, where a lambda built and applied was one frame AND one allocation. `divmod.churn` **8993 frames to 2999** (hand-written 2999, so 3.00x to 1.00x) and its `tracemalloc` peak 168 bytes to 0; `strings.scan` 1502 to 502 (1.00x) and 168 bytes to 0; `strings.split_join` 602 to 202 and 2.75x peak to 1.00x. The gate had to look at `optcall` nodes as well as `builtin` ones, because `?.m(..)` reaches the same render table by a different node kind.
    *F2 and F8.* `Map.remove` rebuilds through `{kk: vv for kk, vv in m.items() if kk != k}`: 49 ops and 4 frames per call down to 39 and 1, which is the hand-written spelling exactly. `Some(x)` emits `x` (a bare `Some` passed as a value still renders the identity lambda, which is the only place it is needed): 6 ops and 2 frames down to 0 and 1.
    *Ported and checked.* All of it is in `selfhost/emit_py.rvl` and `tests/test_selfhost_emit_py.py` agrees byte-for-byte over the whole corpus, including the preamble gating for the eight new helpers. `bench/codegen/python/micro.py` was ALSO stale: its emitted arms were hand-copied and still showed the pre-`fix/436-python-codegen` `??` double render, so it reported 200 extern calls per 100 uses for a defect that had already been fixed. Every row now carries the current spelling, and a row reading 1.0/1.0 is a closed finding.
    *Left open, and why (as of THAT branch; F9 and F3's binder-free arms closed on 2026-09-03, see the note below).* F9 (the `@dataclass` nothing constructs) is untouched. F4's dynamic `isinstance` and F3's payload lambda are the two halves described above. `transforms.list_sort` and `list_dedup` still need item 445. The `ops` gap that remains on `maps.fill` (3.56x), `strings.split_join` (3.16x) and `records.total` (2.74x) is now, in all three, the emitter writing a loop or a runtime dispatch where the hand-written arm writes a comprehension or knows the type; none of it is a frame or an allocation any more.
    **LANDED 2026-09-03 (`fix/436-python-codegen-rest`): F9, and the two match arm shapes that need no payload binder at all.** Same discipline as the note above: no duration is quoted, every number is executed bytecode instructions, Python frames entered, or `tracemalloc` peak, and `bench/codegen/python/run.py` reproduces all of them. The harness gained `--load`, which reports those three counters for the module LOAD rather than the run, because a declaration the emitter writes and nothing constructs is invisible to every other metric in the file: it is paid before the first call.
    *F9, and it was bigger than LOW suggested.* A record type emitted `@dataclass class Row:` for a class NOTHING ever constructs — a record value is a plain dict — so `dataclasses` built an `__init__`, `__repr__` and `__eq__` per declaration at every import. Executing `tests/fixtures/emit_py_corpus/types.rvl`'s three record declarations: **17246 ops, 468 frames and a 105973 B peak, down to 237, 9 and 14711**; `stdlib/fs.rvl`'s five: 23206 / 725 / 128125 down to 268 / 9 / 18951; `bench/codegen/python/programs/records.rvl`'s single `type Point`: 4543 / 142 / 88962 down to **78 / 3 / 3672**. The two imports were emitted whenever the module declared ANY type, so a variant-only module (`result.rvl`, `examples/regressions/fuzz_go_ead437e4.rvl`) paid both for annotations it could not contain; `from typing import …` is now gated on the names the record annotations actually mention and `from dataclasses import dataclass` is gone, which also drops a **cold `dataclasses` import measured at 82866 ops, 2250 frames and 836 KB** from every such module. The CLASS stays, without its decorator: the field names and types are what makes an emitted module readable next to the revl source, and the honest spelling of a shape nothing constructs is a shape. `Union` was in that import list and is not a name `_py_type` can render, so it was dead in the strongest sense. **The rejected alternative was PEP 563**, which is rejected for the same reason it always was, and the reason is now stated without `@dataclass` in it: `tests/test_pyemit_forward_refs.py` execs the emitted module anonymously, and forward-ref quoting is what keeps a class-body annotation from a NameError there. `backends/python/tests/test_reserved_word_idents.py` reads the item-165 injectivity pin off `__annotations__` instead of `dataclasses.fields()` — the same two names, the same guarantee.
    *F9's premise was wrong in SCOPE, and CI said so (fix on the same branch, 2026-09-03).* "Nothing instantiates it" was true INSIDE an emitted module and false outside it: `src/revl/fault.py::_make_record` built the class for every `prop test` and every item-60 auto-mock, and three `tests/test_v2_emit.py` cases built it by hand. Five tests reddened on `frontend (3.11)` and `frontend-cordis`. **The decision was to keep F9 and fix the callers, and the reason is not the measurement — it is that the class CANNOT be a record value.** A field's class attribute is `_mangle`d for Python keyword collisions (item 165) while its runtime key is the raw revl name, so `type Q = { from: Str }` emits `class Q: from_: str` against a read of `q['from']`: an instance of `Q` answers no field read the emitter writes. The alternative was priced rather than assumed — a hand-written `__init__` with no `@dataclass` costs **9 ops and 431 B** on `types.rvl` (242 -> 251), against 17286 / 469 / 106714 for the decorator, so F9's headline numbers were the `dataclasses` machinery almost entirely and option 2 was affordable. It was rejected as WRONG, not as expensive. `run.py --load` is byte-identical before and after this fix on all eight programs. **Keeping the class constructible had been hiding a live defect:** `_let_pattern_stmt` emitted `tmp.a` for a record destructure, which raises `AttributeError` on every record value the emitter itself produces (`let {id, name} = row` after `return { id: 4, name: "ab" }`); the only test covering it passed a constructed `Row(...)`. It now emits the same `_field_read` dispatch `row.a` does, so a k-field destructure costs exactly k field reads (34008 ops/1000 for both, measured). A 236-program emit sweep changes **zero** bytes and `tools/regen_goldens.py --check` reports all seven targets in sync — nothing in the corpus destructures a record, which is why the defect survived. `selfhost/emit_py.rvl` does not implement `let_pattern` at all, so no port and no oracle movement. The external contract is now WRITTEN DOWN, which is the actual root cause: docs/records.md §7 and the `_emit_types` header.
    *F3's payload bind: the two shapes that need no binder, not the general one.* An arm whose body IS the bind (`Some(v) => v`, `Ok(v) => v` — the unwrap `match` mostly exists for) is now the payload expression itself, and an arm whose body never mentions the bind (`Err(e) => -1`) drops the binder AND the payload read, which is unobservable because the payload is `match` or a `__slots__` load on a case the `isinstance` already matched. **`matching.sum_present` goes from 268 Python frames to 2, equal to the hand-written arm**, with `ops` 12678 -> 11082 (1.56x -> 1.36x) and `tracemalloc` 168 B -> 0; `matching.classify` is unchanged, because it is the general shape. **Neither spelling introduces a name**, which is exactly why they are safe where the walrus this item proposed is not: there is nothing to shadow an enclosing local with, nothing for a nested match to rebind, and no cell for an arrow to share — the three item-163 shapes, all still on their lambda and all still executed by `tests/test_163_match_payload_bind_scope.py`. So the enclosing function's bound names, which the exit condition asked for, turned out not to be needed for this half.
    *Why the general case is still open, and what it actually needs.* `Some(v) => v + 1` reads the bind from inside a larger body, so removing the binder means rendering that body with `v` replaced by the payload — an ALPHA-RENAME of the arm body before `_expr` sees it. The reference emitter could do that in a dozen lines. `selfhost/emit_py.rvl` cannot: it reads the IR through `stdlib/value.rvl`, whose API is read-only (`value_kind`/`value_keys`/`value_children`/`value_field` and the scalar exits) with no way to BUILD the rewritten node, and item 429 requires the two emitters to agree byte-for-byte. The options are a `value.rvl` constructor set, a frontend marker (the same shape F4's remaining half needs), or an `@py` extern doing IR surgery, which would be the wrong kind of bridge. Measured cost of leaving it: `bench/codegen/python/micro.py`'s new `match Some(v) => v + 1` row, 13 vs 7 ops and 2 vs 1 frames per evaluation.
    *Ported and checked, and the corpus had a hole.* Both changes are in `selfhost/emit_py.rvl`. The oracle was verified to actually cover them rather than trusted: reverting each port individually reddens `tests/test_selfhost_emit_py.py` (F9 on `types.rvl`; F3 on `control.rvl`, `mixed.rvl` and `result.rvl`). The COMPONENT-body match (`cmatch`/`cmatch_branch`, `_ComponentEmitter`'s arm) had **no corpus file at all**, so `services_match.rvl` was added FAILING FIRST — item 429 exit (3) — with one arm per shape the binder decides. It caught a real defect on the way in: `_ComponentEmitter` renders arm bodies BEFORE `_match_expr` sees them, so every arm read as "never mentions the bind" and a live bind was deleted (`examples/v3_step_scheduler.rvl` emitted `Final(msg)` with `msg` free). `_RenderedBody` now carries the node its text came from. **Goldens: none moved.** `tools/regen_goldens.py --check` reports all seven targets in sync, because the reference IR declares no record type and contains no arm of either shape.
    **Exit:** what is left is F4's dynamic field dispatch and F3's payload bind READ FROM INSIDE A LARGER BODY. F9 is closed. F4's remaining half is a frontend marker on a field node whose target type is a declared or structural record (exact precedent: the `sized_length` and `opt` markers on the same node), and it must be ported to `selfhost/lower.rvl` with its IR-agreement oracle. F3's remaining half needs the arm body ALPHA-RENAMED before it is rendered, which needs a way to build an IR node in `selfhost/emit_py.rvl` (a `stdlib/value.rvl` constructor set) or the same frontend marker F4 wants; its exit test is `matching.classify` at 2 frames while `let v = 5; let r = match o { Some(v) => v * 2, None => 0 }; return r + v` still returns 11 for `Some(3)`, since a wrong answer there is a silent shadowing bug and not a crash. `matching.sum_present` at 2 frames, the exit test as first written, is met. Every one of them needs the `selfhost/emit_py.rvl` port checked directly against the source rather than through the oracle (item 429 exit condition 1). Relates to 276 (the lexer perf work that first surfaced hot-loop codegen), 379 (break/continue, whose stated motivation included a hot-loop speedup), 383 (the map/filter/reduce trio F1 makes quadratic), 391 and 429 (self-host parity).

437. (issue #116) **RUST CODEGEN PERFORMANCE AUDIT: 57% OF EVERY `String::from` THE EMITTER WRITES IS A HEAP ALLOCATION FOR A LITERAL THAT IS NEVER STORED, AND ITEM 284's IN-PLACE SELF-APPEND MISSES THE SHAPE THE SELF-HOST COMPILER ACTUALLY USES (2026-09-02).** Audit of the rust `backends/rust/emit.py` EMITS, against the rust a competent developer would write by hand for the same semantics. Harness landed at `bench/codegen/rust/` (12 benchmark programs in revl, 12 hand-written comparators, a runner that builds both into ONE crate under a counting global allocator and asserts they agree before reporting, plus `census.py`, which counts each wasteful shape in the rust emitted for the four self-host stages that build). **NO WALL-CLOCK NUMBER WAS TAKEN and none is quoted here.** The machine was running about a dozen concurrent agents; a duration measured under unknown load is not evidence and neither is a ratio of two durations sampled at different moments, so `run.py` takes no timing unless `--timing` is passed and the evidence below is entirely allocation counts, heap bytes, generated-code census and complexity class, all of which are properties of the generated code and identical on an idle host. No instruction-count tool was available either (no valgrind, cachegrind or perf on this macOS arm64 host). A timing pass is a one-flag re-run on a quiet machine. Item 277 is the reason for that discipline: it records a plausible rust perf change here that REGRESSED the self-host lexer by 28%, which is exactly what an allocation count would have caught and an intuition did not.

    **The census, over 13,891 lines of rust emitted for `selfhost/{lexer,parser,checker,lower}.rvl`:** 4,895 `.clone()`, 3,920 `String::from(`, of which 1,235 are `&String::from(` (a literal allocated onto the heap to be handed to a helper whose parameter is `&str`) and 988 are `== String::from(` / `!= String::from(` (a literal allocated to be compared and dropped). Those 2,223 sites are 57% of every `String::from` in the emitted self-host compiler, and every one of them is a heap allocation whose value is never stored anywhere. Also 332 index reads cloned, 65 self-appends through `concat`, 3 for-loop iterable clones.

    (a) **✅ FIXED 2026-09-05 (`7831b8c5`); the finding as filed is kept below for the record, WAS OPEN AS RE-VERIFIED ON `2d2fa07f`.** **Self-append through `concat` is not covered by item 284, and it is the shape the self-host emitters use.** Measured on `bench/codegen/rust/programs/concat_append.rvl`: 729,599 heap allocations and 97.2 MB against 2,425 and 212 KB hand-written, so 301x the allocations and 458x the bytes, and O(n^2) copies against O(n) amortised. `_v3_self_append_inplace` (`backends/rust/emit.py:5049`) rewrites `out = out.push(x)` in place but dispatches through `_v3_inplace_persistent` (`:5039`), which lists only `push`/`set`/`remove`; item 284's own comment excludes `concat` because "its receiver type is not known at this node". For the SELF-ASSIGN shape that is no longer true: the receiver is a bare local, so `ctx.var_types[target_name]` resolves it, and item 284's liveness argument (the assign rebinds the receiver over its own value, so the pre-image is unreachable) carries over verbatim. `selfhost/emit_go.rvl`, `emit_java.rvl`, `emit_ts.rvl` and `checker.rvl` accumulate their output through exactly `out = out.concat(...)`; the census counts 65 such sites in the four stages that emit. The Str spelling `s = s + p` never reaches that function at all, because it is a `bin` node handled at `:4321` and turned into `format!("{}{}", s, p)`, a fresh String per iteration: measured separately at 5,999 allocations and 84.3 MB against 13 and 32 KB. **Fix:** add a `concat` arm to `_v3_inplace_persistent` split by receiver type (`v.extend(arg.iter().cloned())` for List, `s.push_str(&arg)` for Str) and a `+` arm to `_v3_self_append_inplace`. **Blast radius:** neither `backends/rust/golden/*.rs` contains the shape, so goldens are byte-unchanged. `tests/fixtures/emit_rust_corpus/{strings,control}.rvl` do not contain it either, which means the self-host byte-agreement oracle stays GREEN WITHOUT A PORT. That is the item-429 trap, not a free pass: port to `selfhost/emit_rust.rvl` AND add a corpus fixture, or the divergence is invisible by construction. **RE-CHECK 2026-09-02:** `_v3_self_append_inplace` is at `backends/rust/emit.py:5066` now (cites `:5049`).

    (b) **✅ FIXED 2026-09-05 (`fedec4ba`); the finding as filed is kept below for the record, WAS OPEN AS RE-VERIFIED ON `2d2fa07f`.** **String literals are allocated for slots that borrow.** `_rust_v3_lit` (`:823`) renders every Str literal as `String::from(..)` unconditionally. `_v3_builtin` (`:4735` onward) then writes `&{arg}` for `concat`/`indexOf`/`split`/`join`/`startsWith`/`endsWith`, whose helper-trait parameters are all `&str`, so the emitted code allocates a String purely to take a reference to it; and the `==`/`!=` fall-through at `:4343` compares against a freshly allocated `String` where `String: PartialEq<&str>` would compare in place. Measured: `literal_arg` 400,000 allocations and 5.50 MB against 40,000 and 0.67 MB (10x); `str_eq_literal` 240,000 allocations and 1.12 MB against ZERO and zero. Census: 1,235 + 988 = 2,223 sites. **Fix:** render a Str literal as the bare `&'static str` when the slot is one of `_BORROW_ARG_BUILTINS` or an `==`/`!=` operand whose other side is `Str`. **Blast radius:** no golden contains `&String::from(`; `backends/rust/test_emit_rust.py:1018` pins `m.remove(&String::from("a"))` and would need updating; no corpus fixture exercises it, so again port `selfhost/emit_rust.rvl:531` and add a fixture rather than trusting a green oracle. **RE-CHECK 2026-09-02:** `_rust_v3_lit` is at `backends/rust/emit.py:830` (cites `:823`) and its docstring still reads "strings are owned `String`".

    (c) **✅ FIXED 2026-09-05 (`8a5d443d`); the finding as filed is kept below for the record, WAS OPEN AS RE-VERIFIED ON `2d2fa07f`.** **`Str.indexOf` materialises BOTH operands as `Vec<char>` on every call.** The emitted prelude (`:4877`) opens with `let hay: Vec<char> = self.chars().collect(); let nee: Vec<char> = needle.chars().collect();` and then scans naively. Measured: 1,500 allocations and 12.97 MB for 300 calls over a 10.8 KB haystack, against ZERO and zero hand-written. **Fix:** `self.find(needle).map(|b| self[..b].chars().count() as i64).unwrap_or(-1)`, which preserves the codepoint-index contract on every input (empty needle gives 0, absent gives -1, non-ASCII gives the codepoint index) and gets `str::find`'s two-way search instead of the O(n*m) walk. **This is the cheapest fix in the item and has the SMALLEST blast radius of any of them:** one function in the prelude, no golden contains the prelude (both checked-in goldens are stdlib-free per item 282), and `selfhost/emit_rust.rvl` does not carry the prelude text at all, so no self-host port is needed. Take this one first. **RE-CHECK 2026-09-02:** the prelude's `let hay: Vec<char>` is at `backends/rust/emit.py:4896` (cites `:4877`).

    (d) **❌ STILL OPEN, RE-VERIFIED IN SOURCE ON `837c7035` (2026-09-05; PR #423 "rust codegen: fix 437 d/e/f" still OPEN, not merged; `backends/rust/emit.py:5023` still `.clone()`s).** **Every `List` index read clones the element.** `:4502` emits `({target})[({index}) as usize].clone()` unconditionally, so `xs[i] == probe` allocates a String, compares it and drops it. Measured on `list_index.rvl`: 150,000 allocations and 572 KB against ZERO and zero. Census: 332 sites. **Fix:** reuse item 282's read-only-position classification (a `==` operand, a `&str` builtin receiver or argument, an interpolation operand) and borrow there. Larger analysis than (a) to (c), and it does touch goldens and needs the `emit_rust.rvl` port. **RE-CHECK 2026-09-02:** `backends/rust/emit.py:4519` still emits `({target})[({index}) as usize].clone()` (cites `:4502`). **LANDED toward #116 (`fix/issue-116-census-v2`): the field-off-an-index subset, which the census finds is the ENTIRE remaining population of this waste.** The `==`/`!=`-operand and builtin-receiver halves for a BARE index read were already on main (`_v3_strip_index_clone`, and the `list_index` bench sat at 0 allocations); what still cloned was a FIELD read off an index (`toks[i].kind == "kw"`), which cloned the whole element, every sibling field, to move one field out and compare it. The `field` render now clones just the field read (`(toks[i]).kind.clone()`), strictly less copied and valid in every position since the result stays owned, and `_v3_strip_index_clone`, generalized to a field-off-an-index node, drops even that field clone in the three read-only slots (a `==`/`!=` operand, a builtin receiver, and an interpolation operand). Census over the four self-host stages: index-read whole-element clones 452 -> 224, and every one of the 228 field-off-an-index sites is gone; the 224 that remain are bare element reads in genuinely OWNED positions (`return xs[i]`, `let a = xs[i]`), which a hand-written rust program clones too, and the derived gate crate's own self-host emit dropped 138 such whole-element clones to 0. Bench `field_index` (`xs[i].field == "lit"` read in a hot loop, the per-read clone isolated from the one-time record build): 315,013 heap allocations and 1,907,568 bytes -> 15,013 and 465,328, byte-identical to the hand-written comparator (21x -> 1.00x), the 15,013 residual being the record construction both sides share. NO WALL CLOCK WAS TAKEN, on the audit's own reasoning. **BYTE-AGREEMENT KEPT:** the field render, the strip generalization, and the interpolation strip are mirrored into `selfhost/emit_rust.rvl` (`render_field`, `strip_index_clone`, `render_interp`), and `tests/fixtures/emit_rust_corpus/perf_field_index.rvl` is the new fixture pinning both tiers over the read-only and owned shapes (item 429). Goldens are byte-unchanged (neither carries an index read). Both gate crates were regenerated and their drift gates are green. **VERIFIED:** `tests/test_selfhost_emit_rust.py` in full (byte-agreement over all 20 corpus fixtures plus `test_selfhost_stages_cargo_build_and_run`, which builds and runs the four self-host stages natively over the changed sites), the crate/wasm drift gates, and `bench/codegen/rust/run.py` (all thirteen build and agree). **STILL OPEN in (d):** a `&str` builtin ARGUMENT that is itself an index read (the receiver and the equality-operand slots are done, this argument slot is not), plus (e) and (g).

    (e) **✅ CLOSED 2026-09-08 as ACCEPTED-WITH-MEASURED-REASON (`fix/issue-116-perf-e-g-close`); the finding as filed is kept below for the record, WAS OPEN AS RE-VERIFIED ON `837c7035`.** **DECIDED: not fixed, because the only zero-allocation form is the calling-convention change item 277 recorded regressing the lexer.** The win is real and re-measured on this branch (`bench/codegen/rust/run.py const_list`: 720,000 heap allocations and 20,360,000 bytes against ZERO and zero hand-written, matching the audit). But revl `List` lowers to an OWNED `Vec<String>` with no Rc/persistent backing at the rust level, so NO clone-based or memoized `Vec<String>` return recovers those allocations — a `Vec<String>` clone re-allocates all 35 Strings, verified by inspection. The only zero-alloc form returns `&'static [&'static str]`, which changes the element type from `String` to `&'static str` and therefore the calling convention at every site the result flows to; the frontend assigns the result `List[Str]` (= `Vec<String>`), so every owned or unifying use (`let k = keywords()`, a `List[Str]` argument/return, a persistent `push`) stops compiling under the borrow representation. That is exactly the class of change item 277 measured REGRESSING the self-host lexer by 28%, and (e) was already deferred on that ground. The decision is made EXECUTABLE, not just noted: `tests/test_116_rust_perf_accepted.py` pins that `keywords()` still emits the 35-`String::from` rebuild and NOT a `&'static [&'static str]` table, so a future hoist flips that test on purpose; `bench/codegen/rust/census.py` gains a `constant list of literals rebuilt per call` shape that keeps the footprint (fourteen such fns summed across the four self-host stages) visible in the standing census; and the `const_list` bench + hand-written comparator are guarded present. Union with (d)'s argument slot (PR #739) and (g) closes issue #116. **The finding as filed:** A param-less fn whose body is a constant list of literals is rebuilt on every call. `selfhost/lexer.rvl`'s `fn keywords() -> List[Str]` is consulted per identifier token and emits 35 `String::from` plus a `Vec` every time. Measured on `const_list.rvl`: 720,000 allocations and 20.36 MB for 20,000 lookups against a 35-entry table, versus ZERO and zero for the `static KEYWORDS: &[&str]` a rust developer writes. revl `List` is persistent with no in-place mutation, so a shared immutable table is observationally identical. Ranked below (a) to (d) on EFFORT, not on win: hoisting it properly is a return-type change (`&'static [&'static str]` plus a read-only surface for it), which is the same class of calling-convention change item 282 took for `&str` params and item 277 showed can go the wrong way if done naively. **RE-CHECK 2026-09-02:** unchanged.

    (f) **✅ FIXED 2026-09-06; the finding as filed is kept below for the record, WAS OPEN AS RE-VERIFIED ON `837c7035`.** **`for x of v` clones the iterable whenever `v` is referenced more than once ANYWHERE in the body.** `:5181` sends the iterable through `_by_value_tail`, which consults `ctx.multi_use`, built at `:5359` as a whole-body reference COUNT with no liveness. So building an accumulator and then walking it once clones the whole thing, including every String field, because the item-284 in-place `push` receiver still counts as a by-value use. Measured on `for_over_local.rvl`: 120,016 allocations and 4.38 MB against 60,015 and 2.28 MB, exactly 2.00x. Census finds only 3 sites in the self-host stages, so the real-world win is small and it is ranked last among the fixes. **Narrow provable fix:** move rather than clone when every other reference to the name is in a statement strictly BEFORE the loop and the loop is not itself nested inside another loop (a move inside an outer loop would fail on the second iteration). **RE-CHECK 2026-09-02:** `_by_value_tail` is reached from `backends/rust/emit.py:5080` and `:5153` (cites `:5181`) and `ctx.multi_use` is still a whole-body reference count with no liveness. **LANDED 2026-09-06 (`fix/issue-116-rust-perf-remainder`):** `_movable_for_iterables` (`backends/rust/emit.py`) marks each `for` node whose bare-name iterable is DEAD after the loop — the iterable is a bare local, the `for` is not nested in another loop or a closure, and the count of the name's references strictly before the `for` in pre-order plus the one iterable reference equals its whole-body reference count — and the `for` emit renders that iterable as a plain move instead of routing it through `_by_value_tail`'s multi-use clone. Pre-order position is a conservative proxy for "executes before"; mutually-exclusive branch siblings can only make the check fail, never wrongly pass. **`for_over_local` 120,016 allocations and 4.38 MB -> 60,015 and 2.28 MB, exactly the hand-written comparator (1.00x)**; `bench/codegen/rust/run.py` asserts both sides still agree on the result. **NO SELF-HOST PORT NEEDED:** `selfhost/emit_rust.rvl` never cloned a `for` iterable (it renders `for ${bind} in ${render_expr(iterable)}`), so the reference change CONVERGES to it — the movable case now emits byte-identically on both tiers. Before the fix the reference cloned and diverged here with no fixture to catch it (the item-429 trap), so `tests/fixtures/emit_rust_corpus/perf_iter.rvl` is the new byte-agreement fixture. The reused-BUT-live clone and the nested-in-loop clone (both of which the port cannot mirror, since it does not clone at all) are pinned in `backends/rust/test_emit_rust.py` instead, with a `cargo check` that both compile. Goldens are byte-unchanged (their only `for` is `for _ in 0..200`, a range).

    (g) **✅ CLOSED 2026-09-08 as a DOCUMENTED NO-OP (`fix/issue-116-perf-e-g-close`); the finding as filed is kept below for the record, WAS OPEN AS RE-VERIFIED ON `837c7035`.** **DECIDED: no fix, because there is nothing to allocate.** Re-measured on this branch (`bench/codegen/rust/run.py loop_length`): ZERO heap allocations on BOTH the emitted and the hand-written side, so the finding carries no allocation evidence and — on the audit's own no-wall-clock discipline — no timing either. It is a pure complexity note (`while (i < s.revl_length())` recomputes `chars().count()`, O(n) per iteration, so O(n^2)) and small in practice (3 `Str`-receiver sites; the rest are `Vec`, where `revl_length` is `self.len()`, O(1)); the audit ranked it last and re-proposed no fix. Closed as a no-op with the shape kept on file executably: `tests/test_116_rust_perf_accepted.py` pins that the `while` condition still recomputes `revl_length()` and does not pre-hoist it, so any future loop-invariant hoist flips that test deliberately. **The finding as filed:** `Str.length()` in a `while` condition is recomputed per iteration. ANALYTIC ONLY, NOT MEASURED. `revl_length` on a `str` is `chars().count()`, so an O(n) walk runs n times and the loop is O(n^2). There is NO allocation evidence for this (0 versus 0 on both sides) and no timing was taken, so it is a complexity argument, not a measurement, and it is labelled as such. It is also SMALL in practice: the census finds 746 `while (i < x.revl_length())` sites but only 3 with a `Str` receiver, the other 743 being `Vec`, where `revl_length` is `self.len() as i64` and O(1). Recorded so the shape is on file, ranked last of all. **RE-CHECK 2026-09-02:** `chars().count()` is at `backends/rust/emit.py:4891`.

    **NEGATIVE RESULTS, measured and fine.** (1) `out = out.push(x)`, item 284's own shape, is EXACTLY what a rust developer writes: 17 allocations and 2,097,152 bytes on BOTH sides, identical to the byte. Item 284 holds and this benchmark is now its regression guard. (2) Pure `Int` arithmetic costs the emitter nothing extra: 0 allocations both sides, and the emitted `checked_add(..).expect(..)` is character-for-character what the comparator has to write, because trapping `Int` is revl SEMANTICS and not emitter waste. There is nothing to win on arithmetic. (3) The `Arc<Box<dyn Svc>>` double indirection on every `requires` is NOT emitter waste: `cordis::Context::require<T>` is bounded `T: Send + Sync + 'static` with no `?Sized`, so `require::<dyn Model>` does not compile against the pinned cordis-rs 0.3.0 and `Box<dyn Model>` is the only expressible form. Removing the extra pointer chase means adding `?Sized` upstream in cordis-rs, per the fork-and-PR rule for the rs runtime, not changing the emitter. (Noted in passing: `backends/rust/emit.py`'s own module docstring claims it emits `ctx.provide_arc("key", Arc::new(impl) as Arc<dyn Svc>)`, which is not what it emits; the docstring is stale.) (4) The item-277/282 `slice(i, i+1)` front-walk residual is real and re-measured at 8,000 allocations against 3, but it is already on file and item 277 records that the obvious fix regressed the self-host lexer, so it is re-measured here and deliberately NOT re-proposed.

    **Suggested order, cheapest first: (c), then (b), then (a).** (c) touches one prelude function with no golden and no self-host exposure. (b) is the largest measured allocation count in the census. (a) is the largest per-site win and the one that needs the item-429 discipline of a new corpus fixture alongside the `selfhost/emit_rust.rvl` port. `backends/rust/emit.py` for all of them, plus `selfhost/emit_rust.rvl` and `tests/fixtures/emit_rust_corpus/` for (a), (b) and (d).

    **LANDED (c), (b) and (a), in that order, on `fix/437-rust-codegen-perf` (2026-09-02).** Every figure below is a re-run of `bench/codegen/rust/run.py` and `census.py` after the change; NO WALL CLOCK WAS TAKEN, on the same reasoning the audit gives above, and the machine was again running about a dozen concurrent agents.
    * **(c) DONE.** `revl_index_of` is now `self.find(needle)` with the byte offset converted to a codepoint index by counting the prefix. **1,500 allocations and 12.97 MB -> 0 and 0**, which is the hand-written comparator exactly. Verified as predicted: no golden carries the prelude and `selfhost/emit_rust.rvl` carries no prelude text at all, so there was no port. The stale `provide_arc` module docstring the audit noted in passing is corrected in the same commit.
    * **(b) DONE, and wider than the audit scoped it.** A `Str` literal now renders as the bare `&'static str` in three read-only slots: the `RevlStrOps`/`RevlStrListOps` `&str` argument slots, the `==`/`!=` operands, and the item-282 borrowed-param call path, which the audit had not separated out and which turned out to be 588 of the sites. **`literal_arg` 400,000 allocations and 5.50 MB -> 120,000 and 1.38 MB; `str_eq_literal` 240,000 and 1.12 MB -> 0 and 0.** Census: **1,235 literal-into-a-`&str`-slot -> 0, 988 literal-compared-to-a-`Str` -> 0**, all 2,223 sites gone, total `String::from(` **3,920 -> 1,697**. Two receiver-overloaded methods needed care: `indexOf` on a `List[Str]` takes `&String`, so it requires a certainly-`Str` receiver, while `concat` on a `List` takes `&Vec<T>`, which a `Str` literal can never be, so a literal there already proves the `Str` overload. The `==` rule borrows unless the emitter POSITIVELY knows the other operand is a non-`Str` type, because a site that compiles today has that operand rendering as `String` or `&str` and both carry `PartialEq<&str>`; requiring a certainly-`Str` other side would have left every `for`-binding and extern-result operand untouched, which is most of them. `literal_arg`'s residual 3x is the one `indexOf` whose receiver is a `for` binding the emitter cannot type.
    * **(a) DONE, both spellings.** `_v3_inplace_persistent` gained a `concat` arm split by receiver type (`s.push_str(..)` / `v.extend(x.iter().cloned())`) and `_v3_self_append_inplace` gained a `+` arm, `_v3_self_append_plus`. **`concat_append` 729,599 allocations and 97.2 MB -> 4,825 and 236 KB** against 2,425 and 212 KB hand-written, so the O(n^2) is now O(n) and the residual 2x is the per-element clone revl's persistent `List` semantics require (the comparator may move, the emitter may not). **`str_append` 5,999 and 84.3 MB -> 13 and 32,768 B, identical to the comparator.** Census: **65 self-appends via `concat` -> 0**. The audit's receiver-type argument held, and one extra rule closes the last ten sites: a receiver `var_types` cannot name, appending something certainly `Str`, is a `Str` receiver, because `List[T].concat` takes a `List[T]`. `list_append`, item 284's own control, is unchanged at 17 and 2,097,152 B on both sides.
    * **RUST DID NOT NEED THE GO TIER'S ANALYSIS.** Item 434 needed `_v3_self_rebind_locals`, a whole-body proof of unique ownership, because a Go slice header aliases silently and nothing in the language catches a second live owner. Rust needs none of it: a second live owner can only arise from a move, rustc refuses a move-then-reuse (E0382), and every by-value move this backend emits clones first, so item 284's uniqueness argument is discharged by the borrow checker on exactly the code that already compiles. The rewrite stays local. One guard was added that the ownership argument does NOT cover: an appended operand that reads the receiver (`s = s + s`) would take a shared borrow of a value already borrowed mutably (E0502) where the persistent form compiled, so `_v3_mentions_name` refuses that shape.
    * **THE ITEM-429 TRAP, CHECKED DIRECTLY RATHER THAN TRUSTED TO THE ORACLE.** `selfhost/emit_rust.rvl` was read for each shape instead of relying on a green byte-agreement run. It carries NO stdlib `builtin`/`len` surface and NO item-282 borrow analysis, so (c), the `&str`-slot half of (b), and the `concat` half of (a) have no mirror there and no port. The two halves that DO have a mirror were ported: (b)'s `==`/`!=` borrow into `render_bin`, and (a)'s `+` self-append into the `assign` step, with `borrowed_str_lit`, `eq_operand_borrowable`, `borrow_str_arg`, `mentions_name` and `self_append_plus`. **`tests/fixtures/emit_rust_corpus/perf_shapes.rvl` is the new fixture**, holding the `==` borrow in both operand orders with a typed and an un-inferable operand, a non-`Str` `==` that must not move, the `+` self-append, and a `+` whose target is not the receiver. It is deliberately free of any `builtin`/`len` node, since those are outside the port's covered subset. The `concat` spelling of the self-append therefore still has no fixture, because there is nothing for it to diverge from until the stdlib surface is ported.
    * **VERIFIED:** `bench/codegen/rust/run.py` (all twelve build, agree and report), `census.py`, `backends/rust/test_emit_rust.py` (114 passed, including the `cargo check` over emitted output), the byte-agreement oracle over all 19 corpus fixtures, and `tests/test_selfhost_emit_rust.py` in full at (b) (138 passed, including `test_selfhost_stages_cargo_build_and_run`, which builds and runs the four self-host stages natively over the 2,223 changed sites). **NOT VERIFIED:** `test_selfhost_stages_cargo_build_and_run` was not re-run after (a) landed, and neither full `tests/` suite was run on either interpreter. The host was carrying about forty concurrent test processes across ten agents and reds under that load were not reproducing, so the gate was left to the merge owner rather than reported from noise.
    * **LEFT, as the audit scoped them:** (d) index-read clones, 332 census sites, unchanged; (e) the constant-list rebuild; (f) the for-loop iterable clone, 3 sites; (g) the `Str.length()` complexity note, analytic only. All four negative results are untouched, and the item-277/282 `slice` residual was left alone as the audit instructed.
    * **ALL FINDINGS NOW CLOSED, issue #116 done (2026-09-08).** After the above landed (a)(b)(c), the remainder closed as follows: (f) FIXED 2026-09-06 (`fix/issue-116-rust-perf-remainder`, `_movable_for_iterables`); (d) FIXED across the field-off-an-index subset on main + PR #648 and the `&str`/`&key` ARGUMENT slot in PR #739; and (e) + (g) on `fix/issue-116-perf-e-g-close` (2026-09-08) NOT as fixes but as recorded decisions — (e) ACCEPTED-WITH-MEASURED-REASON (the only zero-alloc form is the item-277 calling-convention change; `Vec<String>` cannot recover the allocations) and (g) a DOCUMENTED NO-OP (0-vs-0 allocations, a complexity note the audit ranked last and re-proposed no fix for). Both decisions are executable in `tests/test_116_rust_perf_accepted.py`, with an (e) footprint shape added to `bench/codegen/rust/census.py`. The union of #739 (d's argument slot) and this closes the audit; issue #116 is CLOSED.

438. (issue #117) **PETRI-NET REACHABILITY AS A COMPILE-TIME LIVENESS ANALYZER (2026-09-02, ported idea, VERIFIED ABSENT).** Grepped: revl has ZERO Petri results anywhere in the tree, and no liveness analysis of any kind. The static guarantees are all STRUCTURAL: G2 provisions are disjoint per `(key, realm)`, G3 the graph is acyclic, G7 teardown is LIFO-complete. None of them answers a REACHABILITY question, so a composition that is structurally admissible can still reach a dead state at runtime: two consumers each holding a provision the other waits on, or a `subscribe merge(a, b)` fan-in whose sources are consumed elsewhere so the merge never becomes enabled. Item 130's pointwise admission rules 3.1 and 3.6 check each source IN ISOLATION, which is exactly the gap: they cannot see an interleaving.
    **The shape.** A `revl analyze` mode, NOT a runtime: derive a net from the composition IR (provisions and coeffects as places, activation bodies as transitions), then bounded BFS over the marking graph to find dead states, with S-invariants to prove boundedness where the graph is too large. It becomes another fold in the admission gate, so the result is a REFUSAL naming the deadlocked cycle rather than a crash at 3am, which is the same shape items 427 and 428 landed for their checks.
    **Why this is a real port and not a rewrite.** The engine is dependency-free structural mathematics (nets, markings, enablement, bounded reachability, Martinez-Silva signed elimination for S-invariants); none of it needs to know anything about revl. The revl-side work is the DERIVATION from IR to net and the refusal wording, not the algorithm.
    **Open questions before slicing.** (1) What is a place: a provision key, a `(key, realm)` pair, or a coeffect? G2's own unit is `(key, realm)`, which argues for that. (2) Bounded BFS needs a bound, and a refusal that fires only under a bound is a partial guarantee that must SAY so (item 418's lesson: a check must not claim more than it establishes). (3) Does a dead state refuse admission, or warn? A false positive that blocks a legal composition is worse than the deadlock for adoption, so this likely starts as `revl analyze` and only becomes a gate once the false-positive rate is measured. Relates to 130 (streams), 418 (do not overclaim what a check proves), 426 (composition as the analyzed unit).

439. ◑ (issue #118) **A2A 1.0.0 TRANSPORT BINDING FOR REMOTE PROVIDERS (2026-09-02, ported idea; was VERIFIED ABSENT, now SLICE 1 LANDED 2026-09-06, merged 82ae5ae0: `import_a2a` records only OPTIONAL A2A capabilities and REFUSES a REQUIRED capability extension, surfaced as `revl import a2a`; SLICE 2 LANDED (#594): the terminal-only `through a2a` remote-row wire — `message/send` mapped onto the canonical seam envelope, text-in/text-out, non-terminal task faults; SLICE B1 LANDED ON BOTH TIERS (this branch): the item 421 F5 call-argument funnel now rides the importer's two coloured `@ts` bodies (`message/send` and the REST `message:send`) as well as the three `@py` ones, via `a2a_boundary.ts_funnel`, so a peer that reflects the caller's argument into an `error.code`, a task `state` or a reply `kind` cannot put the caller's own bytes on this composition's error channel by picking the tier that answers; the scrub is EXACT-match, the sentence survives, and the emitted module goes through the `tsc --strict` gate. SLICE C3 LANDED: `Untrusted[T]` return tainting on BOTH the canonical and `through a2a` wires — D-424c.9, the synthesized crossing returns `Untrusted[<T>]` so a remote result reaching a `Trusted[T]` sink is refused (G9) without an `endorse` and admits with one, and a local provider of the same service stays untainted. STILL OPEN toward #118, and each waiting on another item rather than on effort here: (1) the load-bearing Task-lifecycle question — `message/stream`/`tasks/resubscribe`/polling and whether a long-running Task maps to one emission, a stream (item 130), or a session (item 250); (2) non-text `Part`s (`FilePart`/`DataPart`); (3) gRPC and HTTP+JSON/REST as `through a2a` sub-transports; (4) the RUNTIME half of `on_failure(withdraw)` — the declaration/check/IR/fault are built and pinned, but wiring the transport fault into the R2/R3 provider-withdrawal cascade is a runtime-backend change, since a synthesized row's `urllib` body is not the seam client that joins the placement bridge's `watch(on_lost)` monitor. `docs/design/439-a2a-transport-binding.md` records the slice map.).** Grepped: revl has NO A2A surface at all. Its interop is strictly revl-to-revl: UDS seams, `RemoteScope` realms, cross-tier placement, and item 151's two-composition topology. So a revl composition can consume another revl composition and nothing else. SLICE G8c LANDED 2026-09-15 (#1099): `revl audit` RESOLVES a composition document instead of compiling it as a module, so the CLI reaches the same G8 surface the property was already pinned over, and the synthesized `remote ... through a2a` crossings render with their folded `net.<host>` reach. This was a fail-OPEN silence rather than a missing feature: the command printed an empty boundary surface and exited 0 for a composition that crosses to a peer over the network, and an empty audit surface is read as an ABSENCE of authority. Admission is whole-composition under `confine=True`, there is deliberately no `--trust-host-code` on an audit, and the three shapes the command cannot resolve now REFUSE BY NAME with a nonzero exit instead of rendering an empty surface: a composition listed beside modules, two composition documents in one invocation, and a layer document. Exit tests `tests/test_439_a2a_transport.py::test_revl_audit_over_a_composition_renders_the_a2a_crossing` and `::test_revl_audit_over_the_four_op_composition_renders_all_four`, three negative tests, and `::test_revl_audit_over_a_module_is_unchanged` as the control that passes on both trees (6 failed on the predecessor, 7 passed on the branch).
    **Why now.** Item 424 gap (c) decided the SEMANTICS this needs and deliberately left the transport abstract: a remote provider is a ROW whose provider is synthesized, remoteness is an admission fact rather than a wiring difference, transport failure is a WITHDRAWAL by default (reusing R2/R3) with `on_failure(result)` as the opt-in, every returned value is `Untrusted[T]`, and a generated client does NOT re-admit the callee (337: the receiver re-compiles from its own source; a client is the sender). An A2A binding makes that concrete without reopening any of it.
    **The shape.** A service declaration whose provider is spelled remote over A2A synthesizes an adapter component that crosses the seam with A2A task semantics (Agent Cards, Task/Message/Part/Artifact, Send/Stream/Cancel over JSON-RPC 2.0, HTTP/REST, SSE, gRPC). Every guarantee still applies AT THE BOUNDARY: G1 declared-only access, the capability cone, tickets, and the F5/F6/F7 redaction that landed today.
    **What must be decided, not assumed.** (1) A2A's Task lifecycle is long-running and streaming; revl's emission model is a single crossing with an inverse. Does an A2A Task map to one emission, to a stream (item 130), or to a session (250)? This is the load-bearing question and it is not obvious. (2) An external agent is not a revl composition, so NOTHING about it is checked: its Agent Card is a CLAIM. That makes every A2A provider item 329's untrusted-author case by construction, and the returned `Untrusted[T]` of 424(c) is necessary but probably not sufficient. (3) Version honesty: the claim must be "A2A 1.0.0", never "A2A", because the protocol moves.
    **Why it is worth doing.** It is the difference between revl components composing only with each other and revl components consuming the wider agent ecosystem as CHECKED provisions. Depends on 424(c) and 426 S1 (the row table). Relates to 337 (polyglot admission mesh), 329 (untrusted-author profile), 151 (two-composition topology).

440. (issue #119) **EXTERN CALL TIERS: THE THIRD TIER AND THE RE-ISSUE SEAM (2026-09-02).** A ported-idea review proposed classifying extern calls into read / idempotent / non-idempotent tiers with a journalled dispatch and a fail-closed ambiguous outcome on restart. **Most of that already exists as item 309 and this item records only the genuine remainder**, because the proposal as stated would have duplicated landed work. What 309 already does, verified in `src/revl/recovery.py`: the WAL journals each effect as it commits; recovery branches on `undo_idempotent`; an UNKEYED inverse whose single at-most-once attempt is spent refuses with `outcome="unknown"` and the operator hint "second attempt cannot be proven safe"; a KEYED one is classified `replay: free` by the `--recovery` audit. That is journal, branch by tier, fail-closed on ambiguity, already shipped.
    **✅ FIXED 2026-09-05 (`379b43bf`); the finding as filed is kept below for the record, WAS OPEN AS RE-VERIFIED ON `2d2fa07f`.** **(a) THERE IS NO READ TIER.** 309's register is two-valued (keyed or not). A pure READ extern is safely re-dispatchable on recovery with no key and no reconciliation, because it changes nothing, and today it is treated as unkeyed and therefore ambiguous. That is a fail-closed error in the SAFE direction, so it is a usability defect rather than a soundness one, but it makes recovery ask an operator about calls that never needed asking. Note revl already knows which externs are pure, so the tier may be derivable rather than declared. **RE-CHECK 2026-09-02:** no read tier exists in the shipped source; 309's register is still two-valued.
    **✅ FIXED 2026-09-05 (`379b43bf`); the finding as filed is kept below for the record, WAS OPEN AS RE-VERIFIED ON `2d2fa07f`.** **(b) THE RE-ISSUE SEAM IS DEFERRED, AND THE CODE SAYS SO.** `recovery.py` carries `TODO(309-slice3)`: an owed deferred emission whose extern is `idempotent(key: p)` MAY be auto-fired on recovery, since the descriptor carries the key and the fire is dedup-safe even if the pre-crash flush landed. It is not done because firing requires re-invoking the emission host body and the minimal fresh-process recover has no runtime for it. So the keyed tier's advantage is currently CLASSIFIED but not ACTED ON: a keyed owed emission is reported as free to replay and then left for a human anyway. **RE-CHECK 2026-09-02:** `src/revl/recovery.py:445` still carries the `TODO(309-slice3)` comment this finding quotes.
    **Exit:** (1) a read tier, derived from the existing purity classification if that is sound, so a read never reaches an operator as ambiguous; (2) the item-309 slice-3 re-issue seam, gated by the item-33 policy knob the TODO names, so a keyed owed emission can actually be auto-fired; (3) a test that a read-tier extern recovers with no operator prompt, and that a non-idempotent one still refuses. Relates to 309 (the register and its partial order), 245 (session commit), A8 (WAL discharge), 418 (the formal layer now models the three teardown entry kinds, which is the adjacent distinction).

441. (issue #120) **TERMINATION IS AGENT-OWNED, AND NOTHING IN THE LANGUAGE DECIDES WHEN A TASK IS DONE (2026-09-02, ported idea; was VERIFIED ABSENT, now CONTROLLER SLICE 1 LANDED 2026-09-06, merged 56925f0d: the goal-contract termination lattice; LANGUAGE SURFACE S1 LANDED 2026-09-06, merged e19b936 / #520: the `Criterion`/`Guard` marker type heads and the checker rules L1/L2/L4/L5/L6, off-by-default and byte-identical when unused; LANGUAGE SURFACE S2 LANDED (this PR): `revl goal audit`, the §5.2 blind-spot report — the class-(c) capabilities the run reaches that the termination contract's own cone does not observe, a PURE function of a compiled IR (`src/revl/goal_audit.py`, `tests/test_458_goal_audit.py`) reading the S1 `termination` header fact, with the no-goal-service case answered rather than rendered empty. STILL OPEN toward full closure of #120: S3 (freeze + operator budgets + the L3 turn rule), S4 (the per-turn fold and WAL trail), S5 (the two `Session.call`/`commit_confirm` chokepoint terms + the honest-vs-liar differential, the design's whole-feature exit criterion), S6 (the read surfaces + docgen), and the §11.3 measurement of "observes N of M" over one real item-248 agent run — the blind-spot report's per-criterion attribution rendering (naming WHICH criterion reads a capability) is a presentation refinement over the same set difference and is not yet computed. `docs/design/458-termination-language-surface.md §7` is the slice plan.** Grepped: no goal contract, no acceptance criteria, no termination controller anywhere in revl (the only hits are in `forks/stc-go`, unrelated). revl's agent story ends at the admission gate: `revl mcp serve` gives an agent `revl_check` and `revl_admit`, and the gate answers "may this run", but NOTHING answers "is this done". The agent decides for itself when to stop.
    **Why this is the same defect class revl already spends effort closing.** Items 427 F2/F3/F4 were all one shape: the operator is shown one thing and the system acts on another. Agent-owned termination is that shape at the task level. The operator approves a goal, the agent decides unilaterally whether it met it, and there is no artifact the operator can check the claim against. A gate that judges every crossing but never judges completion leaves the largest decision unjudged.
    **The shape.** The agent DRAFTS acceptance criteria; the system FREEZES them into an immutable goal contract at activation; from then on a deterministic state machine, reading observable state rather than the agent's own reply, owns every termination decision, with an auditable trail per turn. In revl's idiom this is not new machinery: criteria are revl functions over observable state, verifiers are declared services, the contract is a composition-declared object frozen at activation, and an attempt to effect past a satisfied or violated contract fires a class-(c) ticket exactly as an unapproved crossing does today.
    **What must be decided, not assumed.** (1) Who may amend a frozen contract, and does amendment invalidate the run's prior approvals? The honest default is that it does, per item 427 F4's grant-invalidation rule. (2) Criteria are drafted BY the agent, so a contract is only as good as the drafting; freezing an inadequate criterion is not safety, it is a durable mistake, and the design must say what the operator reviews and when. (3) The value-of-continuation estimate the source system computes across candidate actions is the part most likely to be wrong here: revl has no reward model and should not acquire one casually. Start with satisfied / violated / undecided and no scoring. Relates to 427 (shown versus enforced), 246 (typed approval), 344 (standing grants), 251 (distillation).

442. (issue #121) **DELEGATION IS NOT A VERB: A COMPONENT CANNOT HAND A SCOPED, REVOCABLE REFERENCE TO ANOTHER (2026-09-02, ported idea).** revl already ATTENUATES authority on spawn (`lower.py:10671`: a capability "attenuates on delegation: the child's ceiling"), so the concept of a narrowed child authority exists. What does not exist is a way for one component to hand a scoped reference to one of its own provisions to a component that is not its child, with the HANDOFF ITSELF checked. Today the options are to widen the consumer's declared requires (permanent, not scoped) or to route every call through the holder (correct but eliminates the point).
    **The shape.** `delegate` as a language-level crossing whose receipt is minted BY THE GATE, not by either party: a hash over the delegated surface, an expiry, and a revocation hook wired to the provider's own withdrawal, bound to the consumer's realm. A forged or replayed delegation refuses at the seam the same way item 337's unjudged addresses now do, and the receipt is the artifact an operator reads.
    **Why it matters more after 439.** A remote provider over A2A is close to useless without delegation: every call would otherwise need its own approval, which is the ergonomic failure that pushes people to over-grant. 439 and 442 are the same feature seen from two ends.
    **Open questions.** (1) Revocation versus G7: a delegated reference revoked mid-activation is a withdrawal under a consumer that did not declare it, which is the interaction G7 governs and 426 R1 already flagged as blocked. (2) Does a delegation transfer or share? Sharing needs no G2 change; transfer does, since G2's unit is `(key, realm)`. (3) Depth: does a delegate re-delegate, and if so does the receipt chain or flatten? An unbounded chain is a capability cone nobody can enumerate, which breaks G8. Relates to 439 (remote providers), 337 (seam judgement), 294 (parameterized capabilities), 421 F1 (token attenuation), 426 (rows and claims). **Product delta.** The runtime half beyond the compile-time attenuation is a signed, EXPIRING, REVOCABLE lease attributed to both delegator and delegate, carrying a `may_not_delegate` control.

443. PARTIALLY LANDED 2026-09-02, now ON MAIN (`4dadacc6`, PR #169; branch `feat/443-estop` deleted after landing, verified ancestor of main): the py tier and the formal layer. The five non-py tiers keep their cooperative teardown and have no E-Stop, deliberately — see **Not done** in PR #169. GitHub issue #122 stays open for those. **OPEN ON THE PY TIER (2026-09-29):** an armed E-Stop does not stop a direct `emit` extern (`extern_emit` in `backends/python/runtime.py` has no E-Stop check), and it refuses a witnessed effect only after the host body ran (issue #1504); the halt is also not thread-safe (issue #1487). PR 1518 in review. **CONDUCTOR HALT LANDED 2026-09-03 (branch `agent/122-estop`):** `revl run --placement P --estop-latch FILE` now halts a MULTI-PROCESS placement, which had no operator halt of any kind before — its only stop was `_stop_all`, a cooperative unwind that waits on each child's own `DOWN` line. Issue #122 still stays open: the five non-py tiers are killed rather than halted, and the report says so per component. **E-STOP: THERE IS NO OPERATOR HALT THAT IS NOT A GRACEFUL UNWIND (2026-09-02, ported idea, VERIFIED ABSENT).** Grepped: no `e-stop`, `estop` or `emergency` anywhere in revl. Every stop revl has is COOPERATIVE: teardown replays inverses LIFO, faults route through residue records, withdrawal propagates to dependents. That is exactly right for a composition fault and exactly wrong for an operator emergency. When a human hits the button during a class-(c) storm or a runaway loop with irreversible effects in flight, unwinding 200 brackets first is not a safe answer; it is a long answer.
    **The honest semantics.** Stop dispatching NEW crossings immediately, report what was in flight, and mark the ambiguous ones ambiguous. That is a different verdict from both commit and abort, and item 418's newly-corrected G7 model is where it has to be stated, since G7 now carries the activation's verdict explicitly and `replaysUnder` is read off the teardown contract's table. An E-Stop verdict is a third column in that table, and adding it is a formal-layer change before it is a runtime change.
    **It gives item 440 its counterpart.** 440 records that revl's extern tiers classify a call as ambiguous when its at-most-once attempt may or may not have landed. E-Stop is precisely the event that CREATES that state deliberately: it lands between the journal entry and the completion record, so the ambiguous tier is not an edge case any more, it is the designed outcome of an operator halt. The two features are stronger together than either alone.
    **The shape.** A `revl estop` verb on the CLI and MCP surfaces, held as an operator authority (not something a composition or an agent may invoke on itself, which is the whole point), plus an in-flight inventory in the residue record so the report says what was interrupted rather than what was unwound.
    **Open questions.** (1) Is E-Stop expressible as a verdict in the item-418 teardown model without weakening G7's completeness theorem, or does G7 become conditional on the verdict? (2) What happens to a bracket whose inverse is ITSELF in flight when the stop arrives? (3) Does an E-Stop composition ever resume, or is the instance dead and recovery the only path back? Relates to 418 (the G7 verdict model), 440 (ambiguous tiers), 309 (at-most-once attempts), 397 (residue records).
    **DESIGN AND PY-TIER IMPLEMENTATION, `feat/443-estop` (docs/design/443-estop.md).** The three open questions are answered, formal layer first. (1) **G7 does not weaken and does not become conditional.** Its completeness (`teardown_replays_all`) and soundness (`teardown_only_witnessed`) were already stated relative to `replaysUnder v` after item 418's correction, so they extend to the new `Verdict.halted` UNCHANGED and hold with an empty replay set — no hypothesis added to either. What changed is two COROLLARIES that quantified over every verdict while assuming every verdict settles: `G7.bracket_replays_under_every_verdict` and `Semantics.replays_or_discharges` now carry `v.settles = true`, with the E-Stop counter-instance proved beside each so the hypothesis is exhibited as load-bearing rather than left as an unexplained weakening. The general statement that replaces the dichotomy is `Semantics.disposition_trichotomy`: every (kind, verdict) pair has exactly one of THREE dispositions — replayed, discharged, or **stranded** (registered, not run, and NOT dropped) — and `Semantics.book_lengths_add` proves the three lists partition the stack under every verdict. Stranded is not a synonym for discharged: discharge RELEASES the inverse and the witness, and an E-Stop must keep both, because `revl recover` is what reads them back. (2) **A bracket whose inverse is in flight becomes that activation's single `estop-ambiguous` record.** The halt is a CUT at index `k` into the interrupted verdict's replay order — `haltCompleted` / `haltAmbiguous` / `haltUnattempted` — with `halt_ambiguity_is_at_most_one` and `halt_books_are_total` proving the split is bounded and total. Reconciliation is item 309's existing rule (keyed → re-issuable, unkeyed → refuse with `outcome: unknown`), reached deliberately instead of by accident. (3) **The instance is DEAD; recovery is the only path back.** No resume: the body was cut mid-step and the runtime cannot know whether the in-flight crossing landed. Because the halt strands rather than discharges and writes no `discharge` record, the WAL discharge-descriptors with nothing behind them are exactly what is owed — **an E-Stop is deliberately shaped to look like a CRASH to `revl recover`**, which already has a proven answer for one. **What landed:** `Verdict.halted` + the three-way disposition in `formal/RevL/Semantics.lean`, the E-Stop column and the halt cut in `G7_LifoComplete.lean` (13 new theorems, all registered in the axioms and non-vacuity gates); the third column and two new residue kinds (`estop-stranded`, `estop-ambiguous`) in `docs/design/teardown-contract.md`; a process-global latch plus a cross-process latch FILE in `backends/python/runtime.py`, checked at every seam that dispatches or accepts a crossing (`plug`, acquire, witnessed, compensate, deferred flush, approval crossing), with `Frame._guard` stranding instead of replaying and `drain` committing nothing; `revl estop --latch/--wal/--report/--clear` and `revl run --estop-latch`; `revl_estop` / `revl_estop_report` on MCP, gated on a NEW `estop` operator verb (item 55) whose target set is the whole composition — never folded into `unload` or `commit`, and with no in-language surface at all, which is what makes "a composition may not halt itself" structural. **Stated cost, not hidden:** an E-Stop releases no handle. Brackets are stranded, so descriptors, pool connections and leases stay held until the process exits or recovery runs. It trades resource cleanliness for stop latency, which is the trade the button exists to make. **Not done:** the five non-py tiers keep their cooperative teardown and have no E-Stop — honest and visible rather than silently degraded; the formal layer is tier-independent so a later tier's loop builds against the same table.
    **CONDUCTOR SLICE, `agent/122-estop` (2026-09-03).** The gap the py slice left was not a tier gap but a PROCESS one: `run_placement` had no halt at all, so an operator's only stop for a live placement was the graceful unwind. The conductor now watches the same latch and, on the button, (1) says so on the trace before doing anything that can take time, (2) stops every child WITHOUT asking one to unwind or waiting for a `DOWN`, (3) reports, and (4) interrupts its own main loop, because a halt that needs the main loop's cooperation to be noticed is not one. The two populations are handled differently and the report names which one each component got: a py child is handed the latch in its environment AND its process spec (a sandboxed child, item 411, need not inherit the environment), watches it on a timer via a new `runtime.estop_from_latch` — `_estop_check` engages lazily at the next crossing, and an IDLE process crosses nothing, so it would sit through the emergency — prints `[<name>] HALTED <inventory>` and calls `os._exit` with no teardown; a child on any other tier is SIGKILLed at once, because that is the only halt it has. **The reporting is the deliverable, not the stop.** `_estop_halt_report` names every component left un-torn-down with its process and tier, one line each, merges each latch-honoring child's own `estop-stranded` / `estop-ambiguous` records by name, and counts the residue it CANNOT enumerate as UNKNOWN in the same total ("3 lines, 1 of them UNKNOWN") rather than quoting a comfortable number. `REVL_ESTOP_HALT_WINDOW` (2s) bounds how long a halting child gets to name its inventory; it is not a teardown grace and a child that misses it is killed and reported UNKNOWN. Unarmed is still the default: no latch, no watcher thread, no latch read, and every existing teardown path byte-identical. Non-vacuity: with the halt replaced by `stop_all` the new suite fails on "the E-Stop asked a child to unwind" and on the missing report. **Still not done, and issue #122 stays open for it:** no non-py tier honors the latch, so `revl.estop.TIERS_WITH_ESTOP` is still `{py}` and every other tier's residue after a halt is UNKNOWN by construction; wiring one is a matter of its runtime reading the latch and printing the `HALTED` line.
445. (issue #124) **THE UNIQUE-OWNERSHIP ANALYSIS BEHIND IN-PLACE ACCUMULATION IS WRITTEN TWICE AND IS FLOW-INSENSITIVE, WHICH LEAVES `list_sort` CUBIC AND `list_dedup` QUADRATIC (2026-09-02, from items 434 and 436 F1).** Two tiers now carry the SAME rule in different code: the go backend's `_v3_self_rebind_locals` (item 434 a/b/e) and the python backend's `_py_inplace_locals` (item 436 F1). Both say a local may be written in place exactly when the object it names is reachable through no other name, both prove it the same way (born from a fresh allocation here, every write a self-rebind, every other occurrence a non-retaining read, a parameter never eligible), and both were derived independently. Item 436's own fix note proposed the frontend `unique` marker for this reason; the two implementations are the evidence that it is worth doing, because ts, rust, java and wasm would each write it a third, fourth, fifth and sixth time. **Two gaps the flow-insensitive form leaves measurable, both reproducible with `PYTHONPATH=src python bench/codegen/python/run.py`:**
    (a) **Flow-insensitivity leaves `stdlib/list.rvl`'s `list_sort` at its emitted cubic.** Its inner accumulator is `var res = []` inside the outer loop, pushed into, then handed out with `out = res`. That last statement is an escape, so the analysis refuses the whole local; but the NEXT outer iteration re-declares `res` from a fresh literal, so the escaped object can never reach a later write and the rewrite would have been sound. Measured unchanged at 171700 copies at n=100 and 1353400 at n=200, x7.9 per doubling where the hand-written same-algorithm sort grows x4.0. Seeing it needs a forward may-alias pass with a fixpoint over the back edge (allocation sites as abstract objects; a fresh-literal birth KILLS the alias), which is a real dataflow analysis and was deliberately not attempted inside item 436.
    (b) **The rule closes over calls, so `list_dedup` stays quadratic.** `if (!list_contains(out, x)) { out = out.push(x) }` hands `out` to a call, and an intraprocedural analysis must assume a call retains its argument. `list_contains` demonstrably does not. A whole-program summary (does this fn retain any parameter) would answer it, and the same summary is what would let a local born from a CALL result (`var ys = list_map(xs, f)` then `ys = ys.push(z)`) be owned rather than refused. Note the python backend's existing template inliner already dissolves the SMALL cases by accident: a `sizeof(out)` that inlines to `len(out)` stops being a call before the analysis runs, which is why the gap only bites on fns too big to inline.
    **Exit:** decide first whether the marker belongs in the frontend (one analysis, `unique` on the IR node, six backends lowering it) or stays per-backend; if the frontend, the analysis must be checked against ts/rust/java/wasm for the same accumulation shape and ported to `selfhost/lower.rvl` with its IR-agreement oracle, which is the expensive half. Then (a), whose exit test is `transforms.list_sort` growing x4.0 per doubling instead of x7.9 in `bench/codegen/python/run.py`'s copy table, and (b), whose exit test is `list_dedup` growing x2.0. Both must keep every aliasing case in `tests/test_py_inplace_accumulation.py` green, since a wrong answer here is a silent aliasing bug and not a crash. Relates to 284 (rust's in-place self-append, the first tier to do this), 383 (the map/filter/reduce trio), 434, 436, 437(a).
    **DECIDED: THE FRONTEND, AND (a) AND (b) ARE BOTH CLOSED (2026-09-02, `feat/445-unique-ownership-marker`).** The decision is settled by what the rule is ABOUT rather than by tidiness: "is the object this binding names reachable through any other name" is a question about the SOURCE, and neither prior implementation consulted anything language-specific to answer it — both walk the same IR dicts and differ only in which builtins they trust. Three things followed that a per-backend copy could not have delivered. **(1) The marker is a FACT, not an instruction, and that is what makes it portable to six tiers that genuinely disagree about what to do with it.** `unique: true` on a self-rebinding `assign` says the binding owns its object at that write; each tier then decides. rust needs none of it (the borrow checker discharges the same obligation on the code that already compiles, item 437), no tier can mutate a `Str` (which is why `concat` is never marked and why go's item-434 (e) Builder exists), and a tier that ignores the marker keeps the copying form — always correct. go honours `true` only; python also honours `unique: "copy"`, the local born off another name (`var out = m`) that is owned because python materialises ONE defensive copy at the `let` marked `unique_birth: "List"|"Map"`, where the persistent form made one per write. A backend that does not implement that birth copy ignores those writes and stays correct, which is why the two flavours are separate values and not one boolean. **(2) Sharing it is what made PRECISION affordable, and the precision is the two gaps.** `src/revl/ownership.py` is a forward dataflow with a fixpoint on every loop back edge, asking the question PER WRITE instead of per name: a birth kills an escape, and a self-rebind kills it whichever form the backend picks (in place on a value nothing reaches, or the persistent copy's brand-new container), which is what makes the per-write question well-founded. A `return` needs no rule at all — the state after it is unreachable, so flow sensitivity subsumes the special case both prior versions had to state. On top of that a whole-program summary answers "does this fn retain parameter i", a least fixpoint over the call graph that starts optimistic and only adds retention. **(3) `selfhost/emit_py.rvl` got SMALLER**: it drops ~130 lines that were the reference analysis ported by hand, and reads two markers instead.
    **(a) CLOSED, and better than the exit test asked.** `bench/codegen/python/run.py`, `transforms.list_sort`: element copies **171700 -> 5050** at n=100 and **1353400 -> 20100** at n=200, so growth is **x7.9 -> x4.0** against the hand-written same-algorithm sort's x4.0 — and the absolute counts are now EQUAL to the hand arm, not merely the same growth class. `bytes` 4792/3200 = 1.50x -> **3200/3200 = 1.00x**. `ops` moves the other way, 1.26x -> 1.27x, because `append` is a method call where `+ [x]` is one binary op; that is the trade and it is the correct one, since `ops` is blind to the C-level copying `copies` exists to see.
    **(b) CLOSED, with the whole-program summary the item predicted.** `list_contains` walks its list and answers a Bool, so it retains nothing, and `list_dedup`'s push is owned. The bench grew a `transforms.deduped` case pinning it against the same algorithm by hand (a linear membership scan, so the number isolates the emitter's copying and not the algorithm): element copies **5050 -> 100** at n=200 and **20100 -> 200** at n=400, growth **x4.0 -> x2.0**, equal to the hand arm, with `ops` 1.00x and `bytes` 1.00x. Two summary rules differ from the local analysis and both follow from asking about the CALLER's object: a `return` RETAINS (a returned parameter is an alias by any definition), and a `for` ITERABLE does not (the iterator dies with the call, where inside one body it is held for the loop's whole extent).
    **THE CONSERVATIVE FALLBACK, stated because a wrong answer here is a silent aliasing bug and not a crash.** Every uncertainty resolves to "not owned", which emits the copying form the tiers had before. A parameter is never owned. A name is not owned until a birth this function made. An unmodelled statement shape kills every name in its subtree, and that fallback is deliberately blind to the retention summary. An expression slot not on the list retains. A builtin whose method is not on the whitelist is assumed to hand back an alias of its receiver — the whitelist is go's `_V3_LINEAR_READ_METHODS`, the NARROWER of the two merged rules and not their union, because `xs.slice(a, b)` is a fresh list on python and a header over the same backing array on go. Every callee without a summary retains every argument: an extern, a host root, a service method, a call through a function VALUE, a callee named by anything but a plain `var`, and any call whose argument count does not match the callee's parameter count.
    **LEFT OPEN: the `selfhost/lower.rvl` port, which is the expensive half the item named.** `lower.rvl` streams tokens straight to IR JSON in one pass and never builds the statement tree a forward dataflow with a loop fixpoint needs, so the native IR carries neither marker and diverges from the reference on any document with an accumulation loop — one in the emit_py corpus, `transforms.rvl`. Was recorded as a NAMED strict-xfail (`UNIQUE_MARKER_GAP` in `tests/test_selfhost_lower_ir.py`) on the taint gap's terms: red for a stated reason, XPASSing loudly the day it is ported, rather than dropped from the corpus. **That gap is now CLOSED and the xfail DELETED:** `selfhost/lower.rvl` grew an `own_*` block that builds the statement view the forward dataflow needs and runs it to a fixpoint over each loop back edge, `transforms.rvl` agrees byte-for-byte, and `CORPUS_UNIQUE_AWARE = CORPUS`. The emit_py half DID land and is green. **ALSO LEFT: the four tiers not yet lowered.** ts (item 435 finding (d), `xs = xs.push(e)`, the tier that asked for this marker), java and wasm can each read `unique` now and were not audited here; rust deliberately never will. And the summary's other use the item noted — a local born from a CALL result (`var ys = list_map(xs, f)` then `ys = ys.push(z)`) being owned rather than refused — needs a FRESHNESS summary ("is this fn's result newly allocated and unaliased"), which is a different question from retention and was not attempted.

    **ts LOWERED (2026-09-03, PR #262), which closes item 435 (d) — the finding that asked for this marker.** `backends/typescript/emit.py` reads `"unique": true` and renders `push` / `set` / `remove` / a record update destructively; `"unique": "copy"` is declined there because its `unique_birth` shape `"Map"` covers both a revl `Map` (a JS `Map`) and a record update (a plain object), so the birth copy has no single spelling on that tier. Measured: elements copied building an n-element list go 79800 -> 0 at n=400, equal to hand-written, with microtask turns and Promise allocations unchanged. `selfhost/emit_ts.rvl` ported; the native-chain half hits the SAME `lower.rvl` gap as the py tier and was a named strict-xfail there too (`NATIVE_UNIQUE_MARKER_GAP` in `tests/test_selfhost_compile.py`, `ts:transforms.rvl`); that xfail was DELETED with the py half, and `NATIVE_GATE_GAPS` is empty today. Full detail under item 435 (d). **java AND wasm ARE STILL NOT LOWERED, and each was taken to a concrete blocker rather than left unexamined.** **java: the container the marker points at is IMMUTABLE.** `var xs = []` emits `java.util.List xs = java.util.List.of()` and `revlPush` returns `Stream.concat(..).toList()`, so `xs.add(v)` throws; and because the marker is flow-sensitive a name can be unowned at one write and owned at the next, which would land an in-place write on the immutable result of the preceding persistent one. A sound lowering therefore needs a per-name gate (every write to this name marked AND every birth a literal this tier can make mutable) plus mutable births, which changes the declared type of accumulator locals on a tier whose comments record that choice being tuned twice already (`_let_keyword`'s raw `java.util.List` for an untyped `[]`, and item 433 F8 moving `maplit` FROM `new HashMap<>()` TO `Map.of()`). Separately the java Map accumulator DOES NOT COMPILE today: with `JAVA_HOME=/opt/homebrew/opt/openjdk`, `var m = Map.empty()` infers `Map<Object,Object>` and `javac` rejects both `revlMapSet(Map<Object,Object>, String, long)` and `return m` against a declared `Map<String, Long>` — a pre-existing red that any java lowering lands on top of. The instrument exists (`bench/codegen/java`, allocated bytes per op via `getCurrentThreadAllocatedBytes`; `list_push` measures 221224 B/op emitted against 286760 hand), but its `Hand.java` is a PERSISTENT yardstick by design, so the win needs a new mutable-arm case. **wasm: the list header has no capacity field, so there is nowhere to append.** `$list_push` is `[i32 count][8-byte slots]` and `$alloc`s a fresh region plus a `memory.copy` on every push; an in-place append needs either a capacity field (a layout change reaching `$list_concat`, `$list_slice`, indexing, length and the canonical ABI) or a bump-allocator adjacency trick. Neither is a marker-reading change. Both tiers keep the copying form, which is correct.

446. **DEAD TEST SWITCHES: A CORRECT SUITE GATED BEHIND AN ENVIRONMENT VARIABLE NOBODY SETS IS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM A PASSING ONE, AND THIS IS THE SECOND KNOWN INSTANCE (2026-09-02).** A skip and a pass are the same colour on a dashboard, so a suite that is written, correct, and gated behind a switch that is set in no workflow, no Makefile and no `ci/` script reports green forever while measuring nothing. **Two instances are already on this roadmap and neither was found by looking for the class**; both were found by tripping over a defect the dead suite already covered. Item **430**: `pytest tests/` ran in exactly one job, which installed no cordis-py, so 398 tests across 81 modules executed nowhere in CI.
